# SPEC.md — csift

> **Status: AUTHORITATIVE — implemented.** This is the single source of truth for *what csift does and how*. Its design is grounded in real `~/.claude/projects` data (51 sampled sessions across CC 2.1.133–2.1.168, files up to 225 MB / 115 879 records), and the measurements throughout are cited as the evidence for each decision. All seventeen subcommands (§6) are built; §10 folds in the per-feature design rationale + the empirical measurements behind the deepest three (`recover` / `verbatim` / `agents`). [`AGENTS.md`](./AGENTS.md) (Claude Code loads it as the `CLAUDE.md` symlink) remains authoritative for *how to work in the repo* (git discipline, gates, conventions); this file is authoritative for *what to build*. An engineer who has never seen a Claude Code (CC) `.jsonl` should be able to implement csift from this document alone.

---

## 0. Mission & non-negotiables

**csift = "ripgrep for Claude Code session transcripts".** A fast Rust CLI to **list** and **regex-search** CC session `.jsonl` files. Seventeen subcommands: `list`, `search`, `show` (record FETCH by line / turn / uuid, §6.11), `stats` (one-scan aggregates, §6.12), `agents` (subagent lifecycle, §6.5), `whoami` (§6.3, with the lane sections and reach prediction in §6.18), `files` (which files a session changed, §6.6), `recover` (reconstruct a file's content from the transcript, §6.7), `plan` (locate the plan file bound to a session, §6.7.1), `verbatim` (turn-fidelity reconstruction across compaction, §6.8), `image` (list + extract the images a session carries, §6.9), `status` (one-shot LIVE verdict, §6.13), `wait` (block until a session condition fires, §6.14), and the CHANNEL four `send` (§6.15), `msg` + `ack` (§6.16), `deliver` (§6.17). `list`/`search`/`stats`/`files`/`recover`/`plan`/`image`/`status`/`wait` span each session's subagent transcripts by default (`--no-subagents` opts out); `verbatim` is the exception (top-level thread only, `--subagents` opts in, a target is REQUIRED); `agents` lists subagents as its targets (rejects both span flags); the channel four address exactly ONE lane and take no span flag.

- **Primary consumer is an LLM** (a CC agent searching/recovering its own or a peer session). Output must be clean, token-efficient, and regex-driven. Human/LLM-readable text by default; `--format json` for machine use.
- **Explicitly NO BM25 / embeddings / semantic search.** Pure regex/ripgrep only. Lexical tokenisation across scripts (CJK / multi-byte) is intractable for scoring; regex is the strength and the whole point.
- **The writers law.** Exactly three subcommands write - `send`, `deliver`, `ack` - and they write exactly one place: the csift-owned sidecar directory `<session-sidecar-dir>/csift-channel/` (§1.1). Never a transcript, never the team mailbox, never the cross-session messaging socket, never the session registry, never a settings file. csift never installs a hook (`deliver --recipe` prints the block the user pastes) and never performs an official send (it prints the exact call for the model to make). Every other subcommand is read-only. The reason the three are penned into one directory: the harness owns mutable state that is unversioned and written mid-flight, so a tool that reads it for forensics must not become a second writer of it, while a directory csift alone creates and appends to has no such contract with anyone.
- **Quality gates (hard):** `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test` all green. No `unwrap`/`expect` in library/hot paths (only `main` maps error→exit code, tests may `unwrap`). **No silent truncation** anywhere.
- **Output geometry (crate-wide design law).** A truncating consumer (`| head -N` / `| tail -N`) amputates exactly one end of the output. Therefore: (a) anything load-bearing appears at BOTH ends, or inline on the rows themselves; (b) the head must carry enough to interpret what follows (scope, totals, direction); (c) the tail remains the complete ledger (full summary, integrity notes, cautions); (d) the blessed way to limit output is `--max-count`, which keeps every note intact — piping through `head`/`tail` is the discouraged fallback. (`search` is the surface the law is fully applied to; auditing the other subcommands against it is a recorded follow-up.)

---

## 1. Data location & on-disk layout

Each session lives under the CC projects root (`~/.claude/projects`, honoring `$HOME`):

```
~/.claude/projects/<ENCODED>/
  <session-uuid>.jsonl                                  # the main session transcript (one JSON object per line)
  <session-uuid>/                                       # sibling per-session sidecar dir
    tool-results/<id>.txt                               # externalised large tool outputs (see §4.6)
    subagents/agent-<hex>.jsonl                         # (A) built-in Task/Agent-tool subagent transcript — isSidechain:true
    subagents/agent-<hex>.meta.json                     #     companion {agentType, description, name?, toolUseId}
    subagents/workflows/wf_<id>/agent-<hex>.jsonl       # (B) workflow / OMC workflow-subagent transcript (dominant kind)
    subagents/workflows/wf_<id>/agent-<hex>.meta.json   #     companion {agentType}
    subagents/workflows/wf_<id>/journal.jsonl           # (C) workflow EVENT log {agentId,key,type∈{started,result,failed}} — NOT a transcript
    workflows/wf_*.json  workflows/scripts/*.js         # top-level workflow DEFINITIONS (NOT transcripts — ignore)
```

The three subagent transcript/journal shapes (A)/(B)/(C) and the `agents` subcommand are specified in **§6.5**. The bare `<hex>` (== the record/journal `agentId`) is the canonical agent id; the filename stem is `agent-<hex>`. Kind is the on-disk **path location**, not `agentType`.

`<ENCODED>` is the session's **start cwd**, path-encoded (§2).

**Structural facts the implementation must respect (verified):**

1. **A projects dir is keyed by the session's START cwd, not by every cwd the session visits.** Every top-level `*.jsonl` in a given `<ENCODED>` dir carries a `cwd` field that encodes char-for-char to that dir name (0 anomalies across 11 ground-truth dirs). Deep in-session `cd`s (e.g. `…/beacon/src/beacon/turn_engine`) and subagent cwds appear *inside* the data but do **not** spawn their own top-level project dir. So: `<ENCODED> dir ⇒ its files' start cwd` holds 1:1; the inverse (`any cwd seen ⇒ a dir exists`) does **not**. Never assume it.
2. **Some `<ENCODED>` dirs contain NO top-level `*.jsonl`** — only a nested `<uuid>/` sidecar (subagent transcripts) or a `memory/` dir. `list` must tolerate childless/jsonl-less project dirs (skip them) — never assume every projects dir contains a session file, never crash on one that doesn't. Top-level session enumeration is **non-recursive** (it never descends into the `<uuid>/` sidecar), so it cannot mistakenly pick up a subagent file as a session.
3. **Subagent transcripts use the identical record model** as main transcripts, distinguished by `isSidechain:true`, with a companion `agent-<hex>.meta.json`. csift **discovers + spans subagent transcripts by DEFAULT** on `list`/`search` (built-in + workflow / OMC agents under `subagents/**`); `--no-subagents` restricts to main transcripts. The workflow `journal.jsonl` is an event log, never a transcript, and is always excluded. The dedicated `agents` subcommand (§6.5) reports subagent lifecycle.

### 1.1 The csift-channel sidecar — the ONLY thing csift writes

The channel (§6.15-§6.17) keeps its whole state in files csift owns, inside the same per-session sidecar directory the elicitation sidecar uses (§6.10) and never inside a transcript:

```
~/.claude/projects/<ENCODED>/<session-uuid>/csift-channel/
  messages/<id>.json      the message SOURCE, written once at send and never rewritten
  outbox.jsonl            the SENDER's log, one line per send
  inbox/<lane>.jsonl      what was enqueued FOR one receiver lane (append-only)
  ledger/<lane>.jsonl     what csift DID about those messages (the intent record)
  armed/<lane>.json       the runtime marker `deliver` rewrites on every event it handles
```

`<lane>` is the TRANSCRIPT-form id (a top-level session uuid, a bare `a<16 hex>` agent id, or a teammate `a<Name>-<16 hex>`) — validated against the crate's existing shape predicates, so every id `agents` prints is a usable lane id and no separator, `..` or drive letter can enter a path component. A message id is exactly 16 lowercase hex characters.

Record shapes, one JSON object per line, no pretty printing:

- `messages/<id>.json`: `{id, ts_utc, from:{kind:"lane"|"external", session, lane, label, cwd}, to:{session, lane, form:"transcript"|"routing", routing_id}, mode, ttl_secs, relation, cross_project, body}`. `relation` ∈ `parent | child | sibling | cross-session | cross-project | external | unknown`; the four peer-ish values are what makes the envelope print its no-authority caution (§6.15).
- `outbox.jsonl`: `{id, ts_utc, to_lane, to_session, mode, verdict, channel, official:{delegated, tool, to_form}}`. It lives in the SENDER's own session directory, so a sender can audit what it sent without reading any receiver's files; an EXTERNAL caller has no session of its own, so its outbox line falls back to the receiver's root. This is also why the body lives in `messages/<id>.json` and not here: the message file is what a delivery reads, the outbox line is what the sender keeps.
- `inbox/<lane>.jsonl`: `{id, enqueued_utc, mode, expires_utc}`. A message is NEVER removed from an inbox; delivery state lives in the ledger, so the two files answer different questions ("what was addressed here" vs "what happened to it").
- `ledger/<lane>.jsonl`: `{id, kind:"emit", event, slot, part, parts, vehicle, ts_utc, hook_session, hook_agent_id, block_count}` per emitted chunk, plus `{id, kind:"held", reason, ts_utc}`, `{id, kind:"expired", ts_utc}`, `{id, kind:"ack", ts_utc}`, `{id, kind:"redelivered", source:"compact"|"resume", ts_utc}` and `{id, kind:"refused", reason, ts_utc}` (the refusal's `id` is optional, because a refusal can predate knowing which message it would have been about).
- `armed/<lane>.json`: `{slots_seen:[k…], last_event, last_ts_utc, hook_session, claude_code_version}`.

**Two write shapes, chosen for concurrency, not style.** Appends (`outbox`, `inbox`, `ledger`) open with `append(true).create(true)` — `O_APPEND` on unix, `FILE_APPEND_DATA` on Windows — and write the line plus its newline in ONE `write_all`, because several hook processes fire concurrently on one lane and a seeking append would interleave halves of two lines. The armed marker is rewritten WHOLE through a temp file plus a rename, so a reader racing the writer sees the old marker or the new one, never a truncated one. Reads are tolerant and never silent: a missing file is the ordinary case (nothing was ever sent to that lane) and yields an empty result; a line that does not parse is COUNTED and reported like every other malformed line in csift.

**INTENT versus FACT.** The ledger records what csift emitted; the proof a chunk landed is a `hook_additional_context` attachment in the receiver lane's own transcript carrying the envelope id. `msg` (§6.16) joins the two, and the fact half alone is exactly `csift search '<id>' @<lane> -t harness.hook.context`.

**Slot sequencing is NOT in the sidecar.** It lives under the OS temp dir as `csift-deliver-<ppid>-<event>-<lane>/s<k>.done`: Claude Code runs same-event hooks concurrently and concatenates their output in completion order, so slot 1 wipes and recreates the directory, slot k > 1 waits for `s<k-1>.done` (bounded, 50 ms polls), every slot marks itself, and the last removes the directory. A timeout never blocks a delivery: the slot emits anyway and its chunk carries a one-line warning that the order may be disturbed.

### 1.2 The settings cascade as csift reads it

`send` and `whoami --to` must answer "will a delivery hook actually run in that lane?", which means folding the RECEIVER's settings the way Claude Code does. Five named scopes, later winning: `userSettings < projectSettings < localSettings < flagSettings < policySettings`, with a plugin `settings` base below the user scope.

- **Files read.** user `<claude-home>/settings.json`; project `<project-root>/.claude/settings.json`; local `<project-root>/.claude/settings.local.json` AND, when a git root exists strictly above the project root, `<git-root>/.claude/settings.local.json` — Claude Code canonicalises the local scope to the git root only when a uid-ownership probe passes, and that probe leaves no trace, so csift reads both paths, folds the git-root one first (the deeper path wins) and REPORTS both. The policy tier is COMPOSED and first-wins, not merged: `<claude-home>/remote-settings.json` (the server-managed cache) is the whole tier when present, and the managed files that exist but did not compose are reported with that reason rather than looking absent; otherwise `<managed-dir>/managed-settings.json` plus every `<managed-dir>/managed-settings.d/*.json` (dot-prefixed names skipped, sorted, which is the order they win in). `<managed-dir>` is `/Library/Application Support/ClaudeCode` (macOS), `C:\Program Files\ClaudeCode` (Windows), `/etc/claude-code` (elsewhere).
- **Two blocks do not follow "later scope wins".** `env` merges PER KEY (a later scope overrides only the keys it names) and csift keeps the winning scope per key, so a gate verdict can name a file instead of asserting. `hooks` CONCATENATES per event: a project scope ADDS to the user's hooks. Plugin hooks bypass the merge in Claude Code and are unioned at read time (`<claude-home>/plugins/**/hooks/hooks.json` and each plugin's `plugin.json` `hooks` field), folded as one source labelled `plugin:<name>` under a bounded walk whose exhaustion is reported.
- **Three policy switches run after the fold.** Policy `disableAllHooks:true` empties the hook set; policy `allowManagedHooksOnly:true`, or a `disableAllHooks:true` set outside the policy tier, leaves the policy hooks alone. The switch that fired is reported and becomes a `send` risk line, because a configured `csift deliver` hook a switch removed will never run.
- **UNOBSERVABLE, and named as such rather than assumed:** `--settings` (a path or inline JSON, materialised per invocation), `--managed-settings` (a spawning parent's in-memory tier), `--setting-sources` / `--restricted` (can empty the user, project and local scopes), the trust-dialog state (before acceptance only user, flag and policy env applies in full), and MDM policy (a managed plist or a registry tree), which csift never reads.
- **Gate verdicts inherit that honesty.** The teams gate reads the settings `env` key: `enabled via settings env (<scope>)`, or `no settings-level enable; shell env and CLI flags are not observable -> unknown; use evidence: teams directories N, teammate lanes N`. The harbor (cross-session messaging) gate reads the registry row, whose `messagingSocketPath` is the one on-disk consequence of the gate being on AND bound: `registry messagingSocketPath present -> on and bound`, else `... absent -> unknown`. A gate csift cannot see enabled is UNKNOWN with its evidence, never assumed off.
- **The delivery-slot census** (`deliver_slots`) reads the merged hooks for one event and returns the `k` of every `csift deliver --slot k` command entry, sorted and deduplicated (the same k under two matchers is one slot in the chain). It is a whitespace token walk, not a regex: the binary token may be bare or path-prefixed, and a library path never builds a pattern that could fail. Tolerant throughout — an unreadable or malformed settings file becomes a note on its source report, so one broken file never hides the rest of the cascade.

---

## 2. Path encoding (the cwd ↔ dir name rule)

### 2.1 Forward encoding (cwd → projects dir name) — DEFINITIVE

```
encoded = nfc(session_start_cwd).replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9]/g, "-")   // JS semantics: per UTF-16 code unit
```

The cwd is **NFC-normalized**, then every UTF-16 **code unit** not in `[A-Za-z0-9]` becomes a single `-`, **one-for-one, position-preserving, with NO collapsing of consecutive dashes** (extracted verbatim from the 2.1.228 binary: `gT`/`upo` — `e.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9]/g,"-")` — with `.normalize("NFC")` riding every projectRoot path). Unit-wise matters only off ASCII: an astral char is two surrogate units → **two** dashes; an NFC-composed accent is one unit → one dash. That is the entire algorithm.

- `/`, `_`, `.`, space, `-`, and every other non-alphanumeric → a single `-` each. No special-casing of dots, dot-dirs, or extensions.
- **No dash collapsing.** Two adjacent special chars emit two dashes. **Proof:** the real dir `-Users-testuser-Projects-Acme-widget-factory--cache-worktrees-sunny-meadow` decodes from `/Users/testuser/Projects/Acme/widget_factory/.cache-worktrees/sunny-meadow`: the `/.` (slash then the hidden-dir dot) emits a literal `--`. A collapse rule can never emit `--`, so its mere existence refutes collapsing.
- **Leading `/`** (Unix absolute paths) → a leading `-`; every Unix-encoded dir begins with `-`. No stripping. **On WINDOWS the shape differs:** `C:\Users\x` → **`C--Users-x`** (the drive letter passes through; `:` and `\` are one dash each — a drive-encoded dir leads with a LETTER), and a UNC `\\server\share` → `--server-share` (leads with `--`). Both shapes are first-class `@`-targets; the bare-positional form covers Unix + drive shapes (a bare `--…` token is reserved for the mistyped-flag guard — target a UNC-encoded dir via `@--server-…`).
- **Trailing separator:** cwds are stored without a trailing slash, so no trailing dash in practice.
- **Case is preserved** (`Users`, `Projects`, `Acme` keep their capitals — NOT lowercased). Letters/digits pass through unchanged.

Worked examples (all verified):

| cwd | encoded dir |
|---|---|
| `/Users/testuser/Projects/widget_app_prototype` | `-Users-testuser-Projects-widget-app-prototype` |
| `/Users/testuser/Projects/Acme/widget_factory-worktrees/main` | `-Users-testuser-Projects-Acme-widget-factory-worktrees-main` |
| `/a/.claude/b` | `-a--claude-b` |

Reference implementation (matches `src/path.rs::encode_cwd`: NFC first, then per UTF-16 code unit — ASCII-alphanumeric pass-through, every other unit → `-`):

```rust
pub fn encode_cwd(cwd: &Path) -> String {
    use unicode_normalization::UnicodeNormalization;
    let s: String = cwd.to_string_lossy().nfc().collect();
    s.encode_utf16()
        .map(|u| match u8::try_from(u) {
            Ok(b) if b.is_ascii_alphanumeric() => char::from(b),
            _ => '-',
        })
        .collect()
}
```

**Two more rules CC applies (verified against the shipping binaries — `Siq`, re-verified as `gT`/`upo`/`wn` in 2.1.228):**

1. **Canonicalization first.** CC encodes the cwd's **`realpath` + Unicode-NFC** form (`canonicalizePath`), not the raw string — so `/tmp`↔`/private/tmp` symlinks and NFC/NFD variants *converge* to one dir. csift's `absolutize` does the realpath (`canonicalize`) and `encode_cwd` applies NFC + the unit-wise replacement itself, which is what closes the NFD dash-count divergence: an NFD-decomposed accented path encodes identically to its NFC spelling, matching CC. Skip the NFC and a decomposed accent emits one dash per combining unit, so the same directory is addressed under two names.

2. **200-char cap + hash (`MAX_SANITIZED_LENGTH = 200`).** If the encoded string exceeds 200 chars, CC stores the dir as **`<first-200>-<hash>`** — `Bun.hash` on the CLI, a djb2 variant in the SDK (so the SAME path gets DIFFERENT suffixes depending on which wrote it). Because the suffix is not reconstructible, CC's own `findProjectDir` does **not** recompute it — it **prefix-scans** the projects root for a dir starting with `<first-200>-`. `resolve_target` mirrors this exactly (§2.3 step 3). `encode_cwd` itself stays the *raw* full encoding (it also feeds the §2.4 cross-check); the cap lives in the lookup.

**Collision is REAL and CC does NOT disambiguate it (for ≤200-char paths).** The map is many-to-one, so two *different* cwds can encode to the *same* dir — e.g. `/Users/x/Projects/Acme/widget_factory-worktrees/portal` and `/Users/x/Projects-Acme/widget-factory-worktrees-portal` BOTH encode to `-Users-x-Projects-Acme-widget-factory-worktrees-portal` (the first has `/Acme/` + `_` separators, the second `-Acme` + `-` — all collapse to `-`). CC takes no action: both projects' sessions coexist in that one dir, told apart ONLY by session UUID + the in-record `cwd` field (§2.4). csift faithfully mirrors CC (the encoded dir is the lookup key), so a real-path target whose dir is shared by a colliding sibling will surface that sibling's sessions too — `list` shows the true per-session `cwd` (so the collision is visible), but the scoped commands do not filter on it. The `cwd` field is the authoritative origin; never trust the dir name alone to mean one cwd.

### 2.2 Reverse (dir → cwd) is LOSSY — never attempt it

The forward map is many-to-one: `/`, `_`, `.`, space, `-`, … all collapse to `-`. Given a `-` you cannot recover the original byte. Concrete collisions in the real tree: `feature-session-2` (literal `-`) vs `widget_factory` (`_`→`-`) vs `/` separators — all `-`. So `-A-B` could be `/A/B`, `/A-B`, `/A_B`, `/A.B`, … **Do not reverse the encoding.** When the true cwd is needed for an `<ENCODED>` dir, **read the `cwd` field out of the first record of any contained `*.jsonl`** (§2.4) — that is the only reliable recovery.

### 2.3 Target detection — "is this arg a real path, or an already-encoded dir?"

A `list`/`search` target argument is EITHER (a) an actual filesystem cwd or (b) a pre-encoded `<ENCODED>` dir (optionally under `~/.claude/projects/`). Resolve as follows, in order:

1. **Pre-encoded projects-dir token** if: after stripping any leading `~/.claude/projects/` (expand `~`/`$HOME` first), the remainder contains **no `/`**, matches `^-[A-Za-z0-9-]*$` (starts with `-`, only `[A-Za-z0-9-]`), AND `<projects_root>/<arg>` `is_dir()`. → use that dir as-is.
2. **Otherwise treat as a real filesystem path:** canonicalize/absolutize it, apply the §2.1 forward encoding, and look up `<projects_root>/<encoded>`.
3. **Disambiguation belt-and-suspenders:** if an arg could be read both ways, prefer the filesystem-path interpretation **only when** the arg contains a `/` or is absolute. A bare `-…` token with no slashes that exists under the projects root is the encoded form. (This is unambiguous in practice: a real *absolute* cwd always starts with `/` → encodes to a leading `-`, so encoded dirs start with `-` precisely *because* real paths can't be passed as a bare leading-`-` token without a slash.)
4. If neither a real path nor an encoded dir resolves, error with the attempted projects-root path (no silent empty result).

### 2.4 cwd cross-check (recommended integrity step)

Every top-level session record carries `"cwd":"<absolute path>"`. After resolving a target dir, csift MAY re-encode the `cwd` from the first record of a contained file and assert it equals the dir basename — the authoritative confirmation it located the right dir, and the canonical way to recover the human-readable cwd for display.

### 2.5 One session id under TWO project directories (claims PATH-014 / PATH-015)

The cwd-to-directory map is not one-to-one in either direction, and BOTH departures are measured.

**One id, two directories.** A session id can be on disk under two encoded project directories at once. A renamed project whose directory was copied leaves exactly that, and Claude Code reaches the same shape two ways of its own: the `CLAUDE_CODE_PROJECT_DIR_NAME` override keeps the NATURAL key beside the overridden one and adds both to the directory list a session may live in, and `relocateSessionTranscript`'s fallback mover can strand its source (its own warning says so). The harness does not resolve the ambiguity - its resume lookup walks every project directory for `<uuid>.jsonl` and returns null when a second one has messages.

csift MERGES the copies at `path::resolve_session_files`, the one point every session-operating subcommand resolves through, keyed on the BASENAME (§2.4 / PATH-012: the basename IS the session id, which is what the harness's own cross-directory lookup keys on too). The relation is decided by the BYTES - compare sizes, then the first `min(size)` bytes:

| relation | test | what csift does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `identical` | equal sizes, no differing byte | merge onto the longest (either) |
| `prefix` | no differing byte up to the shorter copy's end, sizes differ | merge onto the LONGEST |
| `divergent` | a differing byte before the shorter copy ends | merge NOTHING; every copy stays in scope |
| `unknown` | a copy csift could not READ, so there are no bytes to compare | merge NOTHING; every copy stays in scope |

A prefix copy is what copying a file that is still being appended to produces, and choosing it would silently drop the records only the longer one carries. An UNREADABLE copy answers `unknown` before any comparison, because reading its failure as an empty file would make it a prefix of every other copy and - being larger by metadata - the winner, so the merge would announce the readable copy as dropped and the command would be left with the one it cannot read; a read failure is a gap in the evidence rather than evidence of sameness, and the disclosure names that copy and the error the filesystem returned. A divergent pair is not one session seen twice: a turn number minted from one copy does not address the other, so csift keeps both and says they differ. An MTIME never enters the rule - the instants on such copies can be re-applied wholesale by a preserving copy, so they are evidence about the copy operation rather than about the harness. An equal-size tie goes to the copy whose directory basename equals the encoding of the FIRST record that carries a `cwd`, then to the lexicographically smallest path, so the answer is the same on every run and every machine.

Nothing is dropped in silence: the resolver names every group on stderr (the `@main` lane-note posture - csift states an inference the caller cannot see), the JSON envelope header of `list`/`search`/`stats`/`files`/`recover`/`verbatim`/`image`/`agents` carries `duplicate_transcripts` (transcripts the merge removed), `duplicate_records_dropped` (their physical lines), `divergent_transcripts` (copies kept in scope BEYOND one per id because the bytes disagree - a merge that dropped nothing is not the same fact as no duplicate on disk) and `unreadable_transcripts` (copies csift could not read at all, which is why their group merged nothing), those commands print a `copies` line beside the scope banner, and a `list` row carries `also_at` plus `duplicate_relation` with `sessions_in_scope` counting DISTINCT sessions. `show` is the one refusal that stays: it counts the UNMERGED set, because an address that names two files on disk must not pick one - it bails naming every copy's path, and a `*.jsonl` target reads the copy it names.

The merge then runs ONCE MORE over the whole resolved UNION, and that second pass is what keeps ONE rule over the set: the gathered sidecars of a DIVERGENT session pair can hold byte-identical copies of one agent transcript, and an invocation naming a session AND one of its agents (`list @<uuid> @<agent-hex>`) reaches the same `agent-<hex>.jsonl` down two paths - merging each half separately let the two halves pick different winners and both copies survived. The union pass reports only what it newly decided: a group that merged NOTHING is still there for it to find, and one group is one note. The BASENAME is the id for both transcript kinds, so an `@<agent-id>` target takes the same merge: a copied project directory carries the whole sidecar with it, one `agent-<hex>.jsonl` is then on disk under two directories, and the agent path finds EVERY copy rather than the first one `read_dir` yields: it stops at the owning session and then probes each project directory for a copy of that ONE basename, which an agent hex being globally unique is what makes sound - and an `is_file` call rather than a second walk of every sidecar in the corpus. Measured over the same root, every duplicated agent basename belongs to one of the duplicated sessions' two sidecars and no agent basename is shared between two different sessions (PATH-014), which is what makes the basename key sound on that side too. The disclosure is the same note, the same header keys and the same `copies` line, and `show @<agent-id>` refuses a duplicated agent exactly as it refuses a duplicated session.

**One session, several directories.** The mirror case, and it costs csift nothing. A session's WORKFLOW SCRIPT path is built from the project directory of the SHELL cwd in force at the time (`Bu(ne())` joined with the session id and `workflows/scripts/`), while the transcript path comes from the session's originalCwd slot (§2.1 / PATH-009), so a deep in-session `cd` mints `<other-encoded-dir>/<uuid>/workflows/scripts/<name>.js` with no transcript beside it. Measured over two projects roots: 55 `<uuid>/` directories sit beside no transcript, their direct children are only `workflows` (18) and `tool-results` (10), and NONE of the 55 carries a `subagents/` subtree or a single `agent-*.jsonl` - the 18 hold 81 files between them, every one a `.js`. Subagent discovery therefore stays anchored at the transcript's own directory: there is nothing of its kind elsewhere, and a persisted-output pointer is resolved by the absolute path the record carries rather than by a walk.

---

## 3. The jsonl record model (one JSON object per line)

> **Parsing philosophy (load-bearing):** real records carry **far** more fields and `type`s than any fixed list. The model must **tolerate the unknown**: deserialize only what csift uses, ignore everything else, and never crash on a new field, block type, record type, or a missing `timestamp`. Field presence is **ragged** across record types, so nearly every field is `Option<T>`.

### 3.1 Complete enumeration of top-level `type` values (CC 2.1.x, verified)

```
user  assistant  system  attachment  file-history-snapshot  queue-operation
last-prompt  ai-title  agent-name  mode  permission-mode
```
plus version-specific extras observed: `last-prompt`, and the catch-all must absorb any future addition. **There is NO `type:"summary"` record in CC 2.1.x** — zero `summary` records exist across all 51 sampled sessions. Compaction is a `type:"user"` record + a `type:"system"` `compact_boundary` record (§4.7), so detection keys on `isCompactSummary` and never depends on a `type:"summary"`, while *defensively* still tolerating a legacy `type:"summary"` (older CC) without relying on it.

### 3.2 Common envelope

Present on message-bearing + most event records; **ragged** — model every field `Option`:

| field | rename | type | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `type` | — | `Option<String>` | discriminator; keep as String (open set), never enum-panic |
| `uuid` | — | `Option<String>` | record identity; used for round-trip stitching |
| `parentUuid` | `parent_uuid` | `Option<String>` | parent link; `null`/absent at thread roots & metadata records |
| `timestamp` | — | `Option<String>` | ISO8601 UTC `2026-06-07T05:43:00.000Z`; **absent on metadata-only records** |
| `sessionId` | `session_id` | `Option<String>` | the owning session uuid |
| `cwd` | — | `Option<String>` | absolute start cwd (re-encodes to the dir name) |
| `version` | — | `Option<String>` | CC version string (e.g. `2.1.159`) |
| `gitBranch` | `git_branch` | `Option<String>` | |
| `isSidechain` | `is_sidechain` | `Option<bool>` | `true` ⇒ subagent transcript record |
| `userType` | — | `Option<String>` | typically `"external"` |
| `isMeta` | `is_meta` | `Option<bool>` | **TRAP — see §4.2:** system-injected pseudo-turn marker |
| `isCompactSummary` | `is_compact_summary` | `Option<bool>` | compaction-summary marker (§4.7) |
| `isVisibleInTranscriptOnly` | `is_visible_in_transcript_only` | `Option<bool>` | co-set on compaction summaries |
| `subtype` | — | `Option<String>` | on `system` records (§4.7) |
| `content` | — | `Option<serde_json::Value>` | on `system` records (free-form) — keep raw |
| `message` | — | `Option<Message>` | role-bearing payload on user/assistant records |
| `toolUseResult` | `tool_use_result` | `Option<serde_json::Value>` | structured echo on tool-result carriers (§4.6) — keep raw |
| `requestId` | `request_id` | `Option<String>` | on assistant records |
| `promptId` | `prompt_id` | `Option<String>` | on user/assistant records |
| `logicalParentUuid` | `logical_parent_uuid` | `Option<String>` | on `compact_boundary` |

Ignored-but-tolerated extras (do NOT model; serde drops them): `entrypoint`, `permissionMode`, `sourceToolAssistantUUID`, `attachment`, `snapshot`, `messageId`, `leafUuid`, `aiTitle`, `lastPrompt`, `hookInfos`, `durationMs`, `level`, … Unknown top-level keys must never cause a parse error (serde ignores unknown fields by default — do **not** add `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]`).

### 3.3 `Message`

```rust
pub struct Message {
    pub role: Option<String>,        // "user" | "assistant"
    pub content: Option<Content>,    // string OR block array (§3.4)
}
```
On `assistant` records the raw `message` is the full Anthropic API message object (`model`, `id`, `stop_reason`, `usage`, `diagnostics`, …); csift models only `role` + `content` and ignores the rest.

### 3.4 `Content` — polymorphic string-or-array

```rust
#[serde(untagged)]
pub enum Content {
    Text(String),          // bare string: older format / genuine-user text / compaction summary
    Blocks(Vec<Block>),    // array of typed blocks
}
```

### 3.5 `Block` — typed content blocks (internally tagged on `type`, with Unknown catch-all)

```rust
#[serde(tag = "type", rename_all = "snake_case")]
pub enum Block {
    Text     { text: String },
    Thinking { thinking: String, signature: Option<String> },   // signature = opaque base64, may be absent
    ToolUse  { id: Option<String>, name: Option<String>,
               input: Option<serde_json::Value>,
               caller: Option<serde_json::Value> },              // caller:{type:"direct"} observed on real records
    ToolResult { tool_use_id: Option<String>,
                 content: Option<serde_json::Value>,             // string OR array — keep raw, see §4.5
                 is_error: Option<bool> },
    Image    { source: Option<serde_json::Value> },
    #[serde(other)]
    Unknown,                                                     // any future block type — never a parse failure
}
```

Block → label mapping is in §5. Note the `ToolResult` `content` can itself be an array containing `{type:"text",text}`, `{type:"image",…}`, and **`{type:"tool_reference", tool_name}`** (emitted by ToolSearch results); model it as raw `Value` and inspect as needed.

### 3.6 serde shape requirements (summary for the implementer)

- Top-level `Record`: a struct with `#[serde(default)]` on every field (ragged presence) and `rename` for camelCase→snake_case. **Do not** model it as an externally-tagged enum on `type` — too many open-set variants; keep `type` as `Option<String>` and branch in logic. (The existing `src/model.rs` does exactly this; extend it, don't restructure.)
- `Content`: `#[serde(untagged)]` string|array.
- `Block`: internally `#[serde(tag="type", rename_all="snake_case")]` + `#[serde(other)] Unknown`.
- `tool_result.content` and `system.content`: raw `serde_json::Value` (string|array|object).
- Metadata records (`last-prompt`/`ai-title`/`agent-name`/`mode`/`permission-mode`/`file-history-snapshot`) parse into `Record` with `timestamp == None` — **never `unwrap()` a timestamp**; skip such records in all time logic.
- **Encoding is not malformity.** The ONE parse entry (`parse::parse_line`) retries a failed parse ONCE against the `String::from_utf8_lossy` form, and only when `std::str::from_utf8` on the payload fails. So a line whose bytes are not valid UTF-8 but whose JSON is sound becomes a RECORD carrying U+FFFD rather than a malformed count, while a merely-bad-JSON line takes exactly one parse and one error. `--raw` still returns the original bytes. Rationale: csift renders every such line lossily on every other path, so dropping the record would lose the conversation on that line to one byte in a field nothing reads. The retry has to be EXPLICIT because serde offers no tolerance to lean on: the `#[serde(flatten)]` exposed-key map buffers every unknown key, so a bad byte anywhere on the line fails the whole record rather than being skipped along with the field it sits in.
- **Zero-copy where it pays:** for the full-scan path, prefer `serde_json::from_slice` deserializing borrowed `&str`/`Cow<str>` out of the mmap slice (`#[serde(borrow)]`) to avoid per-field allocation. (See §7.)

---

## 4. Record semantics (the rules that hinge everything)

### 4.1 Genuine-user vs tool_result-carrier (THE load-bearing distinction)

A `type:"user"` record is **NOT** always a human turn. `tool_result` blocks ride on `role:"user"` records — such a record is a tool-result *carrier*, not human input. Magnitudes are extreme: in one real session **177 genuine** users (167 string + 10 text-block) vs **8 675 tool_result-carriers** (98% of `user` records); another had 332+61 genuine vs 1 619 carriers. Getting this wrong corrupts both the `user` label and turn-delimiting. Two further `role:"user"` shapes that *look* human but are NOT — and sit outside the `user` role under the §5 taxonomy — are an inbound peer message in any of its THREE framings, `<teammate-message>`/`<agent-message>`/`<cross-session-message>` (→ `agent.communication.*`) and a `<task-notification>` automation pulse (→ `harness.notification.*`).

**`is_genuine_user(record)` is true iff ALL of:**
1. `type == "user"` AND `message.role == "user"`; AND
2. `isCompactSummary` is falsey (excludes compaction summaries, §4.7); AND
3. **`isMeta` is falsey** (excludes system-injected pseudo-turns, §4.2); AND
4. **`turnCompanion` is not `true`** — the SECOND authorship flag, which the harness stamps on a record it wrote to ACCOMPANY a turn and which Claude Code's own "is this the person's message" predicate excludes for the same reason. It is not a subset of (3): two of the shipped build's stamping paths touch no meta flag at all (a slash command's expansion messages, and the extra `type:"user"` messages a tool returns beside its result), so reading `isMeta` alone counts such a record as the human's own turn and shifts every later turn number; AND
5. content is a **string**, OR content is a block array containing a `text` block and **no** `tool_result` block.

(`text` and `tool_result` never co-occur in one user record in real data, so "has a `text` block" is a clean genuine signal once carriers and meta are excluded.) `src/model.rs::is_genuine_user` implements (1)–(5); the two authorship flags are read by `classify` too, so the LABEL and the turn BOUNDARY answer alike and a record csift refuses to call the human never reads as `user.message` under a selector either; it **additionally** excludes the machine-synthesized markers of §4.2.1–.3 (interrupt strings, `<local-command-stdout>…`, `<command-name>…`) AND an inbound `<teammate-message>`/`<agent-message>`/`<cross-session-message>` (`is_peer_message` — a peer-agent message is never the operator, and all three framings count, the `<agent-message>` peer form included; detected ONLY at a SECTION BOUNDARY, so a tag merely QUOTED mid-prose stays the human) so none of them start a turn AS the human (codepoint-safe via `trim_end`/`ends_with`/exact `==`/`starts_with`, never a byte-offset slice).

**Boundary predicate `opens_turn(record)` — the single turn delimiter (§6.4).** Turn-delimiting keys on `opens_turn`, NOT on `is_genuine_user` alone. A turn opens on ANY of:
1. `is_genuine_user` (above); OR
2. an **answered AskUserQuestion** carrier (§4.4) — the answer IS the user's message; OR
3. a **tool-use rejection carrying a typed user message** (§4.2.4) — the typed instruction IS the user's message; OR
4. an **inbound peer message** (`is_peer_message_record` — a `<teammate-message>`, `<agent-message>` OR `<cross-session-message>` from another session) — a real delivered message, so it stays a turn-opener even though `is_genuine_user` excludes it (segmentation/turn-COUNT stays byte-stable where peers already opened turns; only the §5 label changes, to `agent.communication.*`). The `<cross-session-message>` record additionally carries `isMeta:true`, `promptSource:"system"`, `userType:"external"` and a top-level `origin` object of `kind:"peer"`; a `queue-operation` enqueue line with the identical content precedes it and is a harness RIDER, never `user.queued`.

Cases 2–3 are genuine user messages, so each opens a turn (§6.4). **Turn-opening is a SEPARATE axis from the §5 label:** `opens_turn` segments the transcript, `Record::classify` assigns the searchable `role.class.sub` label(s). The reconstruction (`reconstructed_user_text`) renders the opener body for each case: the plain genuine text (`user.message`), the full AUQ Q+options+answer unit (`user.answer`), the rejection instruction + a `[plan: <path>]` pointer (`user.rejection`), or the teammate-message body (`agent.communication.inbox`).

### 4.2 `isMeta:true` pseudo-turns + synthesized markers — TRAP, must be excluded

`isMeta:true` `user` records have string/text content that *looks* human but is **system-injected**, e.g. `"Continue from where you left off."`, `"# Autonomous loop tick"`, `"Stop hook feedback:\n…"`, `"<local-command-caveat>…"`, `"[Image: source: …]"`. These are **not** genuine human input and must be excluded from `user.message` **and** from turn-delimiting. The `isMeta` exclusion is load-bearing. Under the §5 taxonomy these are not dropped from search — `classify` reparents the recognized ones through `model/classify/injections.rs` - `harness.schedule.wakeup` (a fired `ScheduleWakeup` / autonomous-loop timer tick), `harness.schedule.tick` (the autonomous-loop DRIVER tick), `harness.resume.prompt` (the resume loader's `Continue from where you left off.` repair prompt), `harness.hook.feedback` (a blocking hook's feedback, over the four names `Stop`, `TeammateIdle`, `TaskCreated` and `TaskCompleted` that one formatter writes), `harness.command.caveat` (the `<local-command-caveat>` the command executor writes) and `harness.enforce.retry` (the harness CONTINUING a turn the model did not finish, on its own instruction: the nine whole texts its own is-this-mine predicate tests by membership - four redo nudges, two classifier-stop forms, the output-token-limit resume and two mid-stream cut-off forms - plus the edit-failed-retry nudge, matched as a prefix). An `isMeta` record that matches NO harness marker (a novel hook wrapper, the `[Image: source:…]` reference) classifies **empty** (excluded), never `user.message`, because a genuine `user.message` is never `isMeta`.

**Beyond `isMeta`, four NON-`isMeta` user-record shapes are also machine-synthesized and must NOT open a turn** (verified on real `~/.claude/projects` data; corpus counts in parentheses):

#### 4.2.1 Interrupt markers (116 + 21)
A `type:"user"` `text`-block (or bare-string) record whose content is **EXACTLY** `"[Request interrupted by user]"` or `"[Request interrupted by user for tool use]"` — a CC-synthesized interrupt marker, not a human turn. None carry extra prose (dropping them as boundaries loses zero user content). Match is **exact `==`** (a real message that merely *quotes* the phrase stays genuine).

#### 4.2.2 `<local-command-stdout>…` (52)
String content (NOT `isMeta`) starting with `<local-command-stdout>` — local-command OUTPUT (machine), not the user's prose. (Its sibling `<local-command-caveat>` carries `isMeta` and classifies `harness.command.caveat` - its producer is the slash-command EXECUTOR, never a hook.) Does NOT open a turn (excluded via `starts_with`); classifies **`harness.command.stdout`** (§5).

#### 4.2.3 slash-command wrapper — BOTH tag orders (`<command-name>…` / `<command-message>…`)
The machine-templated EXPANSION of a slash command. **Current CC emits `<command-message>` FIRST, then `<command-name>`** (verified in real corpora: 14 new-order sessions vs 35 older `<command-name>`-first ones), so detection is `is_slash_command_wrapper` — string content (NOT `isMeta`) whose LEADING tag is EITHER `<command-name>` OR `<command-message>` (the new `COMMAND_MESSAGE_PREFIX`). **`is_genuine_user` excludes BOTH orders, and the templated wrapper does NOT open a turn**: admit either order as human prose and the expansion becomes a turn of its own, shifting every later turn number on a transcript that carries one. Classification is multi-label: any genuine prose the user typed after the command lives in `<command-args>…</command-args>` and is recovered via `slash_command_name` as `/name args`, so a WITH-ARGS wrapper classifies **`[user.message, harness.command.invocation]`** with `user.message` FIRST (the richest-view law, §5.2) — the UNFILTERED render is the extracted `/name args`, never the wrapper XML; a NO-ARGS wrapper classifies **`harness.command.invocation`** ONLY. An explicit `-t harness.command.invocation` still renders the raw wrapper. (No new Matcher synth needle is needed — the name + args are verbatim raw substrings and the rendered seam is a space, so a whitespace-bearing pattern is never prefilter-eligible.)

#### 4.2.4 Tool-use rejection — `is_error:true` tool_result (31 with-message / 36 without)
When the user REJECTS a tool use (ExitPlanMode plan kick-back, or a rejected AskUserQuestion / Edit / …), the result carrier is `is_error:true` with content beginning `"The user doesn't want to proceed with this tool use…"`. TWO sub-shapes:
- **With a typed message** — ends `"To tell you how to proceed, the user said:\n<TEXT>"`. `<TEXT>` IS a genuine user message → **opens a turn** (`plan_rejection_message` extracts the tail codepoint-safely via `split_once`) and classifies **`user.rejection`** (dual-labeled with `agent.tool.result` — it rides on the rejecting tool_result carrier; deduped to the richer `user.rejection`, §5). Both rejected tools share the marker (ExitPlanMode AND an AskUserQuestion-clarify reject). For an ExitPlanMode rejection, the rejected `tool_use_id` resolves through the transcript's `CallIndex` (its plan half maps `tool_use_id → planFilePath`) to append a `[plan: <path>]` pointer so a consuming LLM can Read the plan (ExitPlanMode-only; an AUQ-clarify reject has no plan path).
- **Without a typed message** — ends `"STOP what you are doing and wait for the user to tell you how to proceed."`. The user clicked reject but typed nothing → **NOT a boundary** (→ only `agent.tool.result`, no `user.rejection`).

The plan **APPROVAL** carrier (`"User has approved your plan…"`, no `is_error`, no typed message) is the harness greenlight, NOT a user message → never a boundary.

### 4.3 `tool_use` (assistant calling a tool)

Assistant `tool_use` blocks → `agent.tool.use` (§5). `name` identifies the tool; `input` is an arbitrary per-tool JSON object. `AskUserQuestion` is a `tool_use` (§4.4). A `caller` field (`{type:"direct"}`) may be present. A `SendMessage` tool_use ALSO carries `agent.communication.{sent,signal}` and a `Task`/`Agent`/`Workflow` spawn ALSO carries `agent.communication.sent` (the dual is deduped to the comm view, §5).

### 4.4 AskUserQuestion

A `tool_use` block with `name == "AskUserQuestion"`. Input shape:
```
input.questions: [ { question, header, multiSelect: bool,
                     options: [ { label, description } ] } ]
```
**HARD-WON (verified across all 51 sessions with 0 counterexamples; RE-MEASURED at CC 2.1.258):** a **pending/unanswered single-question** AUQ is **never** flushed to jsonl. Only answered AUQs appear. **The multi-question shape (`input.questions` length >= 2) was on disk while its dialog was open** (measured live on macOS and Windows at 2.1.258; three single-question trials in the same sessions stayed unwritten), so the `status`/`wait` tail reads it as `waiting-hitl` without the sidecar, while the single-question shape still needs the sidecar (section 6.13). The split is a TIMING outcome of the persistence frontier, not a rule keyed on the question count: the binary has no question-count branch on its write path and the frontier is unchanged across builds (ledger ELI-009), so the two observations are stated as measurements and the gate holding the single-question record is an open question. The answer arrives as a `tool_result` whose `content` is a synthesized string:
```
User has answered your questions: "<question>"="<chosen label>". You can now continue…
```
and the carrier's top-level `toolUseResult` echoes the full `questions[]` structure + a **structured `answers{question → answer}` map** (the CLEAN source — the inline synthesized string is noisy/truncated). **Implication:** from the NATIVE transcript alone csift cannot render a "pending question" state — an unanswered AUQ is off-disk, and if an AUQ `tool_use` is on disk its answer is too. The unanswered window is recovered ONLY from the hook-written elicitation sidecar, which csift merges transparently (§6.10).

**The COMPLETE user message = QUESTION + OPTIONS + ANSWER as one unit.** The user does not click an option — she answers in prose (often a counter-question or a scope-expanding decision), so the answer is her selection *plus* her reasoning. `auq_exchange()` reconstructs the whole exchange as one genuine-user unit: `[AskUserQuestion · N questions]` then, per question, `Qn (header): question  options: a | b | …` and `An: <answer prose>`. It is built from the structured `toolUseResult.questions[]` zipped with `toolUseResult.answers{}` (clean), falling back to parsing the synthesized `"<q>"="<a>"` string only when `toolUseResult` is absent. Codepoint-safe (no byte-offset slice into a CJK question/answer).

**The answer is a genuine-user TURN BOUNDARY** (`is_auq_answer_boundary` — true iff a non-errored `tool_result` carrier with a non-empty `toolUseResult.answers` (or a non-blank freeform `response`) OR the synthesized marker on a `tool_result` whose `tool_use_id` names an AskUserQuestion `tool_use` of the same transcript — the marker alone decides nothing, because a tool result opens with it whenever the tool printed it (a shell run over the harness binary, a grep over these phrasings); a CANCELLED/rejected/validation-errored AUQ has `is_error:true` and no `answers` → NOT a boundary). An AUQ answer (e.g. one that expanded the session scope) is a genuine-user message, so it opens its own turn (§6.4) and is surfaced on every surface (verbatim/list/search/recover/budget/topology). The answer classifies **`user.answer`** (dual-labeled with `agent.tool.result`; deduped to the richer `user.answer`, §5).

### 4.5 `tool_result` (tool's response) → `agent.tool.result`

Lives **inside a `type:"user"` carrier record's** `message.content` array, never on assistant. Fields: `tool_use_id?`, `content` (string OR array of `{type:text,text}`/`{type:image}`/`{type:tool_reference,tool_name}`), `is_error?`. Linkage to the originating `tool_use` is `tool_result.tool_use_id == tool_use.id`; **some legacy carriers omit the block-level `tool_use_id`** — fall back to the carrier's top-level `toolUseResult`/`sourceToolAssistantUUID` to resolve linkage.

### 4.6 Externalised (persisted) large outputs

When a tool output is too large, the inline `tool_result.content` string is a pointer:
```
<persisted-output>
Output too large (NNN KB). Full output saved to: <ABSOLUTE_PATH>

Preview (first 2KB):
…
</persisted-output>
```
`<ABSOLUTE_PATH>` = `<ENCODED>/<session-uuid>/tool-results/<id>.txt` (`<id>` is a tool short-id like `b070yh2rb` or a hook form `hook-<uuid>-<n>-additionalContext`). **In addition**, the carrier's top-level `toolUseResult` object carries **structured** `persistedOutputPath` + `persistedOutputSize` (bytes) fields. **Resolution rule:** when `search --resolve-persisted` is set, read the file at `toolUseResult.persistedOutputPath` (exact — no regex on the inline marker needed); only fall back to regex-scraping the inline `Full output saved to:` path if the structured field is absent. Default (flag off): leave the inline pointer as-is (token economy). Resolution failures (missing file) are reported, never fatal.

### 4.7 `type:"system"` records & compaction

`system` records carry the common envelope + `subtype` (+ `level?` ∈ {`info`,`error`}). Subtypes (complete, verified):

| subtype | key fields |
|---|---|
| `stop_hook_summary` | `hookCount`, `hookInfos:[{command,durationMs}]`, `hookErrors`, `preventedContinuation`, `stopReason`, `hasOutput`, `toolUseID` |
| `turn_duration` | `durationMs`, `messageCount` |
| `away_summary` | `content` (short auto-summary of what the session was doing when it went idle; free text, often non-English) |
| **`compact_boundary`** (= compaction metrics) | `content:"Conversation compacted"`, `logicalParentUuid`, `compactMetadata:{ trigger:"auto"\|"manual", preTokens, postTokens, durationMs, cumulativeDroppedTokens, preCompactDiscoveredTools[], preservedSegment:{headUuid,anchorUuid,tailUuid}, preservedMessages:{anchorUuid,uuids[],allUuids[]}, messagesSummarized }` - nine keys, the last only on a `/rewind` summarize (claim CMP-002) |
| `scheduled_task_fire` | `content` (e.g. `"Claude resuming /loop wakeup …"`), `slug` |
| `local_command` | `content` (`<command-name>…</command-name>` wrapper), `level` |
| `api_error` | `level:"error"`, `error:{status, headers{…}, …}` |

**Compaction = TWO records, NOT a `type:"summary"`:**
1. The **summary text** is a `type:"user"` record with `isCompactSummary:true` + `isVisibleInTranscriptOnly:true`, **string** content (commonly starting `"This session is being continued from a previous conversation…"`). → **excluded from genuine-user** (§4.1 rule 2).
2. The **metrics** are the `type:"system"` `subtype:"compact_boundary"` record above.

Every observed `system` subtype carries a §5 leaf (`model/classify/system.rs`, the eleven-row table): the boundary is **`harness.compaction.boundary`**, the three end-of-turn telemetry records are `harness.turn.*`, the scheduler's fire record is `harness.schedule.event`, and the five event kinds are `harness.system.*`. Only the boundary is reached by a FLAGLESS scan; every other subtype needs a selector naming its leaf, and a subtype the table does not name carries no leaf at all and renders as an unmodeled unit under an address (§5.4). **`search`-surfaced:** `search`'s §7 stage-1 transcript-candidate prefilter — which keeps `"role":"user"`/`"role":"assistant"` lines for the 200 MB-file performance contract — ALSO keeps the rare `compact_boundary` record — but that keep is **`-t`-GATED** (a `needs_compact_boundary` bool, `label_selected(&args.categories, "harness.compaction.boundary")`, derived once and `&&`-gating the extra `memmem`): no-`-t` or a selector reaching `harness.compaction.boundary` keeps it; a `-t user`/`-t agent.*` query pays ZERO (the `memmem` is never reached). Behind that gate the keep is a **CONJUNCTION**: the rare literal `compact_boundary` AND the key-only bytes `"subtype"`, tested in that order, because the literal alone is not the discriminator — a payload can carry the word in prose. The census behind that - the file set, the counting rule, both populations and the caveat that the payload population is a MOVING count taken at an instant, because a session writing ABOUT the literal accumulates it in prose - sits at the keep itself in `search::scan::gates`, beside the code it justifies. A refused line is still lifted to a structural spine row by the non-candidate arm, so the survival walk reads through it, and an explicit address still fetches it whole. The role keeps ALWAYS run (conservative superset); when the boundary `memmem` runs it only touches lines that already failed both role checks, and boundaries are rare — so the perf contract holds. So `search -t harness.compaction.boundary` surfaces it: for a message-less system record `record_raw_text` renders the boundary's top-level `content` plus a readable `compactMetadata` excerpt (`[compaction boundary: trigger=… preTokens=… postTokens=… durationMs=…]`) as the match + excerpt, so compaction points can be enumerated and inspected; `verbatim` still also renders the boundary as its own banner. The compaction SUMMARY (item 1 above) is a `type:"user"` record, so it classifies **`harness.compaction.summary`** AND is surfaced by `search`. All other `system` subtypes still parse cleanly, carry no label, and must never crash time logic.

### 4.8 `attachment` & other event records — skippable noise

`attachment` is the **dominant** record type (54% of all records in the largest file — mostly `{attachment:{type:"hook_success",…}}` SessionStart spam). ~21 attachment subtypes exist (`hook_success`, `hook_additional_context`, `task_reminder`, `date_change`, `plan_mode*`, `deferred_tools_delta`, `ultrathink_effort`, `compact_file_reference`, `edited_text_file`, `queued_command`, `skill_listing`, `nested_memory`, `selected_lines_in_ide`, `hook_cancelled`/`hook_blocking_error`, `file`, …). **None carry a §5 label** — they are dropped pre-JSON by the prefilter (§7) and absorbed by the tolerant model (`classify` returns empty for them). `file-history-snapshot` nests its timestamp inside `snapshot` (not top-level). `queue-operation` has a top-level `timestamp` + `operation ∈ {enqueue,dequeue,remove,popAll}`. Metadata records (`last-prompt`/`ai-title`/`agent-name`/`mode`/`permission-mode`) have **no `timestamp`/`uuid`** — skip in time/turn logic.

### 4.9 The Bash shell cwd + file freshness (verified against CC 2.1.237; full-corpus validated)

The mechanisms `files`/`recover` build on. Extracted from the Claude Code binary and
validated against real transcripts (18,185 commands for the cwd tracker; 577 hint
attributions); the crate's implementation notes live at `bash::mutations::cwd` and
`recover::carriers`.

**The shell cwd.** Claude Code keeps NO long-lived shell: every Bash call spawns a
fresh shell at a TRACKED cwd, and that tracked value is stamped on every jsonl record
as the top-level `cwd` field (subagent transcripts included; stable across
compaction). The tracker updates via a `&& pwd -P >| <tmp>/claude-<id>-cwd` read-back
appended to each command — so a command that exits non-zero, and any backgrounded
command, never advances it. A `cd` into a project SUBDIRECTORY persists silently into
later calls (the record `cwd` follows); a reset to the original directory fires only
when the shell ends OUTSIDE originalCwd + the `/add-dir` set, printing "Shell cwd was
reset to <path>" into the tool result. `CLAUDE_BASH_MAINTAIN_PROJECT_WORKING_DIR`
truthy = reset every call, silently. **ANCHOR LAW: read `cwd` off the TOOL_USE
record, never the result record** (the post-command update is asynchronous; ~0.24% of
result records lag). Consequence: no cross-command state machine is needed — each
record carries its own spawn cwd, and only INTRA-command `cd`s need lexical tracking
(`bash::mutations::cwd`; measured 99.967% ending-cwd accuracy, 99.65% agreement with
CC's own modified-file hints). Corpus shape of mutation targets: 19.75% typed
absolute, 62.79% relative (84.21% of those resolve at the zero-inference `cwd-joined`
class), 17.47% dynamic (`$VAR`/glob — never resolved).

**File freshness (readFileState).** Write/Edit gate on an mtime check against the
last Read, with a CONTENT-HASH absolution available only to FULL reads (a windowed
read has no absolution channel: mtime moved ⇒ rejected). Byte-identical rewrites (an
idempotent formatter) never trip the gate. Bash NEVER invalidates readFileState —
that is exactly how staleness arises — but a bash `cat <file>` SEEDS read-state (CC
lexically recognizes read-shaped commands), so a Write can legally follow one.
Rejections always land as `is_error:true` tool_results (the `behavior:'ask'` field is
inert in the runner; corpus 25/25). Three signals ride the stream and are consumed by
`recover`:
- `toolUseResult.staleReadFileStateHint` (Bash results): CC stats its whole read set
  after a WRITE_COMMAND_MARKERS-shaped command and NAMES the modified files —
  `[This command modified N file(s) you've previously read: p1, p2 and M more. Call
  Read before editing.]`, paths relative to the shell cwd, list capped at five.
- `toolUseResult.staleRecovered:true` (successful Edit results): disk had drifted
  since the last read, but `old_string` stayed unique and the edit applied; corpus
  carries roughly twice as many of these as hard rejections.
- the `edited_text_file` attachment: idiom-independent external-change capture,
  gated on read-set membership + full-view read + one shot per read-epoch, with a
  16KB budget that degrades to an EMPTY snippet when exceeded.
A change with none of these signals (read-first then changed with no later
Write/Edit attempt) leaves NO transcript trace — absence of a signal is never
evidence of absence, which is why recover's window accounting (§6.7) exists.

### 4.10 `/clear`: a new session in the same process, joined by the cost ledger

`/clear` (aliases `reset`, `new`; optional `[name]`) ends a session and starts another inside the SAME process, and it writes no lineage anywhere. One generator does it, in a fixed order: the SessionEnd hook with reason `clear` on the OLD id; the conversation state emptied; the cost-ledger SAVER; the session id regenerated with `crypto.randomUUID()`; the SessionStart hook with source `clear` on the NEW id. The transcript file handle is dropped, so the next append opens `<new>.jsonl` in the same project directory; the registry row keeps its pid and swaps `sessionId` in place; `$CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` reports the new id. An SDK consumer's `conversation_reset` frame carries a separately minted uuid that is NOT the session id.

**No field names the predecessor.** The old id lives only as an in-memory `parentId` for the rewind UI. Censused over one projects root, `"parentId"`, `"clearedFrom"` and `"previousSessionId"` occur on zero lines of 9,521 transcripts; a FORK, by contrast, writes `fork-context-ref.parentSessionId` (33 lines there). So any join is an INFERENCE and must say so.

**The instrument is the cost ledger's checkpoint.** The saver runs BEFORE the id swap, so the `cost-state` line it writes carries the OLD session id and lands in the OLD file, and `startTime + totalDuration` on that line is the instant the old session closed. The NEW file opens with the re-stamped session-state lines, then an isMeta `<local-command-caveat>` user record, then the `/clear` slash wrapper itself, then a `system`/`local_command` record with empty stdout. Measured on a live clear the wrapper sits 164 ms before the close instant (the caveat +3 ms, the `local_command` +2 ms) and the nearest competing checkpoint in the same directory was 43,968 ms away. `startTime` is per SESSION and is reset at the clear, so equality of `startTime` across files is not a join; the SUM is. `list` reads exactly that: `minted_by: "clear"` when the first non-isMeta user record is the wrapper, `cleared_from` when a sibling's checkpoint closes within 2000 ms of the wrapper instant, the nearest LINE winning and a tie between two FILES reported and joined to neither (§6.1).

**A checkpoint is repeatable, so it is never a close marker.** The `cost-state` literal has 7 occurrences in the shipped binary (an interned name, the schema, one builder, two policy tables, two readers) and the one writer has five entry points: the registered saver (the clear, a background handover, an in-app resume), a background spawn, a resume or in-app fork, the shutdown-gated teardown, and the exit re-stamp provider - an exit writes TWO lines with the session-metadata re-append between them. Corpus: 14 of 9,519 transcripts carry one, 44 lines, 35 of them not last. The schema is twelve keys with no `uuid`, no `timestamp` and no `message`, so the line is not a chain node and its only address is its physical line; the loader admits only `user | assistant | attachment | system` into the message array, so its values never reach the model except through the restored ledger under `--max-budget-usd`. `status`/`wait` report one at the TAIL as evidence, never as a verdict (§6.12).

**What the clear resets.** In memory: the message array, readFileState, nested memory paths, session env vars, the memory selector, EVERY plan slug, file history, the cost counters, the prompt-assembly sections (system prompt rebuilt, CLAUDE.md and memory files re-read), skills-sent state, slash-command caches, invoked skills, the WebFetch / ToolSearch / agent-definition caches, the metadata cache, and the shell cwd. NOT reset: MCP connections (only the ide server's cache) and the permission cache. Background tasks survive unless a foreground task is still in its turn. No PreCompact or PostCompact hook fires and no summary or boundary is written - a clear is not a compaction. The plan corollary: `forgetPlanSlug` runs on the outgoing id, so a clear-minted transcript has NO plan binding until plan mode is entered again, and `csift plan` reports none rather than inheriting one.

**The task store keeps the startup id.** The list id is `CLAUDE_CODE_TASK_LIST_ID` else the team context's `teamName` else an inherited name else the leader team name else the session id, under `<claude-home>/tasks/<that id>`. With the agent-teams path active the implicit team `session-<first 8 of the startup id>` is minted once at startup, its `config.json` stamps `createdAt` at that instant and a `leadSessionId` written once, and the initializer renames `tasks/<startup uuid>` onto that name. A clear, a resume and a fork never touch it, so the store outlives the session id that named it (§6.12 for the candidates `status` tries).

### 4.11 The SURVIVAL AXIS: which records the conversation still reaches

**A transcript is not the conversation.** Claude Code reconstructs a session by streaming every `user`/`assistant`/`attachment`/`system` line with a string `uuid` into a map where the LAST occurrence of a uuid wins, choosing ONE leaf, and walking `parentUuid` from that leaf to the head. It reads `parentUuid` and nothing else, so a record with none ends the walk, and a `compact_boundary` (its own `parentUuid` null) is exactly such a record; `logicalParentUuid` is never read on that path. Everything the walk reaches is the conversation, and everything else is on disk without being in it. `model::Chain` (src/model/chain.rs) mirrors that rule hop for hop and is the single production answer to "is this record still in the conversation?", and it replaced three earlier heuristics that approximated it (the same-parent draft rule, the replay-copy collapse and the boundary's implicit cut), none of which could see a turn the operator rewound past. Claims MISC-034 (the map and its admission gate), MISC-035 (the four leaf gates), MISC-036 (the walk), MISC-037 (the preserved-messages relink), MISC-038 (the two membership passes), MISC-006 and REC-033 (what the DAG shape can and cannot say), MISC-043 (the dangling boundary), MISC-046 (how many boundaries a transcript carries, and how many of them can be crossed).

**Three values, `Survival::{Live, PreCut, Abandoned{root}}`.**
- **Live**: on the chain, or widened onto it by the two membership passes that run after the walk: the same-`message.id` assistant siblings of a chain assistant (with the `tool_result` carriers parented to them), and every non-conversation descendant of the leaf.
- **PreCut**: physically above the chain FLOOR - the record csift's walk ended on - or removed from the map by the preserved-messages relink. Claude Code drops this history on a resume; csift keeps reading it and EVERY selector still reaches it, flagged and never hidden, because what the model saw AT THE TIME is the question an archive answers. Where that floor SITS is the bounded crossing below.
- **Abandoned{root}**: reached by neither, with `root` naming the line where the branch left the conversation.

**The leaf choice, and why it is disclosed.** The leaf is the `leafUuid` of the newest `last-prompt` line the file carries, subject to four gates: a later `compact_boundary` WIPES the accumulated set; an `explicit:true` line with a null leaf clears it; a recorded leaf that is a proper ANCESTOR of the file-order-last record yields to that record; and a leaf naming no record in this file falls back to the tail, never to the next-newest `leafUuid`. A `last-prompt` line carries no `uuid` of its own (claim REC-103), so it is a session-metadata snapshot and not a conversation record. Which gate fired rides every surface as `leaf_source`, because the leaf decides the chain and the chain decides the answer.

**Drafts and rewinds are the SAME shape with one discriminator, and it is a DESCENDANT.** An abandoned turn OPENER is a `user.unsent` draft when no assistant record hangs below it (a prompt recalled with esc and re-typed) and a `user.rewound` turn when one does (it was sent, it drew a reply, and the conversation later threaded around it). Measured over the corpus's 910 shared-parent opener pairs: 858 earlier members with no assistant descendant, 52 with one, and 0 of the 910 on the final chain (claim TURN-032). Both labels are RETROACTIVE and neither can be assigned at write time, because the record carries no field that marks it (claims TURN-026, MISC-041) and the deciding sibling or descendant does not exist yet.

**A replay copy is the EARLIER line.** A compaction re-anchor re-appends a contiguous block of already-written records with their uuids preserved. The loader's map keeps the LAST line carrying a uuid, so that line is the survivor for opener treatment and for addressing by uuid, and the earlier line renders `[replay copy of L<n>]`. Both stay searchable and counted, the same double-count law §4.7's clone states, one file down instead of across files.

**The BOUNDED CROSSING, and the FLOOR it leaves (claims MISC-036, MISC-043, MISC-046).** A `compact_boundary` carries a null `parentUuid` (278 of 278 boundary records over the top-level transcripts of one live projects root at the time of its census; the root grows, the ratio holds), so the loader's walk ends there and the whole history above the cut leaves the conversation. But the boundary also NAMES the record the compaction re-anchored onto, in `logicalParentUuid` (278 of 278 name one), and the conversation genuinely continues across a compaction: the records above a cut are the same conversation, and until the walk reaches them csift cannot tell a turn the operator REWOUND PAST from history it merely failed to resolve - which is the question the differential measures, 490 emitted hits moving `pre-cut` to `live` on the landmark and 1,150 and 952 on two band transcripts. (One shape that LOOKS like an instance is not: a subagent dispatched before a compaction and returning after it. The returning `tool_result` carrier is parented to the assistant record that issued the call, so the walk climbs over the cut by `parentUuid` alone and never arrives at the boundary. A boundary is reached only where the chain's own path runs through the summary.) csift therefore follows that link, under a BUDGET: the global `--cross-compactions <N|all>` flag says how many boundaries the walk may step over, and the DEFAULT is a measured value rather than a taste (section 7j). Above a boundary it CROSSED the ordinary rules apply - Live where the chain reaches a record, Abandoned where it does not - so a turn the operator rewound past above a cut is finally named for what it is. `--cross-compactions 0` is Claude Code's own reading: csift crossed nothing on purpose, and it then says nothing about crossing at all, on any surface. It ends the walk AT every boundary with no exception, and that is the one place a BUDGET and a GUARD on the `logicalParentUuid` arm decide differently: a boundary whose own `parentUuid` is non-null and names no record is climbable by that arm as a guard, while a budget of 0 buys no hop at all, so its records read `pre-cut` at 0 and resolve at any non-zero budget. No transcript of the measured corpus - 32 streams over the differential's eight transcripts - carries that shape, so compare the budgets rather than assume on one that does.

**A shut door is THREE facts, and the floor's cause says which.** A boundary csift cannot climb through gets one of three causes, because a reader chasing the record needs a different thing in each case. `unresolvable-boundary`: the `logicalParentUuid` is on NO line of the file - the pre-compaction records were deleted from disk (the 52 of 278 this section's census counts), and there is nothing to go and read. `relink-removed`: the file DOES carry that record, on a line a reader can open, and the pre-boundary cut took it out of the uuid map before any leaf was picked. `boundary-loop`: the file carries it, the map holds it, and the WALK has already reached it - the door leads back into the conversation rather than above the cut, which is what a boundary carrying a preserved set does by construction (the relink re-parents that set into the chain, the walk climbs it, and the boundary's own logical parent is that segment's tail). Measured over the 82 top-level transcripts of one projects root at `--cross-compactions all`, 18 end on a boundary and the three split 9 / 0 / 9; the other 64 are 63 `head` and one `no-leaf`. None of the three moves with the flag. The three are kept APART because no single cause can describe all of them: one reason reading `names no record this file carries` is FALSE on the two doors whose record the file does carry, which is half the boundary floors measured here.

The walk still ends ON a boundary, and every such ending leaves a FLOOR - an off-chain record physically above it reads `PreCut`, is NEVER called abandoned, and inside that region the measured same-parent opener rule still marks a draft, so draft detection never depends on the chain reaching that far. It ends there for FOUR causes, not two: `budget-exhausted` when the budget ran out standing on it, and one of the three shut doors above (`unresolvable-boundary`, whose named record is on no line of the file - claim MISC-043's 52 of 278 boundaries over 14 files, the pre-compaction records deleted from disk by the pre-boundary deletion path; `relink-removed`; `boundary-loop`). Nor does `budget-exhausted` by itself mean the flag would move that floor: the budget can run out standing on a boundary whose door is shut, which is why the floor carries the door beside the cause and the footer probes it before naming a value. A walk that reaches a record with no parent that is not a boundary reads `head`, and a parent naming nothing at all reads `dangling-parent`. The blind region is not a partial record set either: no admitted record's `parentUuid` names a progress or unparsed line (12 transcripts probed, empty census).

**The disclosure, on every surface that prints survival.** It is never silent when csift stepped over a cut, when its budget ran out, when a floor is a boundary it could not climb through, or when ANY boundary was left above a floor uncrossed - and silent by construction when it was told not to. That last fact is the one a FOLD reduces a scope to, since the fold drops the cause: a scope of transcripts that each stop at a shut door has crossed nothing and has no cause left, and its only fact is the count above its floors. The text and the six keys answer one question and must agree on it. Text carries up to two lines - `(compaction crossing: N boundary(s) stepped over under --cross-compactions B[, exhausted]; M above the chain floor were not)` and, when M is non-zero, `(the chain floor is L<line> = <local instant> (<cause>); records above it read pre-cut - <remedy>)`. The REMEDY names a flag value only where raising the flag would MOVE the floor, and that takes TWO facts rather than one. The cause has to be `budget-exhausted` - a `head`, `dangling-parent` or `cycle` floor never reached the boundaries above it at all, and a shut BOUNDARY door is a floor at every budget - AND the boundary the budget stopped at has to have a DOOR: a `logicalParentUuid` naming a record still on a line of this file. The door is probed at the walk's own stop, one map lookup, because the walk is standing on that boundary anyway. With both facts the line prints `to try them: --cross-compactions K (or all)` with K = `boundaries_crossed + boundaries_above_floor`, the budget at which every boundary here gets its door opened; what it promises is TRIES and not arrivals, since a boundary further up may still be shut and that is a property of what is on disk rather than of the flag. A `budget-exhausted` floor whose door IS shut prints `raising the budget lands on this same record: it is <the door's own reason>`, because every larger budget walks to that same record and only calls the stop by its other name. The reason is the DOOR's, read off the same `FloorCause::reason` the `(<cause>)` clause uses when the walk reaches that boundary with budget left, so one record gets one description: written from the cause alone the line hard-coded one door's words and described a `boundary-loop` or `relink-removed` floor as `names no record this file carries` at the budget that stopped on it, then correctly at the next budget up. Every other cause prints `raising the budget reaches none of them`, the reason already standing in the `(<cause>)` clause immediately before it, so the remedy says only what a reader can DO. A scope of several transcripts has several floors and the door probe is exactly the fact a fold cannot carry - but MOVABILITY is the scope's own question and survives the fold, ORed across its members, so the scope offers the budget with `where a budget can` when at least one of its floors is one the flag would move and prints `raising the budget reaches none of them` when none is. Without that OR a scope whose floors were all shut doors named a value that moves none of them: the landmark folded with a one-boundary transcript whose door is dangling printed `--cross-compactions 80`, and 80 moves neither floor. `search`, `show --branch-points`, `show --line`/`--uuid`/`--turn`, `verbatim`, `files`, `recover` and `image` print exactly those lines on stdout; `stats`, whose block is one dense line per fact, appends the same counts to its `chain` row as `compaction boundaries crossed N/K (budget B[, exhausted])`. THREE modes print them on STDERR instead, because their stdout is a datum a consumer parses rather than a report: `search --count-by <axis>`, whose stdout is the census table and whose own accounting note already rides stderr, `show --raw --turn`, whose stdout is the file's own bytes, and `recover`'s DEFAULT restore mode, whose stdout is the restored file and whose whole status report already rides stderr. Recover discloses in all FIVE of its modes - restore, `--at`, `--salvage`, `--patches`, `--coverage` - because they are five readings of one scan and a reader who only ever runs the default must not be the one who is never told. A RAW ADDRESS - `show --line N --raw`, `show --uuid U --raw` - prints nothing about crossing on either stream, and that is the one fetch outside the rule by construction rather than by omission: it names its lines outright, so the bytes it prints are the same under every budget and nothing it says moved with the walk, and it builds no chain to find them (a line walk and a uuid prefilter).
