Building one only to disclose it would cost the escape hatch the rendered fetch's price: the chain-building `show --line 1 --format json` took 3.15, 2.55 and 1.62 times as long as `show --line 1 --raw` on live transcripts of 124, 416 and 721 MB (hyperfine medians, 9 runs after 2 warmups, at a load average near 9). The ratio is an instant's reading on one host - a busier host or a file of another shape reads another - so what the rule rests on is its direction, not a figure. The rendered `--line`/`--uuid` fetch does build the chain, because its stamps (`survival`, `turn_index`) move with the budget, so it discloses on stdout and in its six JSON keys; `show --help` states the split. Both of those MOVE with the budget and so must not be silent - an address renders a `survival` that changes under the flag, a census counts records the crossing resolved, and a `--raw --turn` window is a set of lines the chain chose - while a `--line`/`--uuid` address under `--raw` names its lines outright, crosses nothing to find them, and prints nothing. JSON carries SIX keys on the trailing summary of each of those commands, on the `search --count-by` census summary and on each `stats` session row - `crossing_budget` (the number, or the string `all`), `boundaries_crossed`, `budget_exhausted`, `boundaries_above_floor`, `floor_line`, `floor_cause` - present whenever the budget is non-zero, so a reader can tell "asked to cross, nothing to cross" from "asked not to cross", and absent entirely at budget 0. The floor's LINE and its CAUSE are single-transcript facts and are dropped the moment a second transcript folds into a scope total, exactly as `boundary_cut_line` already is; the counts add.

**Two fail-open guards on csift's own side.** The §7d prefilter keeps only role-bearing lines, and a chain built from those alone resolves nothing (1 of 30,613 conversation records reachable on one real session), so every surface splices back the STRUCTURAL row of each line its prefilter drops through `parse::spine_record`: `type`, `uuid`, `parentUuid`, `timestamp`, `isSidechain` and the boundary fields, lifted by a depth-1 key walk into a narrow `parse::SpineRow` on its own output stream - never the payload, and never an otherwise empty `Record` carried in the surface's own record vector, whose width is paid on every line of every scan (§7g). A line the prefilter ADMITTED but the label gates leave unsearchable takes the same road from the other side: it is MOVED onto that spine stream as the identical row (`parse::spine_from_record`) instead of being removed, because deleting it would punch a hole in a chain that threads through attachment records.

Where the axis lands per command is §6.4.1 (live turn numbering and the disk / conversation split); what it does to a bare role selector is §5.6.

### 4.12 PROVENANCE: which message a unit corresponds to

**The question, and why it needs a primitive.** A reader holding one hit asks what it is about, and a key-only answer reaches almost none of them: there is something to read only where a record happens to carry a key naming a target - a refusal's `refusedUserMessageUuid`, a file-history line's `messageId`, an attachment's `parentUuid`. Every other unit, which is most of a transcript, states nothing: an assistant reply, a thinking block, a tool call and its result carry no target key at all. The primitive answers the question for EVERY emitted unit, in two parts that are deliberately different shapes.

**`for_root` is what the unit BELONGS to**, and it is on every hit because every unit has one: the record that OPENED its turn, plus the parent-hop distance up to it. The opener is the turn's own opener and NOT its first record - the shared grouper folds a synthetic pre-user LEAD into turn 0 (a SessionStart hook attachment, a `<local-command-caveat>`, a compaction boundary), and reading position 0 would name that lead as the message every unit of turn 0 corresponds to. FOUR answers, all facts, none an absent field. A unit that opens its own turn corresponds to nothing above it (`note: "turn opener"`). A unit the parent chain walks to the opener from carries the address AND the hop count, because the distance separates the reply to a question from a tool result forty calls into the same turn. A unit the turn GROUPING places but the parent links do not carry there keeps the opener's address with a null hop count and the reason (`note: "not reached by the parent chain"`) - csift knows which turn it is in without being able to say how it got there, and a fabricated count would hide the difference. A unit with NO opener above it - one of that folded lead, or of an abandoned branch whose head opens no turn - names none, because the opener BELOW it would point a reader the wrong way down the file (`note: "no opener above this unit"`). Text renders `[root L<n> +<hops>]` (the hops omitted when the chain did not reach it) and NOTHING on the opener itself, whose tag would restate the line the reader is on; JSON is always the object `{line, uuid, hops, note}`.

**`for[]` is what the record STATES**, one entry per relation, each `{kind, basis, target, reason}`. The relation is an [`EdgeKind`] from a CLOSED set of twenty-seven (`model::provenance`), which is what makes the vocabulary learnable and what lets `--count-by for-kind` declare a key space: a bare string at each family's call site made the set whatever six files happened to contain, unenumerable and impossible to census. `basis` is the honesty column and is never defaulted - `key` when the record names the target, `position` when csift read it off where the record sits (the parent link is stamped from the running tail of the transcript, so what it names is the record above rather than a target its producer chose), `inferred` for a join csift computed from other facts.

**EVERY EDGE IS ONE HOP**, and that is why an edge carries no `hops` member. It names the neighbour this record STATES a relation to - the line above, the call it answers, the id in its payload - and nothing follows the relation further: a run of chained attachments renders each link's own parent rather than the conversation record the whole run hangs off, and a `for[]` entry never reports a distance. The distance question is `for_root`'s and it is answered there once per unit, where it means something a reader can use (how far this unit sits from the message it belongs to). So a DESCENT - one record to many, like a fallback turn's banners - is not this shape twice over: the direction is the one no uuid map answers, and the answer would be a SET where the edge carries one target. Such a relation is rendered as the honest empty with that reason, never as a silent absence.

**SIX of the twenty-seven are the ENGINE's own** and are the only ones not read off a family's key. Four of them are the SURVIVAL axis's answers, which the chain computes for every record it walks: `supersedes` names the message that replaced a recalled draft, `continues` the one the conversation continued from after a rewind, `branches` the head of the branch a record sits on, and `replays` the surviving line a replay copy's uuid also names - `replays` on basis KEY (the shared uuid is what the loader joins on), the other three on position. All four ride JSON alone, because each restates a marker its own unit already prints, and they are stamped beside the survival FIELDS so a unit can never render `[abandoned]` while its `for[]` says nothing. The other two are one hop. `parent` resolves `parentUuid` to a line, on every unit whose record names one, and it STANDS DOWN when ANY OTHER edge on the unit already names that same target - a family's word (`blocked`, `redoes`, `fires`, `attached_to`, `wraps`) or one of the engine's own (`pairs` on a compaction summary, `repairs` on a paired resume placeholder), and equally the LINE `result_of` just named: one relation, one edge, because two words for one relation is a vocabulary a reader has to learn twice. The test is on the TARGET, not on who named it, so the stand-down is not a family privilege - measured on the landmark transcript it moves 83 records, `parent` 169,180 to 169,097 against 80 compaction summaries and 3 paired placeholders. No FIELD is removed by it: the record still has the parent it had, and `for_root` still names the turn it belongs to. `result_of` resolves a `tool_use_id` to the line carrying the call - the one relation in the set that is not a uuid relation, which is why `model::provenance::ProvIndex` sits beside the chain's uuid map and is filled by the SAME walk the tool-pairing sets already run. It is on every unit that ANSWERS a call, which is four leaves and not one: the plain `agent.tool.result`, the `agent.communication.inbox` that supersedes it on a subagent return, and the two USER-FACING duals - an answered AskUserQuestion and a refused tool call are the person's own message and a tool result at once, so `user.answer` points at the ask and `user.rejection` at the call it refused. The duals ride the same carrier and the same id; `--count-by pairing` is unmoved, because the pairing verdict keys on the leaf and those two have always been outside its domain. Both ride JSON alone rather than the label zone (`EdgeKind::rendered_in_label`): the label zone carries what a record SAYS about itself, and these two are on nearly every unit and say what a rendered transcript already shows - the record above, and what the pairing glyph states in one character.

**The four target shapes are the builder's own four answers** (`record_text::for_edge`, one place, so every family resolves and renders alike): a LINE when the uuid names a record of this transcript, the ID with `not in this file` when it does not (a wire id, a record another session holds, a line a compaction deleted), a SESSION alone when the target is a whole transcript and no record in it, and `HitFact::ForUnknown` with a reason when the record names nothing at all. The honest empty is RENDERED, never omitted: a missing edge reads as "there is no such relation", which is a different claim from "this record does not say".

**WHICH SURFACES CARRY IT, and which do not.** Two: `search` and `show`. They share one stamping site and one hit shape, so an ADDRESSED unit carries exactly the provenance a SCANNED one does, which is the refetch law applied to this primitive. The other six spanning surfaces - `verbatim`, `files`, `recover`, `image`, `agents`, `list` - carry their own fields UNCHANGED and no `for` at all. That is a stated absence rather than an oversight: each of them indexes what it emits in its own coordinate space (a per-turn unit, a file event replayed in file order, an image reference, a subagent node, a session row), so projecting `for_root` and `for[]` there means answering the same questions in five more index spaces, not re-rendering a hit. The projection is a declared FOLLOW-ON; until it lands, a reader holding one of those rows addresses its record with `show --line N` and gets the full answer.

**THE RELATION INVENTORY.** The table below is the whole of it: every relation csift can answer about a record, the basis it answers on, and where the answer rides. The first block is the closed `for[]` vocabulary (`EdgeKind`, twenty-seven words), pinned against this table by a drift test in both directions - a variant the table does not name fails, and a `for[]` row naming no variant fails with it. The rest are relations that are real and deliberately not edges. TWO rules put them there. A CROSS-FILE relation is a field on the surface that owns the join, because `ForTarget` carries a session id and not the id trio: extending the edge to carry the trio would fork the one target shape every family builds through, and the surfaces that already answer these (`list`'s lineage row, an `agents` node, `msg`'s verdict) make the join with the files in hand. A relation whose target is not a RECORD - a command, an instant, a lane, an external file, an event - is a per-family FACT with its own key, because an edge resolves a record address and those have none. The `unknown` rows are stated absences, each naming what the record does say instead.

| relation | what it joins | basis | where it rides |
|---|---|---|---|
| `armed_by` | a schedule EVENT -> the call that armed it | — | `for[]` system - always the honest empty: the join is an id matched inside a tool result, which a uuid map cannot make |
| `attached_to` | a record -> the record it was written UNDER | key · position | `for[]` attachments (the payload's own parent key) · `for[]` injections (the parent link) |
| `banner` | a fallback-served turn -> its `model_refusal_fallback` record | — | `for[]` system - always the honest empty: a DESCENT, one record to many |
| `blocked` | a blocking hook's feedback -> the assistant turn it stopped | position | `for[]` injections |
| `branches` | a record -> the HEAD of its abandoned branch | position | `for[]` engine, JSON alone - the unit already renders `[abandoned]` / `[rewound]` |
| `continued_in` | a `continued-in` line -> the child TRANSCRIPT a handover moved to | key | `for[]` bookkeeping - the one target that is a session and no record in it |
| `continues` | a REWOUND opener -> the resend the conversation continued from | position | `for[]` engine, JSON alone - the unit already renders the distance line |
| `fires` | a fired scheduler prompt -> its `scheduled_task_fire` record | position | `for[]` injections |
| `for_tool_use` | an attachment -> the call its payload names | key | `for[]` attachments |
| `forked_from` | a `fork-context-ref` line -> the record the fork cut at | key | `for[]` bookkeeping |
| `hook_run` | a `stop_hook_summary` -> the hook RUN it belongs to | key | `for[]` keys - a hook-run id, not the API tool_use id its field name suggests |
| `interrupted` | an interrupt -> the assistant message it cut short | key | `for[]` keys |
| `leaf` | a `last-prompt` line -> the chain leaf at that write | key | `for[]` bookkeeping |
| `pairs` | a compaction BOUNDARY <-> its SUMMARY, both ways | key · position | `for[]` engine - key off `preservedSegment.anchorUuid`, position where the boundary names none or names itself |
| `parent` | every record -> its `parentUuid` | position | `for[]` engine, JSON alone - the record above, which a rendered transcript shows. STANDS DOWN where ANY other edge on the unit names the same target, a family's word or the engine's own `pairs` / `repairs` |
| `precedes` | a compaction boundary -> its `logicalParentUuid` | key | `for[]` engine - `not in this file` where the predecessor was deleted with the region |
| `preserves` | a compaction boundary -> the HEAD and the TAIL of the segment it kept | key | `for[]` engine, two edges. The TAIL resolves to no line on 51 of 275 boundaries (19%) and renders `not in this file`, the record having gone with the region the compaction cut. The HEAD resolves on all 275 |
| `redoes` | a harness redo nudge -> the assistant turn it sent back | position | `for[]` injections |
| `refused` | a model refusal -> the user message it was for | key | `for[]` system |
| `repairs` | a resume PLACEHOLDER -> the dangling prompt it answers | position | `for[]` engine - only on a paired placeholder |
| `replays` | a replay COPY -> the surviving line carrying its uuid | key | `for[]` engine, JSON alone - the unit already renders `[replay copy of L<n>]` |
| `replies_to` | an inbound relay -> the message it answers | — | `for[]` injections - always the honest empty: no relay carries such a key |
| `reports` | a `<task-notification>` pulse -> the call it reports on | key | `for[]` engine, BOTH carriers - the honest empty on a pulse naming no `<tool-use-id>` |
| `result_of` | a unit that ANSWERS a call -> the call | key | `for[]` engine, JSON alone - the pairing glyph says it in one character |
| `supersedes` | a recalled DRAFT -> the message that replaced it | position | `for[]` engine, JSON alone - the unit already renders the distance line |
| `versions` | a file-history line -> the prompt it versions | key | `for[]` bookkeeping |
| `wraps` | a delivered local command -> the slash wrapper above it | position | `for[]` system |
| `folds` | a unit csift COLLAPSED -> the placeholder that stands for it | position | `verbatim`'s fold marker (`[N agent message(s), M tool call(s)]`, and the same-prefix re-send fold) - a csift RENDER decision, so the target is a line of csift's own output rather than a record on disk. The harness's own mid-turn fold, a `queued_command` absorbed into a running turn, rides `user.queued`'s `queue_operation` / `queue_reason` |
| `opens` / the turn root | any unit -> the record that OPENED its turn, with the hop distance | position | `for_root` on EVERY hit - the one answer that carries a distance, which no edge does |
| `answers` / `rejects` | an answered AskUserQuestion, a refused tool call -> the call | key | `result_of` above - the two user-facing duals ride the answering carrier and its id |
| `returns_to` | a subagent -> the call that spawned it | key | `agents` node `spawn_tool_use_id` + `parent_agent_id` - a CROSS-FILE join |
| `parent_lane` | a subagent transcript -> its owner | position | the id trio (`parent_session_id`) on every spanning row - a CROSS-FILE join |
| `clone_of` | a clone-head transcript -> the session it was copied from | inferred | `list` row `clone_of` - a CROSS-FILE join, inferred from a boundary shape |
| `cleared_from` | a `/clear`-minted transcript -> its predecessor | inferred | `list` row `cleared_from` - a CROSS-FILE join, inferred from a cost checkpoint |
| `session_id_names` | a record -> the session that MINTED it | key | `list --lineage` `minted_elsewhere` - a set, and a different question from the three above |
| `delivers` | a csift-channel envelope -> the record it landed in | key | `csift msg` - it joins a sender's LEDGER to a receiver's TRANSCRIPT, across sessions |
| `sent_by` | a peer relay -> the LANE that sent it | key | the injections fact `peer_sender_task_id` - a lane is not a record |
| `produced_by` | a hook attachment -> the COMMAND that produced it | key | the attachments fact `produced_by` - a command is not a record |
| `checkpoints` | a `cost-state` line -> the INSTANT it closes | key | the bookkeeping fact `checkpoint_closes_at` - an instant is not a record |
| `summarizes` | a compaction summary -> the region it replaced | — | unknown: no key names that region. The boundary names what it KEPT (`preserves`), and `csift verbatim` reconstructs the rest |
| `closes` | a turn's closers -> the assistant turn that ended | — | unknown past the first hop: `hook_run` joins a stop-hook summary to its run, and the rest of the chain is inferred from position |
| `steers` | a mid-turn steer -> what it redirected | — | unknown: no key on the record names one |
| the image handle | an image block -> the record and position that carry it | position | `image` rows and the hit's `image_ids` (`#N`, `L<line>i<n>`) |
| the persisted pointer | a `<persisted-output>` -> the external file holding the output | key | `search --resolve-persisted`, which reads the file - the target is not a record |
| the freshness signals | an external write -> the Edit or Bash call around it | inferred | `recover` boundaries and annotations - the target is an EVENT, not a record |
| the elicitation key | a sidecar marker -> the native record that closed it | key | nothing: `csiftKey` PAIRS the two and the sidecar record is then dropped, so no edge survives to render |

**THE ONE-INDEX LAW.** No provenance relation is computed twice. The uuid-to-line answer is the chain's OWN map, handed back by the build rather than rebuilt; the `tool_use_id`-to-line answer is one map filled by the walk that already existed; the turn root is read off the turn grouping the scan already has. That last one is a re-use of the GROUPING, not a free answer, and the difference is worth stating: `TurnRoots::build` allocates a uuid-to-position map and a hops vector over the turn's own rows, which is one pass and two allocations per turn - paid on a turn's FIRST hit, never for a turn that matched nothing, and once per turn rather than once per pass over it (`TurnRootsCell`, shared by the matched hits and the siblings). What the grouping saves is the segmentation itself: which records are in this turn, which no surface re-derives. A surface that wants an edge PROJECTS one of those - it never re-derives it - and a new relation is a variant on the closed enum plus one lookup, never a second index.

**COST, and where it is paid.** The forward answers are per HIT, not per record: the root walk's ladder (the turn's uuid-to-position map) is built on a turn's FIRST hit and not before, so a turn that matches nothing pays nothing, and the engine's two edges are one map lookup each on a unit that already exists. The per-FILE side is strictly cheaper than before: the three questions a hit asks of a file's tool blocks - the tool's name, whether a result came back, and which line carries the call - are ONE walk over those blocks where they were two. The REVERSE closure (§6.2's `--for`) runs only under its flag, and it is computed PER TRANSCRIPT: one pass per file over that file's own rows, with the uuid and call frontiers file-local, so a closure never continues into another transcript. Joining one ACROSS files - a subagent transcript answering the call its parent made, a `continued_in` handover - is a declared FOLLOW-ON: nothing in the walk reaches past the file it is computed over, and the cross-file join is not built here.

---

## 5. Label taxonomy - `role.category.leaf` (`search -t/--label`, repeatable)

The `-t` axis is a **3-role, 17-category, 73-leaf** dotted taxonomy (`model::Class`; `Class::ALL` is the single source of truth, pinned by a hand-kept oracle table whose length must equal it, and `Class::CATEGORIES` the middle tier). `Record::classify(ctx) -> Vec<Class>` is the engine: **multi-label** (one physical record can carry >1 leaf), pure, tolerant (a record whose shape csift models no leaf for yields an empty `Vec`, never a crash). A selector matches a record iff some selector path is a **dot-SEGMENT prefix** of some label path, and the rendered/JSON label is always the full dotted leaf path.

The space is split by PRODUCER: what wrote the record decides which category it lands in. The alternative is one `harness.meta` bag, and it pools hook-injected text, a hook run's own record, a slash command's caveat, the loop tick, the end-of-turn telemetry, the file-history instrument and every unmodeled `type:"system"` subtype behind one name plus two catch-alls - a shape a selector cannot name and a census cannot describe.

### 5.1 The engine: one dispatch over five mechanical family tables

`Record::classify` is a DISPATCH; the leaf families live in `model/classify/`, one module per axis a transcript actually splits on, each a table from an on-disk VALUE to a leaf with no look at the payload:

| module | axis | table |
|---|---|---|
| `bookkeeping` | the line's own top-level `type` | 18 non-transcript types -> 13 `harness.bookkeeping` leaves |
| `system` | a `type:"system"` line's `subtype` | 11 subtypes -> the compaction boundary, the three `harness.turn` leaves, `harness.schedule.event` and the five `harness.system` event kinds |
| `attachments` | an attachment payload's `type` | the payload types -> `harness.hook.{context,outcome}`, `harness.context.*`, `agent.message.structured` |
| `thinking` | a content block's own shape | `thinking` (text present, absent, or narration-tagged) and `redacted_thinking` -> the four `agent.thinking` leaves |
| `injections` | an isMeta user record's TEMPLATE | the templates csift recognises -> the caveat, the blocking-hook feedback over its four names, the two schedule ticks, the resume prompt, the retry nudges |

Dispatch order is fixed: the elicitation sidecar, then the csift CHANNEL view and the rest of the attachment payload types, then the non-transcript line types (system subtypes included), then the three transcript record types. Two payload types need one more fact than their own value and the caller supplies it: `hook_additional_context` leads with `agent.communication.channel` when its FIRST content string is a csift envelope, and `queued_command` is the person's own mid-turn steer when the record's `origin.kind` says `human`.

### 5.2 The selector law

Three forms, and the first is what a coarse query means:

- a bare **ROLE** (`user`) or **CATEGORY** (`agent.message`, `harness.turn`) selects only the leaves whose content the model receives - the "show me the conversation" ask. Both are `is_bare_face`, and only they consult the per-record delivery and survival verdicts;
- a **GLOB** (`user.*`, or any valid prefix + `.*`) selects every leaf under the prefix, visibility ignored;
- an exact **LEAF** path selects it regardless of visibility - a deliberate drill-down names what it wants.

No `-t` means every label. `-T` excludes with the same grammar, and `is_statically_empty` bails ONLY when `-T` is what cancelled `-t`: a bare category whose whole face is invisible also matches nothing, and that is a different fact with a different remedy. `harness.bookkeeping` is the one such category today (all 13 leaves are invisible): the selector is accepted, matches nothing BY THE LAW, and `search` prints one stderr note naming the remedy (`-t harness.bookkeeping.*`) beside the ordinary empty-result diagnosis, with `empty_visible_face` in the JSON summary. The set is derived (`cli::empty_visible_faces`), so a visibility change moves it without a second edit.

**Retired spellings are HARD ERRORS naming WHY the spelling is not a selector and where its records went** - no alias, no forwarding arm, no `labels[]` twin. The message carries `('<spelling>' <reason>, so this is: <successors>.)` ahead of the valid-selector list, and the middle column is a REASON rather than a release: the only reader of it holds a binary on which the spelling is already gone, so which release retired it changes nothing, while the reason carries to the next spelling they guess. Three reasons cover the thirteen rows - a flat spelling against a `role.category.leaf` taxonomy, a category decided by the producer that wrote the record, and a leaf naming one shape - and the successors below are the strings each error prints, which is what a test holds `SKILL.md` section 10 to row by row:

| retired | successors the error names |
|---|---|
| `harness.meta` (bare) | `harness.hook.*` · `harness.command.caveat` · `harness.enforce.retry` · `harness.schedule.tick` · `harness.schedule.event` · `harness.context.*` · `harness.turn.*` · `harness.system.*` · `harness.bookkeeping.*` · `agent.message.structured` |
| `harness.meta.hook` | `harness.hook.context` (injected text) · `harness.hook.feedback` (a blocking hook's, over four names) · `harness.command.caveat` · `harness.enforce.retry` (the edit-failed-retry nudge) |
| `harness.meta.loop` | `harness.schedule.tick` |
| `harness.meta.attachment` | `harness.context.*` · `harness.hook.outcome` · `agent.message.structured` |
| `harness.meta.turn-duration` / `-away-summary` / `-stop-hooks` | `harness.turn.duration` / `.recap` / `.stop-hooks` |
| `harness.meta.snapshot` | `harness.bookkeeping.file-history` |
| `harness.meta.system` | `harness.system.{refusal,api-error,notice,kill,command}` · `harness.schedule.event` |
| `thinking` / `tool` / `tool-response` | `agent.thinking` / `agent.tool` / `agent.tool.result` |
| `harness.schedule.continuation` | `harness.resume.prompt` |

`agent.message` and `agent.thinking` are NOT retired - they are CATEGORIES, so they parse and select their face. A reader expecting either to be a LEAF gets that face instead, and the leaves under them carry the JSON `label` `agent.message.plain` / `agent.thinking.summary`.

`--additional-context` is retired the same way (a hidden tombstone flag that bails): it gated ONE leaf, and that leaf has a name. `--attachments` keeps its meaning - admit every `type:"attachment"` line for a pattern scan, which equals selecting every attachment-carried leaf with `.*`.

### 5.3 The SHAPE inventory (`model::inventory`)

One row per on-disk shape csift has modeled, along the five axes above: what leaf it carries (or none, where the leaf is decided deeper or csift models none), what admits it to a scan, whether the harness converts it for the model, and whether a person sees it. The candidate gates DERIVE from it (`cli::gated_leaves`, `system_line_leaves`, `attachment_leaves`), the drift tests pin it against `Class::ALL` in both directions, and the `?` in the human column is an honest hole, to be closed with a measured value rather than a guess.

GATE words: `default scan` a role-bearing line already parsed · `explicit -t` the leaf, its category or a glob reaching it must be named (a bare scan never parses the line) · `-t or --attachments` either that or the wide flag · `show address only` csift models no leaf, so the line is parsed on a default scan like any other but is EMITTED only under an address, as `? unmodeled: <shape>`.


| axis | value | leaf | gate | converts for the model | human sees |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TopLevelType | `user` | (deeper axis / unmodeled) | default scan | yes | yes |
| TopLevelType | `assistant` | (deeper axis / unmodeled) | default scan | yes | yes |
| TopLevelType | `system` | (deeper axis / unmodeled) | explicit -t | no | verbose |
| TopLevelType | `attachment` | (deeper axis / unmodeled) | -t or --attachments | yes | no |

| TopLevelType | `permission-mode` | `harness.bookkeeping.mode` | explicit -t | no | indirect |

| TopLevelType | `artifact-comment-monitor` | `harness.bookkeeping.artifact` | explicit -t | no | indirect |
| TopLevelType | `artifact-autoreact-ledger` | `harness.bookkeeping.artifact` | explicit -t | no | indirect |
| TopLevelType | `file-history-snapshot` | `harness.bookkeeping.file-history` | explicit -t | no | indirect |

| TopLevelType | `cost-state` | `harness.bookkeeping.cost` | explicit -t | no | indirect |
| TopLevelType | `fork-context-ref` | `harness.bookkeeping.fork` | explicit -t | no | no |
| TopLevelType | `continued-in` | `harness.bookkeeping.continued` | explicit -t | no | indirect |
| TopLevelType | `queue-operation` | `harness.bookkeeping.queue` | explicit -t | no | no |
| SystemSubtype | `compact_boundary` | `harness.compaction.boundary` | default scan | no | yes |
| SystemSubtype | `turn_duration` | `harness.turn.duration` | explicit -t | no | (unstated) |
| SystemSubtype | `stop_hook_summary` | `harness.turn.stop-hooks` | explicit -t | no | (unstated) |

Leaves with NO shape row are listed in `inventory::UNSHAPED` with the reason: their discriminator is not a line-level field but a SECTION inside a record's text, a fact the surrounding DAG carries, or a tool the record names. The two tables partition `Class::ALL` exactly.

TWO ROWS CARRY A LEAF AND NO MATCHABLE TEXT, which is a different question from the gate: the line is parsed on a default scan and `show --line` renders it in full, but there is no text section for a pattern to match or an excerpt to window into, so the record contributes no hit to a search and no key to a census. The `image` BLOCK row and the `image-reference` template row are the same picture in two forms - an image-ONLY record is exactly that, and `csift image` (§6.9) is the surface that lists and extracts it; an image beside TEXT rides that record's own leaf and is emitted normally. The leaf is keyed on the SHAPE and `isMeta` is part of it: an image-only `type:"user"` record WITHOUT `isMeta` carries no leaf at all and an address renders it as the unmodeled unit `? unmodeled: user/image` - measured over two projects roots, every image-only record on disk is isMeta (94 and 75), so the shape the leaf names is the one Claude Code writes. The `fallback` BLOCK row is the same shape for a different reason: the API served that turn from a fallback model and wrote no text, so `agent.message.plain` records that the turn happened and there is nothing to emit. The emission sweep (`every_visible_leaf_specimen_emits_exactly_one_hit`) exempts exactly these two SPECIMENS by name, never their leaves - `agent.message.plain` is the ordinary assistant message, and exempting the leaf would exempt the whole population the sweep guards.

### 5.4 The 1:1 law

`csift show --line N` never hard-misses a PARSEABLE line. A record whose shape csift models no leaf for renders ONE unit reading `? unmodeled: <type[/subtype|/attachment.type]>` - the top-level type, plus the deeper value where the shape splits on one - with a key=value excerpt of the line's own top-level keys (values cut at 80 characters) as its body, and JSON `label: null`, `labels: []`, `unmodeled: "<shape>"`. The excerpt is fabricated, which is safe here and only here: an address needs no match, so no prefilter and no whole-file gate is reasoning about that text. It is also the ONE rendered path that walks a line's values without knowing what they are, so it obeys the sensitive-headers law: a `headers` member is elided at EVERY depth by exact name and prints as `<elided>` - stated rather than dropped, so a reader learns the set is there and that `--raw` is the way to it. The shape that motivates it is a `type:"system"` `api_error` line, which carries the failed request's whole HTTP response header set inside its `error` object. An explicit `--line` address also admits the NAMED lines past the §7d prefilter, scoped to those lines, so a `show` on a giant transcript parses a few extra lines rather than the file. A SCAN never emits an unmodeled unit (it is gated on `address.is_some()`), so no census, count or `-t` result moves; and a line that is not a JSON object at all is still a hard miss pointing at `--raw`.

### 5.5 Multi-label (one record → ≥1 leaf) + richest-view dedup
A physical record can carry several labels; `search` emits it **ONCE** under the **richest** view when a query selects ≥2 of them, while JSON `labels[]` still lists the full set.

| record (disk shape) | label A | label B | direction ⇨ | richest (dedup) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AUQ answer carrier (non-errored tool_result + answers) | `user.answer` | `agent.tool.result` | — | `user.answer` |
| plan/tool reject w/ typed msg (is_error tool_result + marker) | `user.rejection` | `agent.tool.result` | — | `user.rejection` |
| slash-command wrapper WITH args (either tag order) | `user.message` | `harness.command.invocation` | — | `user.message` (renders `/name args`) |
| `SendMessage` tool_use, `input.type=="message"` | `agent.tool.use` | `agent.communication.sent` | self ⇨ to | `…sent` |
| `SendMessage` tool_use, `input.type=="shutdown_request"` | `agent.tool.use` | `agent.communication.signal` | self ⇨ to | `…signal` |
| `Task`/`Agent`/`Workflow` spawn tool_use | `agent.tool.use` | `agent.communication.sent` | self ⇨ child | `…sent` |
| inbound `<teammate-message>`/`<agent-message>`/`<cross-session-message>` prose | `agent.communication.inbox` | — | from ⇨ self | `…inbox` |
| inbound COORDINATOR relay (isMeta, top-level `origin.kind:"coordinator"`) | `agent.communication.inbox` | — | coordinator ⇨ self | `…inbox` |
| inbound peer control payload (`{"type":sig}`) | `agent.communication.signal` | — | from ⇨ self | `…signal` |
| subagent transcript opener (the delivered spawn prompt) | `agent.communication.inbox` | — | parent ⇨ self | `…inbox` |
| subagent return (one-shot Task tool_result) | `agent.tool.result` | `agent.communication.inbox` | child ⇨ self | `…inbox` |
| `harness.notification.{subagent,task,workflow}` carrying a `<result>` (bg-agent report) | `harness.notification.*` | `agent.communication.inbox` | child ⇨ self | (per-section: both emit) |
| csift-channel delivery attachment | `agent.communication.channel` | `harness.hook.context` | from ⇨ self | `…channel` |

A batched record (≥1 `<task-notification>` and/or ≥1 inbound peer section concatenated in ONE `type:"user"` record) classifies **PER SECTION** (the labels are the UNION), and `search` renders **one hit per section**; a peer tag merely QUOTED inside a notification span is masked by the notification (precedence). A spawn **launch-ack** (`teammate_spawned` / `Async agent launched successfully…`) is `agent.tool.result` ONLY — it is the launch confirmation, not the child's return.

### 5.6 Direction (`from ⇨ to`): where each side is read from
Every `agent.communication.*` hit renders a direction (`Record::direction`); the transcript owner's own id renders as the literal `self`.
- `<teammate-message>`/`<agent-message>`: the `teammate_id`/`from` attribute = FROM; owner = TO (`from ⇨ self`).
- `<cross-session-message>`: FROM = the `from-name` attribute when present, else `from` — `from` is a transport address (`uds:<socket path>`, a bridge id), `from-name` is the sending session's display name, so the name is the readable side. On a record carrying exactly ONE peer section the structured `origin.name` wins over the attribute; they are the same value (Claude Code fills `origin` by re-parsing the tag), and a BATCHED record reads each section's own attribute instead.
- `SendMessage`: FROM = self; `input.to`/`recipient` = TO (`self ⇨ to`).
- spawn tool_use: FROM = self; TO = the spawned child (id-join on the spawn `tool_use_id`, else the name-join, else the raw spawn name / `?`).
- subagent opener: `parent ⇨ self`. subagent return / `<result>` pulse: `child ⇨ self` (child via the embedded `<tool-use-id>`).
- csift-channel delivery: FROM = the envelope header's `from=` value (a lane id, or `external:<label>`); owner = TO. A continuation chunk names no sender and therefore renders no direction.

### 5.7 Render markers + selector grammar
- **Role glyph** leads every hit line: **`◂` user · `▸` agent · `⚙` harness** (gear = machinery). `agent.communication.*` is agent-side, so `▸`.
- **`⇨`** (hollow arrow) = the comm direction `<from> ⇨ <to>`. **`▹`** = ONLY the `agent.tool.use ▹ agent.tool.result` pairing (joined by `tool_use_id`; an unreturned use → `agent.tool.use (no result — pending)`, an orphan result → `agent.tool.result (use not in scope)`).
- **Selector grammar**: §5.2. A `?` glyph leads an UNMODELED unit (§5.4) - it has no role to read, so it borrows none.

### 5.8 Record-level delivery: what a bare ROLE or CATEGORY selector means

A bare ROLE or CATEGORY selector claims to show the conversation the model received.
That is a per-RECORD fact, not a per-leaf one, and Claude Code decides it with drop
predicates of its own (claim CLS-026 for the request assembler, REC-109 for the
attachment converter). Where a predicate disagrees with the leaf table of §5.3,
`Record::delivery_override` mirrors it and nothing else:

| record shape | leaf default | the harness | csift |
|---|---|---|---|
| `type:"system"` + `subtype:"local_command"` | VISIBLE (`harness.system.command`) | RE-MINTS it as a user message and sends it | `Some(true)`: the leaf default and the predicate now agree, and the override states the producer's own verdict per record |
| `type:"attachment"` whose payload type the converter DROPS (nine of them) | visible for most of those leaves | converts it to nothing | `Some(false)`: a bare role or category hides it |
| `type:"attachment"` + `attachment.type:"hook_success"` | invisible (`harness.hook.outcome`) | converts it exactly when the record carries a rendered body | `Some(rendered.is_some())` |
| `type:"user"`/`"assistant"` + `isVirtual:true` | visible | drops it (display-only by construction) | `Some(false)`: a bare role hides it |
| `type:"assistant"` + `isApiErrorMessage:true` + `message.model=="<synthetic>"` | visible | drops it (claim CLS-027) | `Some(false)`: a bare role hides it |

Every other record carries no override, so its leaf default stands. The assembler's two
remaining arms need none: it KEEPS an `api_system` record and drops a `progress` record,
and csift models no leaf for either type, so neither is selectable at all.

ONE leaf is exempt from the override, and it is the single stated exception to the
visible-face law: **`agent.message.structured`**. Its carrier is a `structured_output`
attachment, which is a converter-dropped payload type, so the override reads `Some(false)`
and the rule above would take the leaf out of `-t agent` and `-t agent.message` - leaving
the bare form of a two-leaf category holding only its plain half, with nothing in the
output to say the other half exists. The axis asks whether the model RECEIVED the record;
this leaf is the one case where the model WROTE it, and a bare face asks for the
conversation, of which a subagent's own answer is part whatever the assembler later did
with the line. The API offers a turn free text or a schema-bound answer and never both, so
a reader asking for `agent.message` is asking for whichever one that turn used
(`cli::selectors::keeps_face_against_delivery`). The exception moves FACE MEMBERSHIP only:
the record was not delivered, so the hit's `delivered` field still reads false and an
explicit selector still renders `[not delivered]`.

Only the BARE-FACE forms consult the override. A glob (`-t 'agent.*'`) and an exact leaf
path keep their full sets, so every record
stays reachable by naming it; `-T` speaks the same rule on the exclude side. The label
SET is untouched (`labels[]` is classification, never the delivery verdict); the verdict
rides beside it as the JSON hit field `delivered` (the override, else the leaf default)
and, wherever that field reads false - the override or the leaf's own visibility, under
whichever selector reached the record - as the display-only label-zone marker
`[not delivered]` on `search` and `show` alike.

Candidate admission follows: the `harness.system` leaves are reachable only under an
explicit selector, so a bare `-t harness` could not have seen the delivered record at all. The
key-only `"subtype"` needle is therefore also enabled under a bare `harness` role
(`SearchArgs::scans_system_lines`), and the per-record override drops the rest of what
that admission parsed. The §7f whole-file gate's synthesized-marker set reads the SAME
predicate: a narrower set would prune a file whose only hit lies in the fabricated
`[<subtype> <level>]` head, which is not a raw byte substring of the line. Every other
gated leaf is unchanged, and `gated_leaves_unreached` now reports what the scan actually
parsed.

Measured on the reference corpus, 72 top-level transcripts, one observation per record:
a bare `-t harness` reaches 16 `system`/`local_command` records (12 carrying a
`<local-command-stdout>` body, 4 being command echoes), and 118 records sit OUTSIDE
`agent.message.plain` under the override - the whole population carrying
`isApiErrorMessage` (118 of 118 also carry the `<synthetic>` model).

### 5.9 Record-level survival: the THIRD exclusion axis

A bare ROLE selector narrows on three axes, applied in this order, and only the first is
per-leaf:

1. **Leaf visibility** (§5.3): 28 of the 73 leaves sit outside a bare face by default
   (`Class::llm_visible()` is false for exactly these), and they split by WHY. Most are
   non-message line kinds, and ONE instrument covers them: none carries a `message{}`
   field, while every conversation record does - `user.queued` and all thirteen
   `harness.bookkeeping` leaves, the message-less `type:"system"` records (the compaction
   boundary, the scheduler's fire record, the three `harness.turn` telemetry leaves and
   four of the five `harness.system` event kinds), and `harness.hook.outcome`, a hook
   RUN's receipt the harness converts to nothing. The two textless thinking leaves
   (`omitted`, `redacted`) carry a signature the model echoes back and no text it could
   read. And the two SCAN-LAYER user leaves, `user.unsent` and `user.rewound`, are
   ordinary user records whose line shape excludes nothing; what excludes them is axis 3
   below, which is why this law needs three axes and not one.
2. **Delivery** (§5.8): a per-RECORD mirror of Claude Code's own drop predicates - the
   request assembler's three cases plus the attachment converter's two - disagreeing with
   the leaf table in exactly those five.
3. **Survival** (§4.11): a per-RECORD verdict from Claude Code's own conversation chain.
   A record the chain does not reach is not in the conversation the model received, so
   a bare role skips it.

The three are independent and the second and third are the harness's own rules, not
csift's readings of them: a record can be a visible leaf, delivered when it was written,
and abandoned today. Every OTHER selector form is untouched by all three: a glob
(`-t 'user.*'`), an intermediate prefix (`-t agent.tool`) and a full leaf path reach every
record whatever the three axes say, and an excluded record then renders its reason in the
label zone (`[not delivered]` wherever the JSON `delivered` reads false, which is axis 2
and the invisible leaves of axis 1; `[abandoned]`, `[rewound]` or `[pre-cut]` for axis 3). A `PreCut` record is NOT excluded by axis 3: csift declines to narrow on a
verdict it could not resolve.

JSON carries the verdicts beside the labels, never inside them: `delivered` for axis 2,
`survival` plus `abandoned_root_line` and `replay_copy_of` for axis 3. `labels[]` stays
classification.

---

## 6. Subcommand specifications

> **v0.13.0 CHANGE LEDGER (BREAKING on two counts: the label taxonomy is rewritten, and one session id on disk under two project directories is MERGED, so `sessions_in_scope`, every spanning count and the `list` row count all change on a scope holding one; `csift 0.13.0`).**
> ① **The source tree is re-managed.** `src/` stood at BOTH structure caps - 20 counted direct `.rs` files and 16 subfolders - and a cap that binds is a refactor gate, not a number to raise. The Bash LEXICAL layers move under one path-named root: `src/bash/danger.rs` + `src/bash/danger/{argv,census,lexical,out,removal,shape}.rs` (formerly the seven `src/bash_danger*.rs` files, which were siblings of the crate root precisely because a seventeenth subfolder would have failed the gate) and `src/bash/mutations.rs` + `src/bash/mutations/` (formerly `src/bash_mutations`); the five `bash_danger_*_tests.rs` files lose their `#[path]` attributes and become ordinary `tests` children of the modules that declare them. THIRTEEN SUBCOMMAND FAMILIES then move under `src/commands/` - `agents`, `elicitation`, `files`, `image`, `live` (channel included), `plan`, `recover`, `search`, `session`, `show`, `stats`, `turns`, `whoami` - leaving `src/` as the BASE LAYER those build on: `main.rs` plus `cli`, `model`, `parse`, `path`, `subagent`, `bash`, `chardiff`, `text`, `time_window`, `timez`. Dispatch reads `Command::Foo(args) => commands::foo::run_foo(&args)`. Finally the four `src/model/classify_*.rs` siblings become children of the engine that uses them - `src/model/classify/{support,channel,resume}.rs` plus the promoted-leaf table, which item ③ then replaces with the five family tables - with the `support` glob still re-exported from the model root so every `crate::model::` path that resolved before resolves now; `Record::direction` moves verbatim into a fifth child `classify/direction.rs`, because the engine sat at 599 of its 600 permitted lines and could not host four `mod` declarations - a cap that binds is a refactor gate, not a number to raise. The engine's CASES follow the same nesting (`src/model/tests/classify_*.rs` becomes children under the engine, and item ③ moves them again to `src/model/classify/tests/`, one file per family), which also takes `src/model/tests/` off the 20-file cap it was sitting at; and the five children are declared `pub(super)`, the narrowest visibility that still lets the model root re-export `classify::support::*`. Every `crate::<old>::` path is respelled at every call site in src and tests; no alias, no re-export and no forwarding shim is left behind, so an old spelling is a compile error rather than a deprecation. No logic line changes, and no output byte moves: `--help` is byte-identical for the root and all seventeen subcommands against a build of the BASE COMMIT (measured against an INSTALLED 0.12.3 it differs on one line, `[alias: --dry-run]` against `[aliases: --dry-run]` in `recover --help`, which is clap rendering its own `visible_alias` and moves with the clap patch version rather than with csift), the suite ran 1548 unit + 889 e2e + 1 ignored at that commit - the unit count unchanged and the e2e count one higher, for the single case that pins the external-write interval join (it reads both path spellings, and closes a version sequence over an entry whose version cannot be read) after a respelled call was reflowed across more lines than the old one - and a corpus differential over `list`/`search`/`stats`/`files`/`verbatim`/`show`/`recover`/`image`/`agents`/`whoami` compares byte-identical on stdout, stderr and exit code.
> ② **The label taxonomy becomes 17 CATEGORIES over 73 LEAVES, split by PRODUCER** (§5; claims REC-108, REC-109, CLS-033). The old 38-leaf space had one bag in it: `harness.meta` pooled hook-injected text, a hook run's own record, a slash command's caveat, the loop tick, the end-of-turn telemetry and the file-history instrument, with `.attachment` and `.system` as catch-alls for everything else - so a selector could not name what it wanted and a census could not say what a scope held. The new space asks ONE question of every record, WHAT WROTE IT, and the answer is its category. `Class` carries the leaves, `Class::CATEGORIES` the middle tier, `Class::category` the facet a selector reads, `Class::llm_visible` the 28 leaves whose content the model never receives. Renames land 1:1 (`agent.message` -> `agent.message.plain`, `agent.thinking` -> `agent.thinking.summary`, `harness.meta.loop` -> `harness.schedule.tick`, the three `harness.meta` telemetry leaves -> `harness.turn.*`, `harness.meta.snapshot` -> `harness.bookkeeping.file-history`); `harness.meta.hook` splits FOUR ways by producer (`harness.hook.context` for injected text, `harness.hook.feedback` for a blocking hook's, over the four names one formatter writes, `harness.command.caveat` for the slash-command executor's, `harness.enforce.retry` for the edit-failed nudge), and the two catch-alls become per-value tables. Every retired spelling is a HARD ERROR naming its successors - thirteen of them - and there is no alias, no forwarding arm and no `labels[]` twin. `agent.message` and `agent.thinking` are not retired: they were leaf paths and are now CATEGORIES, so they still parse.
> ③ **`classify` becomes a dispatch over five MECHANICAL family tables** (§5.1): a top-level line `type` (`classify/bookkeeping.rs`), a `type:"system"` subtype (`system.rs`), an attachment payload `type` (`attachments.rs`), a content block's shape (`thinking.rs`) and an isMeta-user template (`injections.rs`). Each is a value in and a leaf out with no look at the payload, which is what makes the mapping checkable against the shape inventory rather than readable only as control flow. Three behaviour changes ride it, each a leaf reaching records that carried NONE: every non-transcript line type now carries a `harness.bookkeeping.*` leaf (a `queue-operation` line that is not the human's text included), an attachment carries the leaf of its own payload type instead of one catch-all, and a thinking block with no summary text or a `redacted_thinking` block takes its own leaf instead of both reading as plain reasoning. A value csift models no leaf for carries none - which §5.4 then renders rather than hiding.
> ④ **`harness.schedule.fire` changes its GATE, not its meaning** (claim CLS-031, refined). It keyed on `promptSource:"system"`, a stamp the submit path puts on EVERY isMeta submission and which the builds before it wrote on nothing; it now keys on the PARENT LINK - the prompt's `parentUuid` names the `system`/`scheduled_task_fire` record the scheduler wrote just before it, joined through `ScheduleFireIndex`, which gained a structural half beside its instant map so a fire record with an unreadable instant still gates the prompt under it. That link is the only mark the producer leaves on the prompt itself, since the armed text reaches the record verbatim. The two peer guards are unchanged.
> ⑤ **The SHAPE INVENTORY (`model::inventory`, §5.3)**: one row per on-disk shape on the five axes above - 22 top-level types, 11 system subtypes, 50 attachment payload types, 8 content block types, 17 isMeta template families - each naming its leaf, its gate, whether the harness converts it for the model and whether a person sees it. It is a RECORD of the mapping, never a second one: a unit test walks every row back through the family table that decides the leaf, and another asserts the shaped leaves and a hand-kept `UNSHAPED` table partition `Class::ALL` exactly. The candidate GATES derive from it (`gated_leaves`, `system_line_leaves`, `attachment_leaves`), so the honest-empties note can never name a stale leaf. Fifteen needles join the §7d prefilter for the remaining non-transcript line types, each a quoted VALUE behind its own leaf's bit in one mask, and the whole group short-circuits on a zero mask - a default scan pays for none of them. A bookkeeping unit renders its key=value HEAD followed by the line's own VERBATIM JSON as the body (clause ⑮ carries the law and what it fixes); today twelve of the thirteen leaves render body-only, because only `file-history` has a head. The body half is the choice `--attachments` already makes for a payload and for the same reason: a byte substring of the source line keeps the §7d prefilter and the §7f whole-file gate sound with no synthesized marker, while a fabricated head registers its own.
> ⑥ **A bare CATEGORY narrows exactly as a bare ROLE does** (§5.2), because both are the coarse "show me the conversation" ask; a glob and an exact leaf still reach everything. `harness.bookkeeping` is the one category whose whole face is invisible: the selector is ACCEPTED, matches nothing by the law, and `search` prints one stderr note naming the remedy beside the ordinary empty-result diagnosis (JSON `empty_visible_face`). `is_statically_empty` bails only when `-T` is what cancelled `-t`, so the honest empty and the contradiction stay different answers with different remedies.
> ⑦ **`--additional-context` is RETIRED** (a hidden tombstone flag that bails, the `turns` precedent). It gated ONE leaf and that leaf now has a name: `-t harness.hook.context`. Naming any attachment-carried leaf is a gate of its own now, so the narrow ask no longer needs a flag at all; `--attachments` keeps its wide meaning. The bail is deliberate over a quiet no-op: a run that silently scanned fewer lines than the caller asked for would read as the definitive absence csift promises an empty result means.
> ⑧ **`Record::delivery_override` gains the ATTACHMENT arm** (claim REC-109): the nine payload types the harness's converter drops are NOT delivered whatever their leaf says, and a `hook_success` IS delivered exactly when the record carries a rendered body. `rendered` is a TOP-LEVEL key of the attachment RECORD, not of its payload - reading it off the payload finds it on no line of either measured corpus - and its ABSENCE is not evidence of non-delivery in general, which is why the test is narrowed to the one payload type where the coincidence was measured.
> ⑨ **The 1:1 law: `show --line N` never hard-misses a parseable line** (§5.4). A shape csift models no leaf for renders `? unmodeled: <shape>` with the line's own top-level keys, and JSON carries `unmodeled` beside a null `label`. A SCAN never emits one, so no census, count or `-t` result moves; a line that is not a JSON object at all is still a hard miss pointing at `--raw`. The fetch domain is now exactly what its name always said - before this, every bookkeeping line type read as absent. Two EXTENSION POINTS land with their first users, so a family adds a variant rather than a field: `Hit.markers` carries display-only label-zone notes through one renderer, and `Hit.facts` carries per-record facts that project themselves into the label zone and into JSON.
> ⑩ **Two files hit the 600-line cap and were re-managed** rather than squeezed: `record_text.rs` and `scan.rs` are paired roots now, with the non-message renders (`record_text/promoted.rs`) and the candidate gates (`scan/gates.rs`) in their own children.
> ⑪ **The mechanical detection covers every row of the shape inventory, and the 1:1 law reaches message records.** The first skeleton commit advertised leaves no arm could produce - `harness.context.image`, `.skill`, the four `harness.enforce.*` - and the drift test walked three of the five axes, so the gap was invisible. The isMeta-template table now carries an arm per row, each keyed on the literal its producer writes: the image reference by its two openings, a preloaded skill body by its first line, the three enforcement sentinels by theirs, and the retry nudges by EXACT equality over four texts, which is how Claude Code's own recogniser tests them. Two arms are structural: `origin.kind == "human"` makes a relayed message the PERSON's and leads the table, and the coordinator relay takes the peer relay's leaf. A `<system-reminder>` matching no more specific template lands under `harness.context.reminder`. The `[SYSTEM NOTIFICATION - NOT USER INPUT]` frame joins the relay preambles as a section BOUNDARY - a conjunction with its own opening, per C-40 - so the pulse it wraps classifies by its own kind instead of vanishing. An image-only block record takes the same leaf its text form does, and `dynamic_skill` (one specimen over 9,484 files on one corpus, none over 10,299 on the other; keys `skillDir`, `skillNames`, `displayPath`) is a listing. The drift test covers all five axes with one hand-written specimen per block and template row. On the fetch side, an ADDRESSED line keeps its raw bytes whatever its role marker says, so a message record with no renderable text renders a unit instead of a miss, naming the axis its leaf would have come from (`user/document`, `user/isMeta`); `? (no label)` is retired, leaving ONE unmodeled form that always names a value; a record that HAS a leaf and merely lacks text keeps it; and an assistant turn the API served from a fallback model takes `agent.message.plain` with an empty body. `show`'s address-miss text says what a miss MEANS rather than listing a fetch domain that is now everything parseable.
> ⑫ **`wait`'s activity census counts all four thinking leaves.** It named `agent.thinking` and its narration sibling one by one, so when the leaf became four the two new ones silently stopped counting - an empty-text block is still a block the model produced. The census keys on the CATEGORY, as the sibling cap already does.
> ⑬ **The coarse attachment keep stops riding the two leaves every query names** (§7d). `user.message` and `agent.message.structured` are carried by an attachment as a second VIEW - a `queued_command` payload whose origin says the human wrote it, a `structured_output` payload - rather than as its kind, and both sat in the set that turns on the keep admitting EVERY attachment line. So `-t user`, `-t user.message` and `-t agent` parsed every attachment line in the transcript and skipped the flagless demotion with them: measured on a synthetic 89 MB transcript of 150,000 `file` attachment lines plus one user line, 236 ms against a flagless scan's 70 ms. Each is reached through a SINGLE payload type, so each takes its own quoted VALUE needle under its own bit, the fifteen bookkeeping needles' shape; the coarse keep stays for the wide flag, the census axis and the leaves whose payload types are too many to name, and the mask is an EXPLICIT-selector gate, so a flagless scan parses only the lines the DEFAULT-ON needle admits. BOTH needles take the v0.11.0 CHANNEL keep's gate - on unless the label set EXCLUDES the leaf, so `-t harness` still short-circuits before the memmem - and that is the selector law rather than a budget. A flagless scan is a SUPERSET of `-t user` and of `-t agent`, so neither `user.message`, which an attachment carries when the harness folded in the person's own mid-turn steer, nor `agent.message.structured`, which the MODEL produced, can be a leaf the narrower query reaches and the wider one misses. The reason the channel keep was made default-on is the same one: a message no ordinary query surfaces is a message nobody can audit, and both of these leaves are words someone owned. What stays gated is everything ELSE an attachment can carry - the coarse keep is untouched, so a flagless scan still parses no `harness.context.*` or `harness.hook.*` payload. The needle only ADMITS the line and the PARSE decides the leaf: a `queued_command` whose payload `origin.kind` is not `human` classifies `harness.context.task` behind that leaf's own gate. The demotion stands down PER RECORD - for a record that IS one of the two views and whose own needle admitted it, never for the gated attachment machinery beside it - so a flagless scan gains those two and nothing else. **What this does NOT close is the class of cost.** A selector reaching a `harness.context.*` or `harness.hook.*` leaf that has no needle of its own still turns the COARSE keep on and parses every attachment line, and a bare `-t harness` reaches those leaves by the selector law, so it pays that too. That is by design: forty payload types cannot each carry a value needle, and the two that do are the two an ordinary conversation query names. Measured on the synthetic 85 MB transcript of 150,000 `file` attachment lines plus one user line, 21 reps per arm, interleaved, medians, with a pattern that MATCHES the single user line - a non-matching pattern is the wrong instrument here, because the §7f whole-file gate then proves the file cannot match and skips every keep, so all four arms read about 20 ms and the cost under test never runs: flagless 73.3 ms, `-t harness` 251.0 ms (3.4x), `-t harness.context.file` 359.5 ms, `-t user` 84.6 ms. The cost is the COARSE keep rather than the breadth of the selector, which is why naming one `harness.context.*` leaf pays it too. Medians of 9 interleaved reps against the commit before: `-t user` 0.283x, `-t user.message` 0.281x, `-t agent` 0.288x, flagless 1.000x, `--attachments` 0.974x over 15 reps; against a flagless scan on the same binary the three selectors read 0.95x, 0.97x and 0.98x.
> ⑭ **A leaf only an ADDRESS can reach is a defect, and it is now SWEPT** (§5.4). The coordinator relay classifies `agent.communication.inbox` and emitted nothing at all. Its arm rendered through `reconstructed_user_text`, which answers only for a record that OPENS a turn, and the relay is an isMeta record the harness wrote into the conversation, so it opens none; it is no attachment either, so the ⑬ fallback beside it did not reach it. The record therefore carried a delivered message that no hit, no census key and no `-t agent.communication.inbox` could surface - 141 such records across 89 files of one projects root and 57 across 49 of a second, every one reachable by `show --line` alone (counting rule: one per record whose `type` is "user", whose `isMeta` is true and whose flattened text opens with the preamble after a left trim). The arm falls back to the record's own raw text, which is what the peer relay beside it already did. **Twice now the same defect has landed on a different carrier** (the person's queued steer was the first, item ⑬), so the pin generalises from a third one-off case into a SWEEP: one test walks every specimen of the shape inventory and asserts that a FLAGLESS scan emits exactly ONE hit for each record carrying a leaf the model receives. Two specimens carry such a leaf and have no text section at all - an image-only record, which is a picture a pattern scan has nothing to match in, and an assistant turn the API served from a fallback model, which wrote no text - and each is exempted BY SPECIMEN with its reason written at it, never by leaf: `agent.message.plain` is the ordinary assistant message, so exempting the LEAF would exempt the whole population the sweep exists to guard.
> ⑮ **The six taxonomy families get ONE stamping site, one matchable-text law and one help section each.** The extension points `Hit.markers` and `Hit.facts` existed with nothing filling them, so each family cut its own dispatch in the same three shared files and the six cuts did not compose. `search::family_facts` is that one seam: it dispatches in a fixed order to `record_text::<family>::facts(rec, raw, section, ctx)` and is called at BOTH hit-construction sites, so a record fetched by `show --line` carries every fact the same record carries under `search`; `Section` is the per-UNIT half, carrying the source BLOCK so a per-block fact is possible. Three shared mechanisms land with it. `ClassifyCtx::line_of_uuid` resolves a uuid a record NAMES to that record's LINE, backed by the chain's OWN uuid map (`Chain::build_by_indexed` hands it back instead of dropping it), so no family builds a second index and a caller with no row space gets an honest null. `Record::attachment_value` memoises its parse on the record, so the four layers that ask one attachment about its payload pay one parse. And the section-7f whole-file gate's stage 2 now re-renders from the record's SOURCE LINE: a bookkeeping line renders from the line or not at all, so without it the gate could prove a file unmatchable on text the emission would have produced. The MATCHABLE-TEXT LAW is the render half (§5): for a line whose content lives in fields `Record` does not model, the family's key=value view is the HEAD and the line's verbatim JSON is the BODY, so a key the summary elides stays findable - applied to the thirteen `harness.bookkeeping` leaves, and deliberately NOT to `harness.system.*`, whose `api_error` line carries the HTTP response headers csift prints on no path but `--raw`. `search --help` and `show --help` stop being single strings every lane edits: the search body is the byte-join of an intro, its closing notes and one section file per family, and `show`'s record-row key list is rendered from a table with a family column. The SPLIT moved no rendered byte - both helps were rendered before and after and compared - and that is a statement about the split, not a standing property: a family filling its section is what the split is for. `search --help` now carries the first of it, a `BOOKKEEPING LEAVES (harness.bookkeeping)` region stating the render law above; `show --help` renders exactly what it did, no family having added a key yet. Each family region is a column-0 head with a two-space body, placed AFTER the JSON schema block and its closing notes - a render law is not a subsection of the JSON schema - and an EMPTY section renders nothing at all, so the five unwritten ones cost the page no space. No test pins either help as a literal, which would go stale the first time a lane wrote a sentence: the frame test asserts that the rendered body IS its sections joined, the ORDER (shared body first, family regions the tail in family order), the two seams (no trailing space where the intro ends, exactly one leading space on the closing notes) and that region shape, counting the column-0 lines of the tail against the number of filled sections.
> ⑯ **The render seam, the per-family fact enums and the one `for` edge.** The first amendment gave the families a FACTS seam and no RENDER seam, so what a line SAYS was still decided in the shared chain - `record_raw_text`'s arms, `promoted.rs`'s per-leaf match, the block loop's body decision - and it left `HitFact` as ONE enum in a 534-line `types.rs`, which the first family to fill it put at the 600-line cap. Four of the six round-one lanes filed that cap as a blocker independently and three invented a layout for it on their own branch. Both are closed here. `record_text::family_render` is the render seam: called FIRST in `record_raw_text`, dispatching by the leaf's CATEGORY through `render_families` - a TABLE rather than a match, because a contract six lanes work against is one a test must be able to walk - and EXACTLY ONE family answers for a record. There first-Some-wins is a TIE-BREAK, not a fallback chain: a second family answering behind the first means both believe they own the record, the winner is a table order no reader of the output can see, and the loser's text is discarded in silence. Release keeps the short-circuit; a DEBUG build asks every family in the list and `debug_assert!`s on a shadowed answer, and the seam test sweeps one specimen per leaf of the two shared categories naming the family that answers for each. Two categories belong to two families and the order is the fact (`harness.hook`: an attachment payload before an isMeta feedback; `harness.schedule`: the scheduler's own record before the prompts it injected). WHICH leaf it dispatches on is `render_leaf`, and its order is what makes the seam behaviour-identical: the LINE's own leaf first, the caller's class only as the fallback, so one line renders the same text under every view of it and the isMeta injections - whose own type names no leaf - still have a way in. Four renders MOVED into their families, each in its own commit and each proved behaviour-identical rather than asserted: the two attachment arms, the bookkeeping head-and-body composition, `promoted.rs`'s six `type:"system"` event heads, and the thinking block's body (`thinking::block_body`, which takes `REDACTED_THINKING_PLACEHOLDER` with it). Two renders stay EMPTY and say why at the function - a thinking leaf is decided on a BLOCK and never renders at the record level, an injection's text is its own `message.content` - and `keys` has no render at all, its facts being keys a record exposes rather than what a line says. The receipt for every move is a corpus-derived command set run against a release binary built from the base commit: 122 of 122 (row, size class) pairs byte-identical on stdout with 0 exit-code mismatches, plus the golden baseline and the whole e2e suite. `types.rs` becomes a paired root whose child carries `Marker`, `Section` and `HitFact`, and `HitFact` carries ONE arm per family forwarding to an enum in that family's own file - so after this a family edits neither. `HitFact::json` returns a LIST of pairs (a family fact can be several keys) and both JSON projectors fold through one rule, `attach_facts`, where an ARRAY value appends and anything else replaces. That is what carries the `for` EDGE, which lands ONCE instead of three times: `HitFact::For { kind, basis, target }` with `ForUnknown` as its honest empty, built through `record_text::for_edge` - the only place the uuid lookup happens - giving three answers, a LINE when this transcript carries the uuid, the id with `(not in this file)` when it does not, and `unknown (<reason>)` when the record names nothing at all. Every edge projects the one `for` key carrying a one-element array, so several on one unit accumulate rather than overwrite; `Basis` (`key` | `position` | `inferred`) is the honesty column and is never defaulted; a DESCENT edge is not this shape and a family owning one writes `ForUnknown` with that as the reason. Two structure caps bound while this landed and each was answered by its own commit before the change that tripped it: `record_text.rs` gains a `seam.rs` child (586 -> 472 lines; the file's high-water mark was 600 at the commit that cut the seam, five commits before the split, and 599 at the one after) and `commands/session/summarize.rs` a `clear.rs` one (573 -> 388), the second ahead of the feature that would have taken it to 594. Finally the skeleton's seam probe asserts PRESENCE rather than vector equality: it pins the wiring, not a family's output, and an equality there made the first per-block family fact a failure in a file that family does not own.
> ⑰ **The `harness.bookkeeping` family fills its render and its relations.** The matchable-text law landed with twelve of the thirteen leaves rendering the line alone, so a reader who named a leaf still read raw JSON to learn what the line said. SIXTEEN of the eighteen non-transcript line types now build a `[<line type> key=value ...]` head from their OWN BYTES - the render parses the source line, which is affordable because every bookkeeping leaf is gated and a scan that did not name it never made the line a parse candidate - and the head opens with the line's own `type`, so `mode`/`permission-mode`, `ai-title`/`agent-name` and the three artifact ledgers stay apart in the output though their label does not tell them apart. The OTHER TWO are `file-history-snapshot` and `file-history-delta`, one leaf, which keep the head `promoted_record_text` builds from the fields `Record` models: that one names the tracked PATH and its version where a raw key walk could only print the snapshot object as JSON, and the promoted tests pin it. The render keys are the WRITER's key order, read out of the shipped object literals (claim QT-045), and two rules decide their values with no third: an ACCOUNT, ORGANISATION or RECORD uuid prints its first 8 characters, everything else is cut at 80 with the shared `... (+N chars)` marker and an OBJECT prints as its sorted key list. A session-shaped value (`bridgeSessionId`, `parentSessionId`) falls under the second rule, because it is a join key carried to another file and half a uuid joins nothing. Because the head is FABRICATED it is not a byte substring of its line, so each line type's quoted value is registered as a VERIFIABLE synth marker under the SAME predicate that admits the line - a default scan arms none of them, and a query naming a bookkeeping leaf pays at most sixteen extra memmem scans per prefilter-rejected line; without the registration four of five head-only patterns on a 21-line synthetic fixture read 0 instead of 1. FIVE line types name something outside themselves. Three name a RECORD and take the shared `for` edge through `record_text::for_edge`: `last-prompt` -> `leaf` (its `leafUuid`), a file-history line -> `versions` (its `messageId`), `fork-context-ref` -> `forked_from` (its `parentLastUuid`, with `parentSessionId` on the target's `session` because that record lives in the parent's file). The other two name no RECORD, and they part company there. `continued-in` names a whole TRANSCRIPT, which the edge target carries as a SESSION with no uuid and no line, so it is the FOURTH edge and renders `[for: continued_in @<session>]`. `cost-state` names an INSTANT, which no target shape can carry without putting a timestamp in a field documented as the target record's uuid, so it alone stays a `bookkeeping::Fact`: `[checkpoint closes <instant>]` with JSON `checkpoint_closes_at` plus `checkpoint_join`, the `startTime + totalDuration` sum `list` joins a cleared session on as `cleared_from` (claim QT-042). Each renders its honest empty when the key it reads is absent. Every unit of the family carries the marker `[not delivered]`, the engine's own word: the JSON `delivered` field has always read false for all thirteen leaves and the text surface said nothing, because the engine prints that marker only for a record carrying a per-record delivery override and a bookkeeping line's invisibility comes from its leaf. `show --format json` gains three record-row keys through its table (`for`, `checkpoint_closes_at`, `checkpoint_join`); the handover relation is an ENTRY of that `for` array rather than a key of its own, so a `continued-in` line's row carries the edge and no `continued_in` key. Claims QT-045 to QT-051, QT-054 and QT-055 carry the facts.
> ⑱ **The THINKING family: two EMPTY bodies, four label-zone markers and two per-block keys** (claims CLS-037, CLS-039, CLS-040, with CLS-018 / REC-010 / REC-012 / NAR-001 / NAR-011 refined and CLS-038 / NAR-012 added). `agent.thinking.redacted` rendered the fabricated words `[redacted thinking]` as its BODY - matchable text with no substring behind it in the raw line, which the 7d prefilter and the 7f whole-file gate both reason about and which §4 admits only through a registered synth marker. `agent.thinking.omitted` had the mirror defect: the leaf is assigned on `trim().is_empty()`, so a whitespace-only block promised no text and printed a run of spaces. BOTH bodies are now empty, decided in the family's own `block_body`, and the CONSEQUENCE is documented rather than left to be met: with nothing rendered, neither leaf is reachable by a pattern scan at all - the ways in are an empty pattern plus an exact `-t`, `--count-by label`, or a `show` address, and `--raw` remains the only path to a redacted block's ciphertext. What each block IS moved to the LABEL ZONE, where a fabricated tag is display-only: `[no summary text]`, `[redacted thinking]`, and two that name text the CLIENT wrote into the assistant's role - `[cancelled stream salvage]` on a thinking block with text and an EMPTY signature carried by a record wearing the `<synthetic>` model sentinel (it keeps `agent.thinking.summary`, because the block IS a summary slot in the message, and its DELIVERY is read off the persisted flags: the `isVirtual` the constructor sets is stripped on the persist path and reaches no line, so a bare `-t agent` reaches it), and `[thinking removed by the client]` on an `agent.message.plain` block whose WHOLE trimmed text is `[Thinking removed]`, the stand-in the strip path pushes when removing every thinking block would have emptied the message. Two per-hit JSON keys ride every thinking BLOCK unit: `signature_len`, the CHARACTER length of the block's own `signature` - `0` when the field is present and empty, `null` when the block carries no such field, because absent and empty are different shapes and the salvage discriminator rests on exactly that difference - and `thinking_text_absent`, true on the omitted leaf alone. Both are named in `search --help`'s THINKING LEAVES region and in the `show --help` record-row key list. (Six family lanes each add one clause here; the ordinal is the merge's to reconcile.)
> ⑲ **The isMeta INJECTION family: the anchoring law, the edges, and the two records in it that are a person or another session.** These are the shapes with no FIELD to key on - the harness writes them as `type:"user"` records shaped exactly like the operator's own message, one fixed template per producer - so the arm that detects one IS the whole guard. Three arms were loose and are anchored: the hook-feedback and `<local-command-caveat>` arms tested the UNTRIMMED string and the edit-failed-retry notice tested `contains`, which on the isMeta-user path can only reach a record whose body QUOTES it. Censused over two projects roots, one count per isMeta user record, the notice occurs anywhere on 1 and 2 records and at content start on 0 and 0, every one of the three opening `Stop hook feedback:` instead - a body the arm above already claims - and the left trim newly reaches 0 records for either anchored prefix, so the emitted leaf moves for 0 of 3,713 and 0 of 1,825 records. `harness.hook.feedback` widens from the one name the corpus holds to all FOUR the producer's closed table carries (claim CLS-035): a blocking TeammateIdle, TaskCreated or TaskCompleted hook would otherwise land in the residue the first time one fires, and csift's prefixes stop at the colon while the producer's separator carries a newline, so the arm is one character wider on purpose.
