Every unit of the family but three carries one `for` EDGE, read from `parentUuid` - which the append path stamps from the running tail of the transcript, with nothing on that path reading `isMeta` (claim CLS-049; measured, 3,179 of 3,180 bucketed records in one root carry a parent and all 3,179 resolve in-file, the exception a `<local-command-caveat>` record with no parent key; 1,218 of 1,218 in the other) - or from the `<tool-use-id>` a notification frame's wrapped pulse names, which is a wire id and renders with the builder's `not in this file` note. `harness.schedule.wakeup`, `harness.schedule.tick` and `harness.resume.prompt` carry none, because no key on those records names one. TWO records here are a person or another session: the relayed human message (`origin.kind` `human`), which is `user.message` with the `[relayed]` marker plus its `relayed` JSON twin and opens NO turn; and the inbound relays, where the arm keys on `origin.kind` rather than on the leaf, because a coordinator relay and a peer relay carry `agent.communication.inbox` alike and each owes its own reason - the coordinator's origin carries only its kind, the peer's names the lane that sent it (`[peer lane <id>]`, JSON `peer_sender_task_id`; 50 of 55 and 109 of 109 by the origin census of claim CLS-046). Finally a TEXTLESS image record renders its block counts (`[context image-only: N image block(s), M document block(s)]`, JSON `image_only`) BESIDE the key=value excerpt it still falls through to, so that excerpt - the head of a base64 payload among its values - stops being the only answer an address gives. The counts ride the LABEL ZONE rather than the unit's text, which is why nothing a scan matches is fabricated and also why the excerpt stays: the body is the line's own top-level keys, rendered by the shared address path every textless line reaches, and a family fact is ADDED to a unit rather than substituted for its body (claim CLS-050, with the shape census: 97 of 3,713 and 78 of 1,825 isMeta user records textless, 94 and 75 image-only, 3 and 3 document-only, 0 and 0 both).
> ⑳ **An attachment unit renders the text the model received, and says which record it is for (BREAKING).** An attachment line keeps two copies of itself - the payload the harness stored, and, on a build that writes the field, the text its own converter produced, persisted back as a TOP-LEVEL `rendered`. csift rendered only the first, so `-t harness.hook.context` showed a hook's injected strings rather than the wrapped form the model read. The body rule is now three sources in one order - the conversion, then a field the payload carries (a hook run's `stdout`, a hook's `content` strings), then the verbatim payload JSON - and the unit says which it took (`[rendered]` / `[payload]`, JSON `body_source`) beside a marker naming itself (`[hook <event> <name>]`, `[hook <event> <name> exit <code> <ms>ms]`, `[context <payload type>]`, `[structured]`, `[mid-turn]`). A `queued_command` can carry a SECOND conversion whose text always differs (1,612 of 1,612 on one projects root); which one the model got depends on the turn it was replayed into, which the record does not say, so the body stays the ordinary one and the unit adds `[human-turn render differs]` rather than choosing. THE SOUNDNESS ARGUMENT is the needle law, not a substring: the persisted value is a JSON string, so the decoded body is NOT a byte substring of its own line (every one of the root's 101,551 conversions opens `<system-reminder>` and runs past a newline the raw bytes carry as `\n`), while a needle the prefilter can derive carries no whitespace, no `"`, no control character and no DEL, and such a run does sit verbatim inside the raw line's JSON string - the reading every decoded `message.content` render already rests on, so no synthesized marker is registered. THE BACKSLASH IS ONE OF THOSE CHARACTERS, and for one release the predicate did not count it: a needle carrying one was emitted while the raw line stored it as `\\`, so the prefilter proved absence over a body csift had just rendered. Measured on a two-record synthetic transcript at the time: a `total_tokens_reminder` whose conversion reads `zzbsPATHC:\Users\devzz end` was on screen under `show --line 2`, while `search 'zzbsPATHC:\\Users\\devzz' --attachments` reported 0 matches and `search 'zzbsPATHC:.Users.devzz'` reported 1 on that same record - and a plain `user.message` carrying `zzbsUSERC:\Users\devzz end` in the same file answered 0 and 1 to the same pair, which is what put the hole in the PREDICATE and in every decoded render rather than in this body. Clause ㉚ below closes it. PROVENANCE rides every attachment unit: two shared `for` edges (`attached_to` from `parentUuid`, resolved to a LINE - 664,691 of 664,769 attachment lines of that root carry one and every one resolves in its own file; `for_tool_use` from the payload's INNER `toolUseID`, which is on 0 lines at the top level) and a `produced_by` fact carrying a hook run's own command, which keeps a key of its own because a command is not a record. An absent key renders its reason rather than vanishing. ONE view was stated here as unchanged: the person's mid-turn steer resolved through the opener renderer before this family was asked, so it rendered its payload JSON and read `[payload]` while an address on the same line showed the conversion. Clause ㉙ below removed that arm, so the steer now reaches this family's body rule like any other attachment line and the two views of one record agree. WHAT IT COSTS: the body DECODES a JSON string where the base emitted the payload bytes unparsed, one parse per record memoised on it. Two synthetic 200,000-line transcripts, two release builds of the same tree, seven interleaved rounds with a same-binary control inside each, `/usr/bin/time` user+sys medians: a file where every line carries a conversion reads 1.220 s -> 1.380 s = 1.131x under `-t harness.hook.outcome -c`; a file where none does reads 1.030 s -> 1.100 s = 1.068x under `-t harness.context.file -c`, inside its own 0.942x control; and the DEFAULT path, a role-bearing transcript with no attachment line at all, reads 0.959x against a 0.967x control, so an ordinary scan pays nothing.
> ㉑ **The five `harness.system.*` event kinds render their own fields, each leaf that NAMES another record carries a `for` EDGE, and an api_error's response HEADERS reach no output path but `--raw`** (§5, §8.2; claims CLS-060..CLS-068, with QT-031 and QT-SYS-001 refined). The five leaves shared one head, `[<subtype> <level>] <content>`, which named the subtype and threw the payload away - so a refusal rendered its banner sentence and dropped both model ids, the rewind target and the category, and an `api_error` rendered a head with no status, no retry counter and no word about what failed. Every field that makes one of these events useful lives in a top-level key `Record` does not model, so each kind now reads the SOURCE LINE and renders its own: `[refusal <subtype>] originalModel= fallbackModel=<v|none> apiRefusalCategory=` in the harness's own key words, so each greps back to the record (the `none` is the no-fallback shape, printed rather than elided because the absence IS the event, and dropped entirely when there was no source line to read, since a negative csift did not look for is a fabricated one); `[api-error status= type=<kind> retry <a>/<max> in <ms>ms]` plus the failure's own wording, which is the whole body because the record carries no `content` key on 29 of 29 and 258 of 258 specimens (an attempt the line omits renders `?` beside the maximum, because printing the maximum alone would read as an attempt number). NEITHER observed shape carries the kind and the wording at one level, so each is read where its own shape puts it: the connection shape (24 and 253) keeps the wording at `error.message` and names no kind at any depth, the HTTP shape (5 and 5, the header-bearing one) names the kind at `error.type` and keeps the response body it received, its wording two hops down the `error` chain - so the wording is the first non-empty `message` on that chain and the kind is read at ONE level, the `type` one hop down being the API envelope's own word; `[notice <level>] <content>`; the bare `[kill]` (no payload at all on 10 of 10 and 23 of 23 specimens - the human's line is a literal in the renderer); `[command] <content>`; and `[scheduled <when>] task= cron= prompt=` for the scheduler's own fire record, off the same parenthesised tail the fired prompt joins on. The line is parsed ONCE per entry - the render and `facts` - rather than once per field. EVERY exposed key is also IN the rendered text, because a key that reaches JSON alone is unsearchable: `retried`/`original_model`/`fallback_model`/`category`, `status`/`retry_attempt`/`max_retries`/`retry_in_ms`/`error`, and `task_id`/`cron`/`prompt` (cut at 200 characters on both sides) each carry one value rather than two. The instant stays the engine's `scheduled_at`, which has ONE writer and fills it for the fired PROMPT; this clause stated that on the event hit the key is null and only the head carries the instant, and clause ㉗ below made the field answer there too, off the event record's own parenthesised tail - the head keeps printing it, by the exposed-key rule this same clause states. The PROVENANCE edges go through the shared `for_edge`: `refused` (a refusal's `refusedUserMessageUuid`, basis key, resolving to a `type:"user"` record on 55 of 55 and 53 of 53 string values) and `wraps` (a delivered command's `parentUuid`, basis POSITION - a run of local commands chains stdout to stdout, so the link names the record ABOVE rather than the wrapper by construction, on 9 of 17 and 10 of 32). `armed_by` and the fallback turn's `banner` are honest unknowns carrying their reasons: a `taskId` is joined by MATCHING an id inside a tool result and a banner DESCENDS from its record, and a uuid map does neither. `retractedMessageUuids` is neither an edge nor a key - those are wire uuids, 0 of 75 and 0 of 79 entries resolving to a line - so offering both would invite a join that cannot succeed. A turn the API served from a FALLBACK model renders under `agent.message.plain` with `[served by <to.model>, fell back from <from.model>]` and JSON `served_by`/`fell_back_from`, read off the parsed `Block::Fallback { from, to }` rather than the source line, so a scanned unit and an addressed one carry the same fact. `Block::Fallback` and the inventory's block specimen gain the two-sided shape the API actually writes: the flat `model` key they modelled appears on 0 of 48 and 0 of 53 blocks. ONE FAMILY STAYS OUTSIDE THE MATCHABLE-TEXT LAW, and section 4's sensitive-headers invariant is why: an `api_error` line carries the whole HTTP response header set inside `error`, so this family renders the fields it names and never the line, and `--raw` is the one documented way to those bytes.
> ㉒ **The EXPOSED KEYS: eighteen facts a record states about the CALL behind it** (§8.2 carries the wire contract). Claude Code stamps a record with which agent definition, skill, plugin and MCP tool the request ran under, which API request and which content block of its response it is, why the call failed, what a person typed refusing a tool, which scheduled task submitted the prompt and which hook run closed it. csift parsed none of them, so a reader who wanted any of it fell back to `--raw`. They ride every hit and every `show` record row as JSON, through the one stamping site, so an ADDRESS carries exactly what a scan does; four of them also render a label-zone tag, and the split is the family's statement about what is worth a reader's line - `attribution`'s SPECIFIC half (a skill outranks the plugin that owns it, both outrank the MCP pair, and the agent name is data because it is on every record of a subagent lane), `[ends turn]`, `[feedback: <60 chars>]` and the api-failure headline; the four ids are joins and ride JSON alone. The unit's ONE `for` edge comes from the two pointer keys and goes through the shared builder, so both render the same three answers; measured, neither id is a record uuid (0 of 272 and 0 of 418 `interruptedMessageId` values, 0 of 6,849 and 0 of 6,308 hook-run ids resolve to a `uuid` of their own file), so an ordinary transcript renders the id with `not in this file`. **WIDTH was the design problem and BOTH alternatives were measured rather than argued.** Every parsed line of every scan carries a whole `Record`, so eighteen `Option`s in that struct are eighteen fields on every row - the C-41 law. Lifting the keys from the source line on demand instead - a depth-1 key walk at the one place a `Record` is built from bytes, behind a byte gate over the key names, which is where it has to run because the engine keeps a record's source line only for a line its `Record` cannot answer for - cost 48 to 79 percent of USER CPU on three §7 acid rows over a 396.7 MiB transcript (search 1.774 -> 2.635 s, files 2.233 -> 3.825 s, stats 2.406 -> 4.298 s), being a second whole-line pass on top of the one serde already makes. The shape that answers both halves is ONE `Exposed` behind ONE `Box`, `#[serde(flatten)]`-read by the SAME pass: the row grows by a POINTER rather than by the 216 bytes eighteen fields measured (`size_of::<Record>()` 1,368 -> 1,376, pinned exactly in `parse::tests::spine` and quoted in AGENTS 3.3), and the allocation behind the pointer is paid only by a record that names at least one key. Inside `Exposed` the width is still paid down where that is free (`Box<str>` rather than `String`, `u32` rather than `u64`, the 429 quota object deserialized straight into the two members csift reads of it), because a record carrying one key carries the whole struct. The shipped shape was then measured on the acid rows the change touches - the 24 default-path rows plus the five census rows, tiny and compacted, 21 reps, base against tip - and all 56 (row, size) pairs sit inside a same-binary control band of 0.2467 taken on the same host with the same rows and reps: the compacted class, where the millisecond-scale jitter of the tiny class does not dominate, reads a mean ratio of 1.0153 with a maximum of 1.0533 and a minimum of 0.9937, and 0 rows disagree on an exit code. Five rows render different bytes, every one of them a `--format json` row and every one LARGER, which is this family's own JSON riding each hit. The LANDMARK class, the one that set of rows did not cover, was measured separately on the 396.7 MiB landmark: four default rows within 0.965 to 1.004 of the base, and peak RSS on a landmark `search` DOWN 2.0 percent (1,305,640,960 -> 1,279,279,104 bytes), which is the shape behaving as its arithmetic says it should - the row is narrower and the box is allocated only where a key is named. THREE facts are as much about what is NOT read. `error` is a STRING on the synthetic failure record and an OBJECT on a `type:"system"` `api_error` one, and that object's `headers` member holds the failed request's whole HTTP response header set - so the field's deserializer answers `None` for any non-string and the object never enters the record model at all (the sensitive-headers law, enforced where the value would enter). And the api-failure accessor is gated TWICE, on the flag its one factory sets and on the failure SAYING something, with ONE list of parts deciding both the gate and the headline: a record naming only its quota used to build a failure and render `[api failure unknown]`, a word no document declared. Measured, the second gate changes no live record - 925 of 925 flag carriers on one projects root and 998 of 998 on another name at least one part. The third is TOLERANCE, and it is the same trade one level down: every one of the eighteen decodes leniently, so a key of an unexpected JSON type (`"apiBlockIndex":"3"`, `"toolEndsTurn":"yes"`) reads exactly as an absent key rather than failing the whole record and taking the conversation on that line out of every surface. It never COERCES - a string is not a number - because csift reports what a record says; and the retry in `parse::parse_line` keeps the one tolerance the flattened map would otherwise have cost, a line whose bytes are not valid UTF-8 (0 of 1,811,997 lines on one projects root, so the shape lives in the fixtures rather than in the corpus). **That retry MOVES a documented number, and the move is deliberate** (SS 3.6): the accidental tolerance serde gave only covered a bad byte sitting in a field `Record` does NOT model, so a bad byte in a MODELLED field used to fail the record and be booked malformed. It is now decoded lossily and counted as a record on every surface at once - measured on a four-line fixture carrying two such lines, `search` moves from 2 exchanges plus `(2 malformed line(s) skipped)` to 4 exchanges and no note, the JSON summary's `skipped_lines` from 2 to 0, `stats`' whole-file line-type census from `user x2` to `user x4`, and `show --line` from a `no such record(s)` bail to the rendered text with U+FFFD in place of the byte. `--raw` returns the bytes either way. `list` gains the same family's one session-level answer, `minted_elsewhere`: the SIBLING sessions a record here names as the one that MINTED it, a SET with its count because one transcript can hold runs hydrated from more than one session, answered by the `--lineage` whole-file pass only for the measured reason that of the two disagreeing files on one root, one file's run starts below the head window. Claims REC-140..REC-151 land with it. SUPERSEDED IN THIS BLOCK, twice and in one direction only: clause (52) adds a NINETEENTH key, `turnCompanion`, which is read by a predicate rather than projected as a fact, so the eighteen counted here are the eighteen still PROJECTED; and clause (51) moves the width to `size_of::<Record>()` 1,384, the API's stop-details object having landed behind its own pointer inside `message`. The 216 bytes remain the measurement of eighteen inline fields, which is what the rule rests on - one field per key on every parsed line of every scan.
> ㉓ **A `for` edge cuts a UUID to its handle and prints every other id whole.** The label zone rendered an unresolved target as its first EIGHT characters whatever the target was. That is the right handle for a uuid - the leading hex digits identify one, the remaining 28 are the same shape, and the compaction excerpt has printed exactly that handle for versions - and it is the wrong one for a WIRE id, whose head is the part every id of its kind shares. Two reach this render through real edges: the injections family's `attached_to` on a notification frame's `<tool-use-id>`, and the attachment family's `for_tool_use` on a payload's inner `toolUseID`. `hook-bb000000` cut to eight is `hook-bb0`, the word `hook` and three of eight digits; `toolu_01zzreef` is `toolu_01`, the tool prefix and the version digits every tool-call id carries. Both keep what does not distinguish and drop what does, which is worse than printing the id long. The cut is now gated on `path::is_uuid`, so a uuid keeps the handle it has always had and everything else prints whole. JSON never moved: `target.uuid` has always carried the id entire, which is why this was a TEXT defect only.
> ㉔ **A `for` edge can name a whole TRANSCRIPT, and the handover relation becomes one.** `record_text::for_edge` resolved a uuid to a line or answered the honest empty, and had no parameter for the SESSION a target sits in. Two relations needed one. `fork-context-ref` names the record its prefix was cut at AND the session that record lives in, which the bookkeeping family stamped onto the built edge through a helper of its own - the hand-built target the single-builder law exists to prevent. `continued-in` names a child transcript and NO record in it, which the edge could not express at all, so it shipped as a family fact rendering `[continued in @<session>]` beside the `for` array rather than inside it. The builder now takes a `session` and answers a fourth way: with a session and no uuid the edge is RESOLVED and renders `[for: <kind> @<session>]`, an `@` target a reader feeds straight back to csift, where before such a target read `[for: <kind> @<session> unknown]` - the word `unknown` belongs to `ForUnknown` and must not say that a line named nothing when it named a file. So the family's local `in_session` helper is gone, `continued-in` is the edge `for: continued_in` (BREAKING: its JSON moves from the `continued_in` key into the shared `for` array, where every other relation already lives), and a `fork-context-ref` carrying a session but no `parentLastUuid` renders the parent session rather than an honest empty, because the session IS what that line named. ONE of the five bookkeeping relations stays a family fact and says why: `cost-state` points at an INSTANT, and no target shape can carry a timestamp without putting it in a field documented as the target record's uuid.
> ㉕ **A leafless unit renders its family's facts too.** `render_label` returned early for a hit with no leaf - the 1:1 law's unmodeled unit - and the extension zone, where a family's markers and label-zone facts are rendered, was computed after that return. So an ADDRESSED record csift models no leaf for carried its facts into JSON and lost every one of them on the text surface: `show --line N` on an attachment of an unmodeled payload type printed `? unmodeled: attachment/<type>` and nothing else, while `--format json` on the same line carried both `for` edges and the `produced_by` fact. A record with no leaf is still a record the one stamping site ran for, and the attachment family's three provenance facts are on every attachment line whether or not csift has a leaf for its payload type. The zone is now computed once, before the split, and both arms render it: the SHAPE stands in for the leaf path and everything the families attached follows it in the same push order a labelled unit uses.
> ㉖ **A `show` record row carries the queue facts its `search` hit carries, and the pair has ONE writer.** `queue_operation` and `queue_reason` - the event a `queue-operation` line records and the reason a removal gives - reached a `search` hit and no `show` record row, so running a hit's own `refetch` command answered null for a record the query it came from had just reported in full. That is the one thing a refetch must not do. Both keys are on the row now, null on every other line type exactly as they are on a hit, and `show --help`'s key table names them under the shared block. The second half is the engine's own: the scan read the record and the ADDRESS site hard-coded both to null, two copies of one rule sitting a file apart, so the pair now comes from one `queue_facts` helper both construction sites call. That half is not observable today - every `queue-operation` line renders through the bookkeeping family's head and so never reaches the addressed unit - and it is a deduplication rather than a fix: the two sites cannot disagree again, which is what the address site exists to guarantee.
> ㉗ **`scheduled_at` answers on BOTH halves of the schedule pair.** The engine writes the field for the fired PROMPT, joining the instant off its `system`/`scheduled_task_fire` sibling through the per-file index. On the EVENT record - the line the instant is actually written on, in the parenthesised tail its content ends with - the same field read NULL, which said the scheduler's own fire record does not know when it fired. It now reads the record's own tail there. One field, one meaning, one writer: the instant the schedule fired, whichever half the hit is. The head keeps printing it as well, because every key the system family exposes is also IN the rendered text - a key that reached JSON alone would be unsearchable.
> ㉘ **The provenance edge is ONE key, named once on each surface.** Four families build `for` edges and every one of them folds into the same array through `attach_facts`, so the key belongs to no family - but each of the four listed it in `show --help`'s key table under its own block, spelled four different ways (`for:[...] (kind/basis/reason + target{...})`, `for:[{kind|basis|target|reason}] (what an injection was written for)`, and two more). A consumer reading that enumeration to learn which keys exist read FOUR keys. It is one row under the shared block now, spelled with every field the target carries, and a family that starts carrying edges adds none of its own; a unit test holds it there. The other half is the same defect from the other side: `search --help`'s JSON schema enumerated every hit key EXCEPT `for`, so the array shipped on the wire and appeared in no list a reader could find. It is in the schema now, beside the sentence that says what an edge's four members mean. The bookkeeping family's `continued_in` row goes with them, that relation having become an edge.
> ㉙ **The person's mid-turn STEER renders the text the model received.** `record_text_emission`'s `user.message` arm resolved its body as the reconstructed opener text, then the VERBATIM payload JSON, and only then the render seam - so a `queued_command` whose payload `origin.kind` is `human` and whose record also carries a persisted conversion showed the payload on the one view a reader reaches under `-t user`, while an address on the same line showed the conversion. Two readers, two bodies, one record. The payload arm was put there to give the steer a body at all (without it the record classified `user.message` and emitted nothing), but the seam gives it a BETTER one - the attachment family's body rule prefers what the harness persisted - and `record_raw_text` still falls through to the verbatim payload for a record no family answers for, so nothing that had a body loses one. The arm is gone, and with it the body fact's `UserMessage` exception, which existed only to name the payload the unit was showing. Measured over one projects root, one parse per non-blank line: 20 of the 164 `queued_command` lines whose payload `origin.kind` is `human` carry a `rendered`, and those 20 are the records the two views disagreed about.
> ㉚ **The needle safety predicate counts the BACKSLASH** (claim REC-110 refined). A raw jsonl line stores a backslash doubled, so a needle carrying one occurs nowhere in the bytes the 7d line prefilter and the 7f whole-file gate scan - the same reason a quote is on that list. `matcher::json_escapes_in_string` counted the quote, the control characters and DEL and not the backslash, and the omission had a reason that stopped being true: `required_literal` never emits one, because a backslash is a regex METACHARACTER and a whole-pattern literal carrying one is rejected before the predicate is reached. `hir_needles` walks a PARSED pattern, where the metacharacter is gone and the literal run reads `C:\Users\dev`, and that run was emitted whole. So `search 'C:\\Users\\dev'` reported a definitive absence over text `show --line` rendered - on a converted attachment body and, identically, on a plain `user.message` whose own text carries a backslash run, which is what makes it the predicate's hole rather than any one family's. Counting it SPLITS such a run exactly as a quote already splits it: the needle becomes `Users`, which does occur verbatim in the raw bytes, so the gate stays sound and stays cheap. Every surface that stated the exception - the attachment family's module doc, AGENTS 3.3a and its section 4 prefilter paragraph, SKILL, `search --help` and clause ⑳ above - is rewritten to the rule, and the e2e that pinned the drop now pins the match.
> ㉛ **The unmodeled excerpt elides an opaque `data` blob, so the keys that identify the shape can print.** The key=value excerpt is the one rendered path that walks a line's own values without knowing what they are, and it already elides a `headers` member by the section 4 secrecy law. A `data` member joins it for the opposite reason: two on-disk shapes carry one and both are opaque blobs - a `{type:"base64"}` image or document source, and a `redacted_thinking` block's ciphertext - whose HEAD identifies nothing, while the per-value cut is 80 characters, so the blob swallows the whole budget and the SIBLING keys that do identify the shape never print. Measured on a 1,222-character payload addressed with `show --line`: the `message` member rendered 80 characters of base64 and neither `media_type` nor the block's own `type` reached the screen; with the blob elided both do and the member overflows by 34 characters instead of 1,248. The rule is the NAME, at every depth, like `headers` - not a length threshold, which would be a number nobody measured and a small blob could evade.
> ㉜ **A bare face keeps the answer the MODEL wrote, and the two manuals name every key they emit.** `agent.message.structured` is carried by a `structured_output` attachment, a payload in `inventory::NEVER_CONVERTS`, so `Record::delivery_override` answers not-delivered for it and the bare-face delivery axis dropped the leaf from `-t agent` and `-t agent.message`: measured on a three-line transcript (a user record, an assistant text turn, a `structured_output` attachment parented to it), both faces reported ONE census key, `agent.message.plain`, and the second leaf of that category was reachable only by a glob nobody is told to type. The exemption is `cli::selectors::keeps_face_against_delivery`, at the one narrowing site inside `selector_matches`, keyed on the leaf PATH so it can never widen to the category - after it both faces report two keys. It is the SINGLE stated exception to the visible-face law and the reason is the axis's own question: delivery asks what the model received, and this is the one leaf the model produced; the API serves a turn free text or a schema-bound answer and never both, so the coarse ask answers with whichever that turn used. Nothing about the record moves - its `delivered` field still reads false and an explicit selector still renders `[not delivered]` - and the other two narrowing axes are untouched, so an invisible leaf stays invisible (`agent.thinking.redacted` is still outside a bare `agent.thinking`) and a survival verdict still decides on its own. The reachability pin drops from 29 entries to 28, which is now exactly the `llm_visible() == false` set, and the constant names the leaf that left and why, so the two reasons a leaf can be absent cannot trade places unnoticed. Four parity surfaces carry the exception: SKILL's three-step visibility paragraph, `search --help`'s axis 2, SPEC §5.8 and AGENTS §3.3a. No Claude Code fact moved - the converter still drops that payload - so no claim was minted; CLS-044's `depends` gained the boundary of what csift does with the verdict, with the before-and-after census as its check. THE SAME COMMIT ROUND tightened the help-parity sweep that guards the other half of this: it asked whether a key's characters occur anywhere on the page, which for `to`, `from`, `line`, `uuid`, `mode`, `kind`, `text`, `body` and `for` is a question about English - deleting the `to` row from the `show` key table left the whole suite green. A key now counts as named only as a top-level entry of a rendered key block (extracted brace-balanced, split at depth 0, so a nested `for:[{...}]` cannot truncate the list) or as the leading identifier of a backticked span. That deletion now fails by name, and the first honest run found TEN keys the loose rule had been passing: the five draft-distance keys on `show --help`, and `produced_by`, `signature_len`, `thinking_text_absent`, `tool_use_id` and `source` on `search --help`. Eight were named but written bare and are now in backticks on both manuals; `tool_use_id` and `source` ride a hit and were absent from the hit schema, which now lists them.
> ㉝ **A PDF page says which page it is.** A `Read` with a `pages` parameter returns one image element per requested page and the page number is on no key of the element - the harness's mapper labels only a FAILED page - so the number is `toolUseResult.firstPage` plus the element's position among the content elements after the leading text, counted over POSITIONS so a failed page does not shift the rest (claim IMG-016, end-to-end: five producer hops read in the shipped build, and 14 image-bearing results over one projects root carrying `firstPage` 14 of 14 with the leading text's N, the image count and `file.count` equal on all 14). `image` gains ` p<N>` beside the media type and the document's basename once per record group (one transcript, one line, one document) in text, `page` and `pdf_path` in JSON; the derivation is gated on the record's own result reading `parts` with a `firstPage` AND on the record carrying exactly one `tool_result` block, so a screenshot, a pre-`firstPage` build and the REPL tool's re-emitted pages (which carry no `firstPage` and flatten several documents into one list, claim IMG-018) all keep a null page rather than an assumed 1. Nothing about addressing moves - the page is provenance, not an id. THE SAME FACT reaches the `agent.tool.result` unit as the keys family's `pdf_pages` - the label zone `[pdf pages A-B of <basename>]`, JSON `{first, last, count, path}` - read through the one function `image` reads, so `show --line` and `image` cannot disagree about which pages a record carries; the existing `[N images: L<n>i1, ...]` annotation and its extraction hint are untouched. The whole-PDF `{type:"document"}` block a Read with no `pages` returns arrived in the SAME build window and stays unlisted (claim IMG-017), because `image` lists images and `ext()` has no `application/pdf` row.
> ㉞ **One session id under TWO project directories is MERGED, and the merge is decided by the bytes** (§2.5; claim PATH-014). The shape is real on disk - over one projects root, 30 encoded directories hold 81 top-level transcripts over 78 distinct basenames, so 3 basenames occur under two directories, two of the pairs byte-identical and one a strict byte PREFIX (the shorter copy stopping 322 records before the longer one's end, which only copying a file that is still being appended to produces). Claude Code reaches the same shape two ways of its own - the `CLAUDE_CODE_PROJECT_DIR_NAME` override, whose enumeration deliberately adds the NATURAL directory beside the overridden one, and `relocateSessionTranscript`'s fallback mover, which its own warning says can leave the source behind - and its resume lookup walks every project directory and returns null rather than choose when a second one has messages. csift resolved BOTH copies before this: `list` printed two indistinguishable rows, `stats` printed the session twice with a doubled scope total, `agents` printed every node twice, `search -c` doubled while `--count-by session` hid the doubling under one key, and the whole sidecar was walked twice, because a copied project directory carries it along. The merge now happens at `path::resolve_session_files` - the one point every session-operating subcommand passes through, and BEFORE subagent gathering for that reason - keyed on the basename (PATH-012) and decided by comparing sizes then the first min(size) bytes: `identical` and `prefix` collapse onto the LONGEST copy, `divergent` merges nothing and keeps every copy in scope, because two files claiming one session id with contradictory content are not one session seen twice and a turn number minted from one does not address the other, and `unknown` merges nothing either - a copy csift could not READ has no bytes to compare, and reading that failure as an empty file would make it the winner and announce the readable copy as dropped. An `@<agent-id>` target finds every copy the same way - it stops at the owning session and then probes each project directory for a copy of that ONE basename, rather than returning the first owner enumerated (which picked a copy by `read_dir` order and disclosed nothing) or walking every sidecar twice - and hands them to that one union merge rather than deciding them itself. An mtime never enters the rule (a preserving copy re-applies them); an equal-size tie goes to the copy whose directory basename encodes the first record's `cwd`, then to the smallest path. `show` keeps refusing: it counts the UNMERGED set and names every copy's path, because no `@<uuid>` separates them. BREAKING: `sessions_in_scope` and every spanning count change on a scope holding a duplicated session, and `list` emits one row where it emitted two.
> ㉟ **The merge discloses what it dropped, on every surface that counts** (§2.5). The no-silent-truncation law makes the resolver's report part of its return value (`Resolved { files, duplicates }`) rather than a fact only the resolver knows. Three disclosures, each in the place a different reader looks. The RESOLVER names every group on stderr at the moment it decides - unconditionally, the same posture `@main` takes with its lane note - so a subcommand with no summary of its own still discloses. The JSON envelope header of `list`/`search`/`stats`/`files`/`recover`/`verbatim`/`image`/`agents` carries FOUR keys, all 0 on an ordinary scope rather than absent: `duplicate_transcripts` (transcripts the merge removed from the scope), `duplicate_records_dropped` (their physical jsonl lines, the same domain `show --line` addresses), `divergent_transcripts` (copies KEPT beyond one per session id because the bytes disagree) and `unreadable_transcripts` (copies csift could not read at all, which is why their group merged nothing). The last two exist because the first two read 0 both when nothing was duplicated and when a duplicate merged nothing, and a reader parsing only the stream never sees the stderr note that separates them. The UNKNOWN key has a reader, and WHICH surfaces have one is a measured fact rather than an obvious one: an unreadable copy is in scope like any other, so a surface that reads EVERY file in its scope dies on it - fail-loud, after the resolver's stderr note and before its own header - but `agents` opens a session file only through its SIDECAR, so a copy with no `<uuid>/subagents` directory beside it is never read, that run prints its header and exits 0, and the key is then the only place the fact reaches a reader of the stream. The TEXT half is one `copies` line per duplicated id, printed beside the scope banner rather than as a trailing footer: the merge is a fact about the resolved SCOPE like the span line above it, that is the one place every spanning surface already discloses its range, and a banner is reached even when the result is empty. The `-c` and `-l` terminal modes print neither - their stdout is a bare integer and a pure id stream, and the stderr note is what a pipeline reader gets. `list` additionally carries the report on the ROW: `also_at` (the other copies' paths) and `duplicate_relation` (`identical` | `prefix` | `divergent` | `unknown`), with `sessions_in_scope` counting DISTINCT sessions - and a divergent group, which merges nothing, keeps a row per copy with each naming the other.
> ㊱ **Subagent discovery stays anchored at the transcript's own directory, and that is now measured** (§2.5; claim PATH-015). A session CAN own a `<uuid>/` directory under a project directory that holds no transcript: the harness builds the workflow-SCRIPT path from the project directory of the SHELL cwd in force at the time while the transcript path comes from the originalCwd slot, so a deep in-session `cd` splits them. The directory name invites the conclusion that discovery is missing work there, and the census says it is not - over two projects roots, 55 `<uuid>/` directories sit beside no transcript, 0 of them carry a `subagents/` subtree, 0 hold a single `agent-*.jsonl`, and the 18 under a `workflows/` shape hold 81 files between them, every one a `.js` under `workflows/scripts/`. What lives elsewhere is scripts and externalised tool output, and a persisted-output pointer is resolved by the absolute path the record carries rather than by a walk. So no code changed and `sidecar_dir_for_session` carries the measurement at the place a reader would otherwise widen it; what would change the reading is a single `agent-*.jsonl` under such a directory, which the claim's census instrument is what detects.
> ㊲ **The `for` relation vocabulary is CLOSED** (`model::provenance::EdgeKind`, section 4.12). Every family wrote its relation word as a bare string at the call site, so the set of words was whatever the six family files happened to contain: nothing could enumerate it, two families could spell one relation differently without either noticing, and a census over it would have been a census over an open set. It is now an enum of nineteen with one `as_str`, which the label zone and the JSON both read, and `record_text::for_edge` takes it instead of a `&'static str`. No relation was RENAMED at the close - every word is the one its family already rendered, `hook run` included, whose two words were the distinction between a hook-run id and the API tool_use id its field name suggests (that one word is respelled `hook_run` later in this same version, clause ㊵, before either spelling ships) - and the cross-family pin that says so landed FIRST: 39 (leaf, kind, basis, target shape) rows over the reachability fixture, asserted as an equality in both directions, plus the rule that a null basis belongs to the honest empty and to nothing else. What that pin proves is the ROW SET, and it is worth saying what it does not: rendered BYTES moved elsewhere in this same version. An edge naming a CALL (`for_tool_use`, `reports`) now resolves through the tool index and renders `L<n>` where it printed the wire id with `not in this file` - measured on one compacted corpus transcript, 192 of 199 such renders changed - and the two user-facing duals gained `result_of` while losing `parent` to the one-relation-one-edge rule. The pin cannot see the first of those, because every `for_tool_use` row of the reachability fixture is the honest empty; a claim about bytes needs the corpus comparison, and that is where it is made.
> ㊳ **PROVENANCE is answered for EVERY unit, not only for the records that carry a key** (section 4.12). An assistant reply, a thinking block, a tool call and its result carried no `for` at all, which is most of a transcript. Two additions close it. `for_root` names the record that OPENED the unit's turn plus the parent-hop distance up to it, on every hit, rendering `[root L<n> +<hops>]` and nothing on the opener itself; its JSON is always an object because its four answers - IS the opener, walked there in N hops, placed by the grouping and not reached by the links, and no opener above this unit at all - are four facts a missing key could not separate. Two ENGINE relations join the vocabulary: `parent` (`parentUuid`, basis position), which STANDS DOWN when ANY OTHER edge on the same unit already names that same TARGET - the test is on the target and never on who named it, so a family's word and the engine's own both do it - and `result_of` (a tool result's `tool_use_id`, basis key), the one relation in the set that is not a uuid relation - hence `model::provenance::ProvIndex` beside the chain's uuid map. Both ride JSON alone: the label zone carries what a record SAYS about itself, and these two are on nearly every unit and repeat what a rendered transcript already shows. COST: the root walk is per HIT and its ladder is built on a turn's FIRST hit and not before, so a turn that matches nothing pays nothing; the per-FILE side got CHEAPER, because the three questions a hit asks of a file's tool blocks (the name, whether a result came back, which line carries the call) were two walks over those blocks and are now one (`build_tool_index`). Three pre-existing tests were rewritten in place for the new label zone, each with its old and new assertion in the commit body.
> ㊴ **The two ways to WALK the provenance: `--count-by for-kind` and `--for <ADDR>`** (sections 4.12 and 6.2). The census is what closing the vocabulary bought - a key space a reader can be handed - and it multi-counts like `label` because one unit carries several relations at once, distinct per record, counting a relation whether or not csift resolved its target. `--for` runs the arrows backward: name a record and get everything that is for it, TRANSITIVELY. The address is `L<line>` or a bare number (per FILE) or a record uuid (which finds its record in whichever scanned transcript carries it, the closure itself running per FILE); three structural relations admit a row - the parent link, a `tool_result` answering a member's call, and a record naming a member's call by id in its own text or payload - and a row that joins contributes its own uuid and its own call ids, so one forward pass computes the closure (a parent precedes its child in file order, REC-075). THE PARENT ARM STOPS AT A TURN BOUNDARY, without which it is not a closure at all: every record carries a parent link, the next turn's opener included, and an unconditional walk returned lines 2..7 of the seven-line fixture where the answer is 2..5. A new message is not FOR the record above it - that is what a turn boundary means - so an opener joins only through a relation it states, which is how a `<task-notification>` reporting on a call inside the set still comes back while the person's next question does not. It is on `search` and not on `show` because it returns a SET from possibly several transcripts with a summary (`for_of`), which is a scan's output shape, and it gates per record beside `-t`/time/pattern rather than as an address, so the excerpt cap stays at its default and no unmodeled unit is emitted.
> ㊵ **The one two-word relation word is respelled `hook_run`** (section 4.12). `--count-by for-kind` prints `<count>  <key>` per line, which is the shape every other census axis prints and which every reader of that surface splits on whitespace - so a key carrying a space is one no such reader can read back, and `hook run` was the only key in the closed vocabulary that carried one. The word still says what it said: the id a `stop_hook_summary` names is a HOOK RUN id and not the API tool_use id its field name suggests. It is a BREAK against the vocabulary clause ㊲ landed earlier in this same version and against nothing that ever shipped - neither spelling is in a released binary - and it moves three surfaces together: the census key, the `[for: hook_run ...]` label-zone tag and the JSON `for[].kind`.
> ㊶ **NINE relations the engine already knew and could not state** (section 4.12). The closed vocabulary went from nineteen words to twenty-seven, and every one of the nine is a value csift computed anyway: the SURVIVAL axis's four (`supersedes` the message that replaced a recalled draft, `continues` the one the conversation continued from after a rewind, `branches` the head of an abandoned branch, `replays` the surviving line a replay copy's uuid also names), the compaction event's three (`pairs` its two halves both ways, `precedes` the `logicalParentUuid` the boundary carries in place of a null parent, `preserves` the head and tail of the segment it kept, as two edges), `repairs` on a paired resume placeholder, and `reports` widened to BOTH carriers of a `<task-notification>` pulse. TWO of them change an ANSWER rather than adding a shape. `pairs` on a compaction summary and `repairs` on a paired resume placeholder both name the record above, which is what `parentUuid` names there, so the one-relation-one-edge rule stands `parent` down on those units and the existing edge changes KIND: on the landmark transcript, `parent` 169,180 to 169,097, against 80 summaries and 3 placeholders. And `reports`: the relation lived in the isMeta injection family, whose gate is the authorship flag, so it answered for 95 of the 4,017 pulse-carrying records on one projects root and said nothing about the 3,922 ordinary carriers; read at the engine off the record's own text it answers for both, and resolves through the tool index (2,470 pulses carry a `<tool-use-id>`, 2,459 of those name a call of the same file, so 61% of pulses get an address). COUNTING RULE, because the numbers do not reproduce without it, and it has TWO halves: the FILE SET is every `*.jsonl` under one projects root - the top-level transcripts AND the `<uuid>/subagents/**` sidecar ones, excluding `journal.jsonl`, `elicitations.jsonl` and anything under a `csift-channel/` directory - and the READING is the text the classifier itself reads, the bare string or the newline-join of the content array's `text` blocks, one row per RECORD carrying `<task-notification>` in it. Each half moves the answer: the top-level transcripts alone miss the sidecars, and a string-only reading gives 3,805 rows instead of 4,017 and moves the FRAME count from 95 to 56 - 41% of the one population the family used to own. The engine's reader takes that same reading, so its DOMAIN is the whole population rather than a subset of it. The four survival relations render NO label-zone tag and take the same gate the engine's two one-hop edges take, because each restates a marker its own unit already prints - so a default text scan renders every byte unchanged and builds none of them; the other five DO render, because no family owns their leaf and nothing else on the unit gives their target as an address. TWO measured guards ship with them: a compaction boundary whose `anchorUuid` is its OWN uuid falls back to the position pairing (the `/rewind` summarize path passes exactly that, claim CMP-030), and `branches` is never emitted on the branch head, where it would name the record the reader is on.
> ㊷ **The RELATION INVENTORY, and the two rules that keep a relation out of the vocabulary** (section 4.12, mirrored in SKILL). A reader could be handed the twenty-seven words and still not know what csift can answer, because the words are not the whole map: some relations ride a FIELD on another surface and some ride nothing at all. The inventory is one table over all of them - the relation, what it joins, its basis, and where the answer lives - and its `for[]` block is pinned to `EdgeKind` in both directions by a drift test, so a variant the table does not name fails and a `for[]` row naming no variant fails with it. The two rules are stated rather than left to be inferred. A CROSS-FILE join is a field on the surface that owns it (`clone_of`, `cleared_from`, `continued_in` and `minted_elsewhere` on `list`; `spawn_tool_use_id` and `parent_agent_id` on `agents`; the channel verdict on `msg`), because `ForTarget` names a session and does not carry the re-feedable id trio beside it, and widening it would fork the one target shape every family builds through. A target that is not a RECORD - a command, an instant, a lane, an external file, an event - is a per-family FACT with its own key (`produced_by`, `checkpoint_closes_at`, `peer_sender_task_id`), because an edge resolves a record address and those have none. The `unknown` rows are stated absences with the reason at each: `summarizes` has no key naming the region a compaction summary replaced (the boundary names what it KEPT, and `verbatim` reconstructs the rest), `steers` has none at all, `closes` resolves one hop and infers the rest from position, and the task-id half of `reports` is a declared follow-on with its share AND its yield measured on the counting rule clause 36 states in full - 1,236 of 4,017 pulses name a `<task-id>` and no `<tool-use-id>`, of which 1,228 would join (`toolUseResult.taskId` 1,206, `agentId` 21, `backgroundTaskId` 1), taking the relation from 61% of pulses to about 92%; it means reading `toolUseResult` for every tool result in the file, which section 7 keeps off a scan.
> ㊸ **The survival walk CROSSES a compaction boundary, under a budget** (claims MISC-036, MISC-043, MISC-046, MISC-006, REC-102; section 4.11 carries the rule, section 7j the default's measurement). Claude Code's loader reads `parentUuid` only, so it stops at a `compact_boundary` and the whole history above the cut leaves the conversation. csift stopped there too and flagged that region `pre-cut`, which was honest but left the commonest forensic question unanswerable: a turn the operator rewound past ABOVE a cut read `pre-cut` like everything else up there, because csift declines to call a record abandoned in a region it could not resolve. The boundary names the record the compaction re-anchored onto (278 of 278 over one live projects root carry a `logicalParentUuid`), and the walk now follows it - up to N boundaries, set by the new GLOBAL flag `--cross-compactions <N|all>`, threaded to every `Chain::build` / `ChainView::build` site on the seven chain-walking commands (`search`, `show`, `stats`, `files`, `recover`, `verbatim`, `image`). Above a CROSSED boundary the ordinary rules apply; above the FLOOR everything off-chain stays `pre-cut`, and the floor now has a stated CAUSE - `budget-exhausted` when the budget stopped the walk, and one of three when a boundary's door is shut: `unresolvable-boundary` (the logical parent is on no line of the file; 52 of the 275 boundaries are that shape), `relink-removed` (it is on a line, the pre-boundary cut took it out of the map) or `boundary-loop` (it is on a line the walk has already reached). Over one projects root, 18 transcripts end on a boundary and those three split 9 / 0 / 9. Whether a bigger budget would MOVE that floor is a second question, and the footer answers it by PROBING the boundary the walk stopped at rather than by reading the cause: the budget can also run out AT a boundary whose own logical parent is gone, where every larger budget lands on the same record, so a flag value is named only where the probe says the floor moves. SURFACE CHANGE, and it is a real one: on a compacted transcript a record's `survival` can move `pre-cut` to `live` or to `abandoned`, and a turn that becomes abandoned leaves the numbering. `--cross-compactions 0` restores the previous reading - measured byte for byte against a build that predates the flag on 32 of 32 streams, with the one known exception section 4.11 names, a boundary whose own `parentUuid` is non-null and names no record, of which the measured corpus holds none - and under it csift says nothing about crossing at all, neither a footer line nor a JSON key. The CORPUS SET behind those numbers is a rule rather than a list: every uuid-named top-level transcript of one projects root that carries at least one boundary and sits between 9 and 21 MiB inclusive (1 MiB = 1,048,576 bytes), which is seven, plus the section 7 performance landmark, each read under `search --format json`, `search --count-by label`, `stats --format json` and `show --branch-points --format json`. Reading that band in decimal MB drops two of the seven, at 20.05 and 20.71 MiB, and both are transcripts the crossing MOVES. At the default, over those eight: the walk steps over a boundary on THREE of them (2 on the landmark, 1 on each of two band transcripts) and none on the other five, which stop at a shut door or at a head. Records move BOTH ways and the release note has to carry both. Toward `live`, counting EMITTED HITS of the search stream: the landmark 83 live / 30,612 pre-cut / 305 abandoned at budget 0 against 573 / 30,122 / 305 at the default, and the two band transcripts 1,415 / 1,151 / 5 against 2,565 / 0 / 6 and 499 / 989 / 25 against 1,451 / 0 / 62. Toward `abandoned`, counting WHOLE-FILE records off the search summary's `abandoned_records`: 349 against 607 on the landmark, 688 against 1,919 and 483 against 1,618 on the two. `show --branch-points` child SURVIVALS move from 1 live / 443 pre-cut to 8 live / 436 pre-cut on the landmark and from 1 live / 3 pre-cut to 4 live / 0 pre-cut on each of the two. Every `stats` whole-file chain TOTAL (`turns`, `abandoned_turns`, `rewound_turns`, `replay_copies`) is unchanged at every budget on all eight. Budget `all` reaches the same floor as the default on all eight, and their streams are identical in every key except the one that echoes the budget: 24 of the 32 pairs differ, by `3` against `all` and by nothing else (the 8 that match exactly are the `--count-by label` censuses, which do not echo it).
> ㊹ **`SKILL.md` is rewritten as the always-CURRENT mastery manual, and the rule that keeps it one is in AGENTS section 1.** The file had become a ledger: 37 lines carried a csift version rider (`since v0.11.0`, `(v0.12.0)`, `RETIRED at 0.13.0`, "where 0.11.0 read 659"), and a reader had to reconstruct which sentences described the binary in front of them. It carries none now - the Surface line against `csift --version` is the only staleness detector, which is what makes carrying no history safe. The topic order is orientation, then the five cross-cutting laws every command obeys (targeting, the five laws, labels, provenance, survival), then the commands, then the machine surface, then the failure modes; survival and provenance moving AHEAD of the commands is what lets each command section state its own behaviour on both axes in one clause instead of restating the chain rule five times. Facts are stated ONCE and cross-referenced by section: the six largest duplicate clusters were the two teammate ids (6 copies), the `deliver --recipe` law and the `error.headers` elision (5 each), and the writers law, the survival core rule, the 1:1 law and the three default-scan attachment leaves (4 each). Every retirement now lands in ONE section, so a caller who errored on a retired `-t` path, on `--additional-context` or on `csift turns` has one address. The wrong-assumptions table splits: an index of beliefs whose correction lives elsewhere, and full rows only for beliefs with no other home. Two statements were STALE and are corrected against the binary rather than carried over - the text excerpt is one line per record (whitespace runs collapse, measured on a table-bearing message), and sixteen of the eighteen bookkeeping line types build a key=value head from their own bytes rather than only `file-history`. Size, one tokenizer throughout (tiktoken `cl100k_base`): **59,591 as it ships**, 2,863 over the 56,728 it replaced. Three numbers stand behind that one, because the file was rewritten and then CORRECTED twice - 56,687 as the rewrite landed, so its dedup and rider removal paid for every section it added; 57,608 after the first correction round; 58,538 after the second. The 921 the first round added is not padding - section 10 grew from five rows to the thirteen retired selectors the binary actually prints plus a second table for the two retired FLAGS, 9.2 gained the crossing default's value, 7.4's closed enumeration of label-zone tags was falsified by a sweep of every `[`-led literal in `src/` and replaced by the fifteen tags the binary prints that the file named nowhere, 20 gained the three of its four stderr lines it had never quoted, and the help's inverted newline pitfall took its correction in SKILL as well. Against that, three compressions returned 1,028 tokens: the shape table's byte-identical third column on all 73 rows, the two per-row sentences the `harness.notification.*` and `harness.context.*` families each repeated, and six facts the file still stated in two sections. The 930 the second round added has the same shape, and it is one finding repeated: every sweep behind the first round read one arm per construct and missed the FALLBACK arm beside it. Re-read, those arms are three more label-zone tags on `search`/`show` (+168); the relation inventory's `folds` row was quoting a fold marker the binary never prints (+75); thirteen bracketed forms the OTHER commands print gained a telegraphic clause each (+480); and `whoami`'s third closing summary reached both manuals (+17). Against those, five restatements dropped and three dense paragraphs made scannable returned 132. A closing retrieval battery on the corrected file found that same one-arm reading a level UP, in the file rather than in a sweep: 9.1 said all three survival verdicts render a label-zone marker, which `search` and `show` do not do for `pre-cut` - 0 occurrences over 400 hits covering 2,123 `pre-cut` records, against 1,304 `files` rows and 199 `image` rows on the same transcript - and 13.1's `(s)` plural shorthand read as bytes against 13.3's `[1 image: #265]` (+203). A second reader of the corrected sections then found the same shape a THIRD time, in the sweep test rather than in the file: its allowlist matched a dynamic tag by PREFIX, so the one entry reading "`Marker::render` and the `for` edge builder" was also absorbing `render.rs`'s image suffix - a third construct, in another file, that is not a label-zone tag at all and that 7.4's closed-set promise therefore did not cover. The entries are now the whole literal matched for EQUALITY, nine of them with a reason each, and 7.4 names the image suffix as the one `search` bracket outside the zone (+63). One reading the round had left open was then MEASURED rather than argued: on a fixture whose boundary names a `logicalParentUuid` no line carries, so the walk floors at any budget, a `files` row for a write above the cut reads `survival` `pre-cut`, keeps a NUMBERED `turn_index` and prints the ordinary `turn N` slot, while the abandoned row beside it reads null and `turn abandoned (root L<n>)` - so 12.1's parenthetical, which had attached the null to a sentence covering both verdicts, now states the turn SLOT and defers the key to 18.2, where the null is scoped to the abandoned row alone and pinned by a new crossing contract (+56). The two 73-row transparency tables and the relation inventory keep their exact ROW SETS and the drift tests that pin them to `Class::ALL` and `EdgeKind` still hold in both directions. ONE header line moved: the shape table's third column was the same 27 characters on all 73 rows - `csift show @<id> --line N --raw`, an escape hatch that does not vary by leaf - so it is stated once above the table and the header reads `| leaf | on disk |`. The pin follows the header rather than the other way round: `model::inventory::tests::skill` takes it as a literal in two places and both are updated with it, which is what makes the column's removal a decision a test records rather than a cell a reader stops seeing. The same fold runs twice more inside the tables: the five `harness.notification.*` rows each carried the identical sentence about the `[SYSTEM NOTIFICATION - NOT USER INPUT]` frame their leaves share, and the seven attachment-borne `harness.context.*` rows the identical sentence about their `[context <payload type>]` render - each is now one sentence above its table, naming the two `harness.context.*` leaves it does NOT cover. SECTION 5.9's axis-1 paragraph is corrected against the same reading: it still read "nine leaves" with a seven-and-two split, a v0.12-era count the taxonomy rewrite left behind, against the 28 that `Class::llm_visible` and section 5.3 have carried since; its delivery axis now points at 5.8 rather than 5.5 and names five cases rather than three.
> ㊺ **The root help names every `floor_cause` value** (section 4.11). `floor_cause` was named as a KEY on seven help pages and its value domain on none, which left `--help` thinner than SKILL on a closed enum a consumer branches on - the five-document contract's one mechanical half, and the half that rots first. The global `--cross-compactions` doc now names all EIGHT, sorted by what a reader can DO about each: `budget-exhausted` alone is movable by a larger budget, and only when that boundary's door is open; `unresolvable-boundary`, `relink-removed` and `boundary-loop` are the three shut doors, each naming which fact shut it; and `head`, `dangling-parent`, `cycle` and `no-leaf` mean the walk ended BELOW the boundaries and never reached them at all. It rides the global flag rather than a per-command page because the flag is global and the enum is one. HELP TEXT IS SURFACE, so this bumps the triple with the rest of the release.
> ㊻ **The root help's newline pitfall said the opposite of what the binary does** (section 8.2's `body` contract, which has been right since v0.12.3 clause ①). The root `--help` carried "Text-mode excerpts keep a record's LITERAL newlines (a multiline Bash command renders as-is), so piping text output through `head -N` can cut mid-record" - and `match_excerpt` collapses every whitespace run to a single space, so a text-mode excerpt is ONE LINE per record and a `head -N` cuts whole records off the END rather than cutting into one. The pitfall was real and the mechanism was inverted, which is the worse kind of wrong: a reader avoided the pipe for a reason that does not exist and learned a false fact about the render. Measured on a two-record fixture whose user text carries two newlines and whose assistant text carries a paragraph break, both read back flat in text and in `excerpt`. The help now states the collapse, names `body` and `--raw` as the two ways back, and keeps the pipe advice on its true footing - a capped run names what it dropped, a pipe amputates one end of the ledger. The SAME measurement refutes a second sentence that was in SKILL alone: `body` was described as "the same text with its newlines intact", which holds for a record csift renders from its own text and NOT for an opener csift RECONSTRUCTS - `user.message`, `user.answer` and `user.rejection` are flattened a layer earlier, in the model, so their `body` is flat and `--raw` is the only way to those newlines (measured: an answered AskUserQuestion whose source joins its questions with newlines reports a single-line `body`). Both `body` descriptions in SKILL section 18.2 now say so. The historical v0.6.x ledger entry that introduced the false pitfall row is left standing as the record of what that release's docs said; this entry is the authoritative one. HELP TEXT IS SURFACE.
> ㊼ **`whoami`'s JSON stream has THREE summary terminators and both manuals named one or two** (section 6.14). An ordinary run closes `{identities, live_child_lanes, other_live_lanes}`, `--to` closes `{queued, refused}`, and `--peers` closes `{peers}` - three shapes that share NO key, so a consumer reading the terminator has to know which mode produced it. `csift whoami --help` described the terminator as `{kind:"summary", identities}`, one key of five across the three; SKILL 18.2 named two of the three and said in so many words that "the two shapes do not overlap". Both now name all three with what each key means, including that `identities` reads 1 where the caller resolved and 0 where DETECTION failed, which is a different fact from an empty scope. The same help edit closes a second parity gap the newline correction opened: the root help introduced `body` without saying it is NULL unless the excerpt cap was lifted (`search --no-truncate`, or a `show --line`/`--uuid` address), which SKILL states three times - a reader who took the help at its word would look for a key the default stream does not carry. HELP TEXT IS SURFACE.
> ㊽ **The introspection ledger is SCHEMA 2: version-ranged states, one MEASURED receipt each, and the prose law as a gate** (AGENTS section 7.1 carries the schema and the twenty-two gate rules). `INTROSPECTION.json` had grown a `checks[]` history - one appended record per release audit - and the history became the file: 1,856 checks behind 739 claims, 1,117 of them superseded by a later check on the same claim, every one carrying the calendar dates, csift versions and process words the prose law bars. A fact record does not need its own minutes. A claim now carries its `code[]` sites at CLAIM level - csift's tree is ONE tree at any time, so where a fact is relied on is never a property of a Claude Code version range - and one or two `states[]`, exactly one of them open and LAST. A state is a range (`from`, a `from_basis` saying where that floor came from, and `to` = null while open) plus the claim text for it, the two legs, the provenance and ONE `verified` receipt; `located` and `first_seen_claude_code` retire into the range. WHAT A RECEIPT IS became the round's largest correction: a receipt is the LAST check at the badge's build whose instrument names an ARTEFACT OUTSIDE csift's own tree - a build or executable, a byte offset in one, a corpus or projects root or jsonl population, a registry directory, a live trial - because the artefact is the test and never the command, so `grep` over `src` is the same text pass as reading the file while `grep -ab` over a shipped build is a measurement. Re-derived over the schema-1 file with that rule: every one of the 739 claims has an artefact check somewhere, 652 open states take the LAST check at the badge's build and 87 take an earlier one, because the newest check there is a text pass. Each state also gains a `probe`, the machine-readable twin of `instrument` naming the half a stranger re-runs first - `bytes` | `corpus` | `csift` | `live` | `repo` - derived from the INSTRUMENT and from nothing beside it: a command is never truncated (a cut at a semicolon names a different command than the one that ran), a `bytes` probe's rule says it is for RE-LOCATION of a producer hop rather than measurement, a `repo` probe re-verifies what csift does rather than what Claude Code does and so can never back a receipt, and a scope the instrument does not state reads `not stated:` rather than a template. The gate grows from twelve rules to twenty-two. Rule 13 gains the open state's position (LAST, because the list is a timeline) and `from_basis`; rule 10 now scans `residue` and a receipt's `note`, which is where every one of the fourteen unconsumed text instructions was hiding; rules 15 to 20 are the state's own text and its probe, a closed state's receipt, a retired selector handed to csift as an argument - the bare `tool` among them, with the valid `--count-by tool` census axis spared by POSITION rather than by word - the file's own shape, every heredoc handed back to its own language to parse, and the receipt's artefact itself - the classifier the selection rests on, living in the gate rather than only in the pass that writes the file, because a rule applied solely by the thing it grades is not a rule. Rule 12 learns that a hop can name the BUILD it was read in, which is what stops a Windows artifact and an older version being compared against a build nobody looked in and then marked `absent`; it refuses a run of four or more DOTS the way it refuses an empty excerpt, because that is a lossy render of non-printable bytes that slipped through as an ellipsis while checking nothing; it holds an ELIDED excerpt to the run BEFORE its first ellipsis at its own offset, since that bucket had been compared against nothing at all - replacing an already-elided excerpt with wholly fabricated text moved no figure over 230 of 7,625 anchors - while most `...`-bearing excerpts are byte-exact anyway and the README carries the count the walk derives; it refuses an excerpt that reads as PROSE whether or not the hop wears a mark, which is how census prose wearing an ellipsis anchored nothing and passed; a MARKED hop is decided by its mark BEFORE the eliding, so it lands in one of the five counts and never in two; and it compares BYTES rather than a UTF-8 round trip. Rule 21 reads the `bytes` probe's own literal in the builds its state names, a CLOSED state's among them wherever this host holds them - a probe's literal is a string whose presence is decidable at any build, which is where this rule and rule 12's byte LOCATIONS part company - because four of them counted ZERO in every one - two cut past where the build stops agreeing, two carrying an elision inside the literal - and a probe that counts nothing cannot be run. Rule 22 reads the same field from the other side and holds a probe's KIND and its SCOPE to the SAME artefact: the kind follows what the command reads, the scope is derived from the instrument, and nothing compared the two - thirty-eight states carried `corpus` beside a scope reading `the shipped Claude Code binary the state names`, and one carried `bytes` beside `the top-level *.jsonl of one projects root`, each half defensible alone and the pair contradicting itself. Only the two kinds whose artefact is FIXED are tested, because `csift` names a run, `live` a gesture and `repo` this tree, and any of the three may name either noun without contradicting itself. Rule 8 stops matching rows with a regex and REGENERATES the whole README block, because a regex checks what it names and three numbers on that page - a row whose key is not a bare word, every share percentage, a parenthetical count - were never read at all. The SWEEP that produced the file obeys the same line: it masks every quoted run, literal, byte offset, version AND whitespace run before any rule fires and restores each BYTE FOR BYTE, so a rewrite can neither cut a comma out of a jq argument list, nor normalise the double space out of an asctime literal, nor flatten the indent a python heredoc's block structure lives in - seven instruments and seven `probe.command` fields carry a heredoc rule 19 parses a PROGRAM out of - an interpreter word in front of the opener and a line that terminates it - and it hands each of the fourteen programs in them back to its own language to parse; three more instruments and one more field carry a bare `<<` that opens no program, which is why a count of them owes its rule, because a whitespace normalisation leaves every word of a sentence intact and every program in it dead - a date that is the OBJECT of an epoch is a fact and stays, and a second detector reads 0 punctuation orphans where a removed run had left its neighbour standing alone. The 1,117 folded checks are not dropped either: every fact token with no survivor in the state is carried into `residue`, and each carried entry NAMES what superseded it.
> ㊾ **The RENDER stops being the serial half of a search** (section 7k). Everything the scan does in parallel is spent before the first output byte, and on the landmark session a whole-scope `--format json` dump cost 1.94 s of CPU on ONE thread while nine rayon workers waited, while a wide-match text search (`search 'the'`) paid 0.45 s of its render phase's 0.59 s inside `println!`, which is one `write(2)` plus a backward newline scan per row - 297,976 of them. TWO changes; neither moves an output byte, and one moves an EXIT CODE - a write that cannot land (a closed pipe, `| head`) now reports `csift: error: Broken pipe (os error 32)` and exits non-zero wherever the FAILING write goes through the buffer, while a `println!` aborts the process with 101 as it always did. The rule is the WRITER, so a buffered surface has no exception inside it: its scope and duplicate banners go through the same `Out` as its rows, and one query answers a closed pipe one way whatever its scope. Every row-dumping surface writes through ONE `text::Out`, a `BufWriter` over a held `StdoutLock`: `search`'s text, JSON and `--raw` streams, both of `show`'s, `files` in all four detail modes and both projections, the `agents` tree and its flat rows, the `image` listing, and both of `verbatim`'s (its documented `--subagents` span is 125,269 text rows on the landmark, and 55,218 JSON ones). The lock is HELD, and a stray `println!` fails two ways: on the SAME thread the lock is reentrant, so the line lands AHEAD of what the buffer holds (no error, exit 0), and inside the parallel render closure it DEADLOCKS the process at scale. Both are stated at the module and both are guarded mechanically - every module that builds or writes through an `Out` denies `clippy::print_stdout` and `clippy::print_stderr`, each legitimate site carrying its `#[allow]` and reason - and every stderr note the contract orders after a byte stream is preceded by an explicit flush. Then `search` renders its per-EXCHANGE rows in bounded parallel chunks through `text::write_ordered` and writes the chunk buffers in INDEX ORDER, so the stream is the one a serial loop produced; the look-ahead is fixed at 8 rows per chunk and 16 chunks per wave, and since a chunk buffer grows by doubling the memory bound the pipeline adds is UP TO TWICE those 128 rendered rows (10 to 20 MB against a peak of about 1,570 MiB on the landmark's 411 MB of JSON, where 13 interleaved reps put the two medians 0.1 MiB apart inside overlapping spreads; on ONE fixture of 4.2 MB rendered rows three runs of five interleaved pairs read medians of 715.8, 844.2 and 723.7 MiB against a 512.2 MiB one-copy figure, the widest single observation 929.3 and the narrowest 522.3, and on a second fixture of 4.55 MiB rows between 0 and +127 MiB, so the observable peak is no floor on the bound and no ratio carries between fixtures or between runs). The one cross-row decision the text render makes - which row carries the single image-extraction hint - is taken by `first_image_row` BEFORE any worker starts, because a chunk cannot know what earlier chunks found. Medians of 7 interleaved reps on the landmark at a host load of 7 to 27: the JSON dump 2.958 s -> 1.532 s, `search 'x'` 1.247 -> 1.040, `search 'the'` 1.140 -> 0.931, a `--for` closure 1.080 -> 0.901, `show --branch-points` 0.397 -> 0.355, the `agents` tree 0.664 -> 0.619, `files --by timeline` 0.890 -> 0.858, and the small-output rows between 0.987x and 0.998x. Over the 128 tiny-and-compacted acid pairs the ratio median is 1.009 with a maximum of 1.069, against a same-binary control band whose median is 0.9997 and whose maximum is 1.065. What a render change cannot reach is stated rather than claimed away: the same query with `-c` floors the JSON dump at 1.120 s, `search 'the'` at 0.920 (it is AT its floor now), `search 'x'` at 0.910 and the heavy regex at 1.140 against its full 1.189, because that row's output is 2.5 MB and its clock is the regex over the scope's 1.18 GB. `-c`, `-l` and `--count-by` are untouched; `recover` keeps `println!` by measurement, its row loops being bounded by ONE FILE's history (over the 40 landmark paths carrying the most `files --by timeline` rows, at most 2,912 lines in any reporting mode, under a millisecond of writes inside a run of 1.1 to 1.4 s) while its restore mode already writes the whole content in one call. The `json!` Value tree is MEASURED and NOT replaced: an ablation puts 2.26 s of the render's 2.48 s of CPU in building and serializing it and 0.22 in the write, with the projection logic itself about 0.1 - and a streaming projector would have to hand-order every key (a `BTreeMap` serializes SORTED, a stream emits in call order) and re-shape `attach_facts`, which merges a family's facts into an already-built object. The parallel render reaches the targets without it.
> ㊿ **Four surfaces that describe csift's own output are held to the bytes, and carry no csift chronology** (section 5.2 carries the first). A manual and an error message are both read by someone holding a binary, so a sentence about a csift they have never seen costs them the fact it was carrying, and a quoted string that is not the bytes sends them looking for output no surface writes. ONE, the RETIRED SELECTOR MESSAGE: each of the thirteen hard errors carries `('<spelling>' <reason>, so this is: <successors>.)`, and the middle column is a REASON rather than a release, because which release retired a spelling changes nothing for a caller whose binary no longer has it, while the reason - a flat spelling against a `role.category.leaf` taxonomy, a category decided by the producer that wrote the record, or a leaf naming one shape - carries to the next spelling they guess.
