TWO, the SUBAGENT BRAND: one fact, that a row is a subagent transcript whose bare id is not a re-feedable target, is spelled FOUR ways across the seven surfaces that print it - `list` writes `SUBAGENT  <hex>  ·  parent SESSION <uuid>`, two spaces after the word and two around the separator; `files`, `recover`, `stats` and `verbatim` write one space after the word and two around the separator; `show` writes one of each; `image` suffixes its row `  SUBAGENT <hex> · parent <uuid>`, with no `SESSION` at all - so a manual quotes the string ITS OWN command prints (SKILL's `list` row, section 6.9's `image` row), and one that copied its sibling's would send a reader matching the manual against the output to match nothing. THREE, the CHRONOLOGY: over the eighteen rendered help pages there is no csift version rider left, no date on which something was measured, and no sentence describing a past csift; each was rewritten as the fact it was there to give. Every date that remains is an EXAMPLE the grammar needs - twenty-three ISO8601 instants in the `--since`/`--until` blocks, two more in the time-grammar list, one timestamp-format sample and one synthetic record instant - which is the same boundary the introspection ledger's prose law draws: a version floor of the thing being MODELLED is a fact about that object, while csift's own riders and the days on which something was measured are chronology. The rule reaches every page because `--help` is surface. The measurement that makes this a statement rather than an intention is a diff of all eighteen pages against the previous release under one basename: 602 rendered non-blank lines differ counted once per PAGE, 479 counted once per distinct line TEXT, the two figures differing by the lines that render on more than one page and by the two lines of the `--claude-home` entry that `search --help` moved within its page, which a diff reports as removed and added - the new root `--cross-compactions` block alone is 126 of the first and 7 of the second, and eleven version riders naming seven versions sat on five of the eighteen pages against none here. FOUR, the COMMENTS: a `.rs` comment carries a non-ASCII character only as a quotation of csift's own rendering - the glyph exactly as csift prints it, inside a backtick span - and a glyph never writes the comment's own typography or logic, because one character would then mean a quotation on one line and an arrow on the next with nothing in the character to say which. The set of glyphs a comment may quote is DERIVED, never typed, from the two shapes a page reaches the terminal by: every non-ASCII character inside a string or char literal of a non-test source file under `src/`, decoded through its escapes, plus every one in a `///` doc block on a clap argument or command, which clap renders verbatim as that item's help; a decoded code point that reaches no terminal is excluded with its reason. The pre-commit gate holds the character and the balance of the span, and the em dash is one of the printed glyphs (an absent instant renders as `—`), so it sits inside a quotation like any other and the older outright ban on it is the same rule read narrowly. Measured at the rewrite: 2,027 comment lines carried a non-ASCII character, 1,653 of them outside any quotation, against 0 violations at the tip, with the printed set at 26 glyphs.
> (51) **Five shapes the model receives that csift named as nothing** (§5, claims CLS-069 through CLS-073, REC-152). Each was a record the harness writes and csift carried no leaf for: not on any scan, absent from every census key, and reachable only as `? unmodeled: <shape>` under an address. ONE, the harness's TURN CONTINUATIONS. A turn the model does not finish is continued by a whole message written into it as an `isMeta` user record, and the producer interns NINE of them in one constant block collected into one array whose single consumer is its own is-this-a-record-I-wrote predicate, tested by membership. csift knew four - the redo nudges - and now knows all nine: the two CLASSIFIER-STOP forms (the second the first plus a sentence about a call that was already running), the OUTPUT-TOKEN-LIMIT resume, and the two MID-STREAM CUT-OFF forms, one per lane. All nine are matched by WHOLE-MESSAGE equality rather than by prefix, because they are complete bodies and the producer tests them that way; five carry an em dash, so a text retyped with an ASCII hyphen matches nothing. The leaf's one-line description widens with them, to the harness continuing a turn the model did not finish on its own instruction, and the shape inventory gains a row per event rather than one for all four. The classifier stop writes THREE records and only one needed a rule: the notice beside it is decided by its subtype and the refused assistant turn by its own blocks. TWO, the API REFUSAL as a fact on that assistant turn. `message.stop_details` is the API's own structured account of why it stopped - a type, the classifier band, its own sentence, and whether a fallback claimed a prefill - and the client builds none of it: it copies the object off the response frame onto every record of the turn, strips exactly one member, and constructs one of its own only on paths that null every remaining member, so a true prefill claim came from the API. The exposed-keys family stamps it on every emitted unit of a record that names a member: the label zone `[api <type>: <category>]` and, on the wire, `stop_details` beside the coarse `stop_reason`. The gate is the OBJECT and not the leaf, because a key is true of a record whatever csift labelled it; an object whose every member is null is refused, because the absent key already says what four nulls would. `size_of::<Record>()` moves from 1,376 to 1,384, the object sitting behind its own pointer inside `message`. THREE and FOUR, two attachment payload types the shape inventory had no row for. `mcp_instructions_delta` is what the harness writes when the set of MCP servers whose instructions belong in the request changes; the converter turns it into CONTENT - a `# MCP Server Instructions` section carrying the blocks added, and a second naming servers that disconnected - and persists that conversion back, so it is `harness.context.listing` with the model column true and the body rule takes the persisted conversion, which is what makes injected server instructions findable by a pattern scan. `thinking_drop` is the harness's accounting of the thinking blocks it dropped from a request, and its converter arm is an EXPLICIT empty list, so it joins `NEVER_CONVERTS`, reads `not delivered` whatever its leaf default says, and takes `harness.context.task` beside `thinking_stripped`. The HUMAN column on both, and on `thinking_stripped`, is read off the transcript render's own hide set rather than inferred from the leaf, and that list is PER BUILD: the verified build's hide set names `mcp_instructions_delta` and neither thinking payload - it cannot, since neither name occurs anywhere in that build - while the 2.1.278 one names all three, so the two thinking rows are read off the build that knows the types. FIVE, a BUNDLED SKILL BODY that ships no files. The `Base directory for this skill:` line the skill leaf keys on is written by ONE wrapper in the registrar and only for a registration that names FILES; one with none keeps its body getter untouched, so the same body reaches the same record bare. The leaf answers to a second head, the workflow-authoring reference's own - which is what the leaf already means, the prefix being an artefact of shipping files - and the documentation says what stays residue: the bare bodies share no head, and the records carry no field saying which command expanded them, the parent link naming the command's wrapper on only three of the four measured records. This clause SUPERSEDES clause (22) on the record's width alone: 1,376 becomes 1,384, and every other statement (22) makes about the exposed keys stands.
> (52) **A turn-companion record is not the person, so it opens no turn** (§4.2, claim TURN-034). `turnCompanion` is the harness's SECOND authorship stamp, on a `type:"user"` record it wrote to accompany a turn rather than receive from the person, and Claude Code's own is-this-the-person's-message predicate excludes it beside `isMeta`, before it looks at the content at all. The two are not one set: of the fourteen stamping sites in the verified build, twelve build an object carrying `isMeta` and TWO do not - a mapper over a slash command's expansion messages, and the tool-dispatch path for the extra `type:"user"` messages a tool returns beside its result - and both spread an EXISTING record and add the one key, so the divergent shape is constructible from either. Reading `isMeta` alone made such a record the operator's own turn: it opened a turn, shifted every later turn number on that transcript, and moved the live-turn count every surface derives from the same predicate. `is_genuine_user` reads both flags now, so `opens_turn` opens no turn on a companion, and `classify` reads them at the same arm, so the LABEL agrees with the BOUNDARY and a record csift refuses to call the human is not `user.message` under a selector either. A companion matching a template csift models keeps that leaf; one matching none renders `? unmodeled: user/turnCompanion` under an address, naming which stamp the harness used. The key rides the flattened, boxed exposed-key struct rather than a field of `Record`, so it costs a record naming no exposed key nothing, and it is the one exposed key read by a PREDICATE rather than projected as a fact - what a reader acts on is the leaf and the boundary it changes. The census that makes the rule a rule rather than a guess: over a frozen snapshot root and one projects root, every record carrying the flag carries `isMeta` too, so the divergent shape has zero specimens and the exclusion is taken from the harness's own predicate. This clause SUPERSEDES clause (22) on the KEY COUNT alone: nineteen keys ride `Exposed`, eighteen of them projected as facts on a hit and this one read by a predicate.
> (53) **A uuid prefix means its hex DIGITS** (§2 of AGENTS carries the grammar, claim PATH-016). The grammar admitted a dash-less run of 4 to 11 hex and the resolver compared it LITERALLY against the canonical basename, so a run past the first segment held its ninth digit against the dash at position 8 and matched no uuid at all: `@<8 hex>` resolved, `@<9 hex>` read "no session or agent id starts with". The dashed spelling had the mirror-image gap - a literal prefix of the `8-4-4-4-12` layout was a prefix from 12 characters up and "not a recognized @-target" at 9 to 11. One rule replaces both. A prefix is a dash-less run of 4 to 11 hex or a literal prefix of the layout that reaches its first dash (9 to 35 characters, a dash exactly at each template position it reaches), and either spelling is compared by its digits against every candidate id with the dashes dropped on both sides (`path::id_has_prefix`), so `@1a2b3c4d5` and `@1a2b3c4d-5` name the same sessions. The union domain, the uniqueness rule and both errors are unchanged, and a dash-less run of 12 or more is still an agent id. The rule rests on the harness minting every session id it names a transcript after in the dashed layout, which the claim traces and censuses.
> (54) **A `cost-state` checkpoint's two numbers are read as numbers** (clause ⑰'s `checkpoint_closes_at`, claims QT-042 and QT-043). The bookkeeping fact read `startTime` as a string and parsed it as an ISO instant. The harness writes a number: its builder takes the cost ledger's session start (`startTime:Yke()`), and the schema its own readers validate the line against declares every numeric key `number().nonnegative().finite()`. So the fact read absent on every real line - 96 of 96 on one projects root - while `list`'s `/clear` join, which read the same pair as numbers, kept working; the fixtures behind the fact had been written with a string `startTime`. The two now share ONE reading, `Record::checkpoint_close_ms`: both numbers, non-negative and finite, summed, and absent otherwise, which is the line the harness's readers refuse. TEXT renders the instant through the ms-precision local form every search header uses, `[checkpoint closes 2026-08-31 18:58:32.913 AEST(UTC+10)]`, and JSON `checkpoint_closes_at` keeps the UTC instant; the empty label names the requirement, `(needs startTime and totalDuration as numbers)`. The `search --help` sample head now carries epoch milliseconds where it carried an ISO string.
> (55) **The leaf tree tags `agent.message.structured` `[scan/needle]`** (§5.3). `search --help` gave the leaf the gate word `[-t/att]`, which its own legend defines as an attachment leaf only a selector or `--attachments` admits. The leaf's one shape row is a `structured_output` attachment with `Gate::DefaultNeedle`, so a flagless scan emits it through its own value needle, which the legend calls `[scan/needle]` and the channel paragraph on the same page already said. A test reads the inventory: every leaf whose shape rows ALL carry `Gate::DefaultNeedle` must carry that word in the tree, with this leaf as its positive control. HELP TEXT IS SURFACE.
> (56) **The root help names all seven `status` verdicts** (§6.13). Its `status` entry listed six and dropped `idle-background-open`, the verdict for a turn that ended with background tasks open, while `status --help` and the enum carried seven. `Verdict`, its slugs and a test-only `Verdict::ALL` now come from one declaration, and a test holds the root entry and the `status --help` verdict paragraph to every member. HELP TEXT IS SURFACE.
> (57) **`recover --help` counts SIX modes** (§6.7). The head said "FIVE MUTUALLY-EXCLUSIVE MODES" and listed the default restore plus four flags, while the MODE section of the same page names five exclusive flags, `--list-backups` among them. The head now counts the default restore and the five flags of the `mode` group, lists `--list-backups` with the others, and a test reads the number word and the listed flags off the clap group. HELP TEXT IS SURFACE.
> (58) **A file-history head's instant is local text** (§5, claim QT-025). The `harness.bookkeeping.file-history` head printed its line's raw UTC string (`[file-history snapshot at 2026-09-01T05:49:11.520Z: ...]`), the one text instant csift left in UTC - on all 7,694 such units one projects root carries. Both forms render through the millisecond local form the search and show headers use: a delta's top-level `timestamp`, and a snapshot's nested `snapshot.timestamp`, the only instant that line carries. The UTC string stays matchable in the verbatim JSON body, a delta's instant still rides the hit's `ts_utc`/`ts_local`, and a line naming no instant still prints `?`.
> (59) **The `@` hex grammar decides its four edge shapes, and every miss names the rule** (AGENTS section 2, claim PATH-016). Clause (53) made a prefix mean its digits; four shapes around it were decided by accident and are decided now. A dashed prefix past 11 characters (`@1a2b3c4d-5e6f-4a7b`) resolves, as the same digits dash-less would. A dash followed by one or two hex (`@1a2b3c4d-5`, `@1a2b3c4d-5e`) resolves too: the dashed spelling starts at the first dash. A dash-less run of 12 or more hex is an AGENT id - that agent, or the one agent id it starts - and never a session, because a 17-character agent id and a session's first 12 digits cannot be told apart by shape; a session prefix that long keeps its dashes. An agent-id PREFIX resolves across the whole domain the prefix grammar reads: 4 to 11 digits through the union prefix match, 12 to 16 through the agent path's fallback. The three misses name that grammar: the unrecognized-target error lists `@<id-prefix>` and `@<agent-id>` with what each resolves, the too-short error names both lists, and the agent miss says a 12+ run never names a session.
> (60) **A quoted default needle opens no gated leaf** (§5.3, claim QT-005). The channel keep's needle `csift-channel v1` is a bare value substring, and a `queue-operation` rider carries whatever a `<task-notification>` summary says - so a pulse that mentioned the envelope was admitted to a FLAGLESS scan and classified `harness.bookkeeping.queue`, a leaf the gate law admits only under a selector naming it: 13 such units on one projects root. The scan now takes every `user.queued` and `harness.bookkeeping.*` record whose own leaf this scan does not reach back out of the record set, per record, the way the attachment views are demoted - a `last-prompt` line as the spine row the byte walk lifts for it, every other bookkeeping type as nothing, which is what the non-candidate arm leaves for it. A selector naming the leaf still emits the line, and an address still renders it.
> (61) **`search`'s WHEN flags take a leading-`-` value in the space form** (§6.2). The grammar tolerates `-1y`, and `stats`, `list`, `files`, `agents`, `image`, `verbatim` and `status --background-since` all accepted `--since -1y`; `search`'s `--since` and `--until` did not carry `allow_hyphen_values`, so the same spelling was a clap usage error there alone. `--turn` already carried it and `--max-count` reads a negative number through `allow_negative_numbers`.
> (62) **`--for` bails on an address that names no record, the way `show` does** (§6.2). The closure answered an address past the end of the file, or a uuid no line carries, with an empty set and exit 0 - the same output as a real address the pattern matched nothing under. The address law makes a miss a hard error, so `search --for` now bails with `show`'s own wording (one shared builder, `search::address_miss`) when no transcript in scope holds the addressed record. Holding is read off each file's BYTES (`search::names_a_record`): a line number names a line the file has that is a JSON object, a uuid names a line carrying it as its own `uuid` - so neither the pattern's prefilter nor the whole-file gate decides it. A merged elicitation-sidecar record has no line and is not an address `--for` resolves.
> (63) **Every `wait --help` example carries `--timeout`** (§6.14). Two examples omitted the flag `wait` requires, so each failed when run as written. Both carry it, and a test reads every `csift wait` example line off the rendered page and requires it. HELP TEXT IS SURFACE.
> (64) **A `show` record row carries every key its `search` hit carries** (§6.3, §8.2). The `show` record row and the `search` hit each typed its own key list, and the row had drifted seven keys short: a pulse's `task_ids` and `orphan_kind`, plus `delivered`, `is_error`, `denial_kind`, `resume_paired` and `scheduled_at` on every row. Both are now built by ONE projector (`search::record_row_json`) that every key the two surfaces share comes from; each adds only its own keys on top (`search` the id trio, `excerpt` and the two refetch commands; `show` `kind`, the id trio, the turn slot, the draft distance and `text`). A row is what a hit's `refetch` prints, so the two cannot disagree about a record. > (65) **The stopped-agents note names its instant in the local form** (§6.13). The note `status` and `wait` print for the harness's stopped-agents notice cut the record's raw UTC timestamp to nineteen characters, the one instant on the text surface outside the canonical local form. It renders through `timez::format_timestamp` now, which keeps the second precision the note's (count, second) de-duplication keys on, and an absent timestamp renders the canonical `—`.
> (66) **`whoami --to` takes the `--from` its note names** (§6.17). The lane-unknown note `send` and `whoami --to` share tells a lane caller to pass `--from @<your lane id>`, and only `send` had the flag. The reach engine classifies its caller with the same function `send` does and decides partly from the relation between sender and receiver, so `whoami --to` takes `--from` (`requires = "to"`, the same grammar: `@<lane>` inside Claude Code, a free label outside) and hands it to that classifier. Without it the caller is assumed as before and the note still prints.
> (67) **The stopped-agents notice on the queue is a rider** (§5, claim QT-005). The rider law refused a queued `<task-notification>` and a queued peer message but not the harness's stopped-agents notice, which the harness hands to the same pending-notification queue (CLS-008) and whose queue line carries the key set a person's line carries (QT-006). `queued_class` now refuses it by `is_agents_stopped_notice`, the template predicate its delivered twin is refused by, so it takes `harness.bookkeeping.queue`. Censused over two roots: no other synthetic user marker occurs on a queue line.
> (68) **`status` and `wait` refuse a target naming several sessions** (§6.13, §6.14). `resolve_live_target` took the first top-level transcript a target resolved to, so a project path or an encoded directory made `status` and `wait` answer for whichever session sorted first. It now counts the DISTINCT session ids among the resolved top-level transcripts and bails above one, naming the count, the `@`-grammar and `csift list <target>`; two copies of one id are one session, which the resolver's own duplicate note already discloses.

Every path per id is kept now, a `*.jsonl` target goes through `resolve_named_agent` (the named file, never REPLACED by an unnamed transcript of its id: `dedup_union` leaves the unnamed copies of a named id undecided and merges the NAMED paths among themselves under the ordinary relation rule, so two paths naming byte-identical copies of one transcript read as one - on the live root's 107 agent transcripts sitting in both copies of a duplicated session - one observation per `agent-<id>.jsonl` present in both copies' sidecars at the same path under `subagents/`, 19 at the built-in location and 88 under a workflow run, all byte-identical - two such paths answer `search -c` 1 with `duplicate_transcripts` 1, where leaving every named id undecided answered 2 and 0), and the note's location phrase reads the members' paths: under N project directories, or under one session's sidecar at the built-in and the workflow locations. It now reads `select(.kind=="agent").parent_session_id` off `agents --agent <id>` and names the `no subagent matched` answer as the root case. HELP TEXT IS SURFACE.
> (71) **A missed `--line` range is named in the range grammar** (§6.11). `run_raw` and `run_rendered` each built a missed range as `L{a}-{b}`, a spelling `parse_range_spec` rejects on input with the correct one, so the error named an address the reader could not paste back. Both go through `show::range_miss_label`, which names every range in a spelling the parser accepts when pasted back: `L{a}..{b}` for a range that RESOLVED in order, and the token AS WRITTEN for one that resolved backwards - an open range starting past the end resolves to (start, last line), and `L99999..5003` is refused as reversed, so `--line 99999..` on a 5,003-line file misses as `L99999.. (file has 5003 lines)`. Measured on a 3-line fixture: of the misses for `99999..`, `10..20` and `5..-1`, the resolved spelling pasted back into `--line` was refused for two (`L99999..3`, `L5..3`); the three named now (`L99999..`, `L10..20`, `L5..-1`) all parse. `show::addresses::every_range_a_miss_names_parses_when_pasted_back` pastes each back in both fetch modes.
> (72) **A `--line` range is clamped to the file and its overhang counted apart** (§6.11, §8.2). `resolve_line_specs` kept an explicit range end past the last line, and `run_rendered` counted every addressed line with no hit as a non-record line, so `--line 4995..5045` on a 5,003-line file read `non_record_lines: 42` for 42 lines that do not exist. A range that STARTS inside the file is now clamped to the last line and `LineSpecs::past_end` holds the overhang; clamped ranges all extend from the last line, so their union past it is one run and the count is the widest overhang, never a sum. The summary gains `lines_past_end`, the text surface a footer and `--raw` a stderr note, all through `show::past_end_note`. A line or range starting past the end is left whole and misses, and `show::miss_label` names the file's line count in both fetch modes (the rendered mode printed only the address). The field now declares `conflicts_with`; `want_subagents` is unchanged (`--subagents` opts in, `--no-subagents` restates the default). > (74) **`stats` counts its scope before the row cap** (§6.12). `run_stats` called `scope_span` on the rows AFTER `--max-count` truncated them, so the banner and the header's `sessions_in_scope` / `top_level_sessions` / `subagent_sessions` described the kept rows rather than the target: `stats . --max-count 1` read 1 where `list . --max-count 1` read 1,713. > (75) **Smart-case reads the letters a pattern spells** (§6.2). `build_matcher` decided case with `pattern.chars().any(char::is_uppercase)`, so the `S` of `\S` made `\Sbash` case-sensitive: 38 records on one transcript where `(?i)\Sbash` read 46, and `\Wbash` 47 against 68. `search::pattern_has_uppercase_literal` (`matcher/smart_case.rs`) walks `regex_syntax`'s AST instead, the layer ripgrep's smart-case reads: a `Literal` counts, and inside a bracket class so does a literal item and either end of a range, so `[A-Z]` is a request for case; a Perl or Unicode class and an assertion are no literal. > (100) **The channel chunker counts the harness's unit** (§1.1, §6.17, claims MISC-012, MSG-013). `CHUNK_BUDGET` = 9,200 was counted in Unicode scalars, while the helper every hook string passes through compares `e.length`, JavaScript String.length, UTF-16 code units, against 10,000 and persists anything longer to disk behind a pointer and a preview that do not carry the envelope header. An astral character is one scalar and two units, so a chunk packed to 9,200 of them overshot the cap by up to 1.84 times, two units for every astral character (the first part of a 20,000-emoji body measured 9,200 scalars and 18,019 units, 1.80 times the cap, its ASCII framing counted once); measured through `deliver` on a scratch Claude home, 6,000 emoji rendered ONE chunk of 6,486 scalars and 12,486 units, and a 7,500-character CJK body (two BMP ideographs to one extension-B) one of 10,486 units. `envelope::render` now builds a running unit count of the body and `pack` measures the header, the tail and each run in units, taking the longest run of WHOLE characters that fits (`partition_point` over the running count), so a character the budget would halve waits for the next part and the header is never cut from the chunk; the part-count fixpoint is unchanged. The same bodies render two parts of 9,199 and 3,335 units and two of 9,200 and 1,334; a 14,000-character ASCII body renders its two parts of 9,200 and 5,334 as before, BMP text being one unit per character. `send`'s chunk count reads the same `render`. `commands::live::channel::tests::envelope` gains an astral body (under the budget and one unit under it, where every continuation boundary falls where a character count would split a pair) and a CJK body, and the existing tests measure in units. > (103) **`recover --help` states the anchor rule per side** (§6.7). The page said "Every anchor demands a SINGLE simple command ...; pipes, chains, substitutions ... all stay in the boundary lanes", which is `bash_anchors`' rule for a READ anchor only (`read` is set when the command has one segment). WRITE anchors are SEGMENT-scoped - one per admissible write segment, the command marked `multi_segment` - and the caller admits them from a compound command only on a clean result echo (empty stderr, not interrupted; an errored result is skipped outright) and refuses one whose path another segment WRITES - a mutation row the shell heuristic records for the same resolved path (a redirect or `tee` into it, `cp`/`mv` onto it, `mv` away from it, `rm`, `touch`, `sed -i`), because segment order is not replayed within one command - while a read or a mode change of that path (`wc -l`, `chmod +x`) leaves the anchor standing; a segment is a run between `;`, `&&`, `||`, `|` and newline. Measured on a fixture holding one Bash call, a quoted heredoc to a file followed on the next line by `wc -l` of it, with a clean result: `recover --file` restores the two lines and `--coverage` counts one `bash-write-anchor`, on the released binary and on a build of this change alike. The page now names the literal-operand and substitution-free rules for every anchor, the single-command rule for reads, and the per-segment rule with its clean-result and same-path-write conditions for writes, the `||` chain among the separators. Measured on four one-call fixtures, a quoted heredoc to a file followed by a second segment on that file with a clean result: `wc -l` of it, `chmod +x` of it and `|| echo failed` each count one `bash-write-anchor`, and `cp` of another file onto it counts none. `cli::tests::pages::the_recover_page_states_the_read_and_the_write_anchor_rules_apart` reads the sentences and runs `bash_anchors` on a chained write, a piped read and a lone read, and `recover::tests::bash_anchors::a_second_segment_refuses_the_write_anchor_only_by_writing_its_path` runs the four shapes through the extractor. HELP TEXT IS SURFACE.

Output is byte-identical: 8 of 8 `whoami` forms (env, a teammate, a workflow lane, the routing form, `--peers`, each in text and JSON where it applies) on a two-session fixture, and 6 of 6 alternating text and JSON runs on the live root. The e2e `channel::peers_edges::the_census_reads_each_live_session_s_own_children` holds a second live session whose child must be read from its own sidecar, which fails when the reuse is taken for every row or for the wrong one.

(83) made `agents`' lifecycle read `JournalCache::settled_by_run` before a lane's tail, and every lane that rule settled read `completed` with a completion instant - a lane its run killed, and a lane its journal closed with `failed`, looked exactly like one that returned. > (115) **A `--file` that names several files is refused** (§6.7). `recover --file` matches a record path by exact string or by a component-aligned suffix, and the suffix arm named every path the argument ended. The extractors now match through `recover::Target`, which records every file its target matched in a transcript (`ScanResult::matched_paths`) - a full path as written, a relative one as the file its record's shell cwd resolves it to (124); `run_recover` unions them over the scope and refuses, BEFORE any replay and in every mode, a target that matched more than one - naming each candidate and the count and pointing at a candidate or a longer suffix. A target naming a single file behaves as before. A `--files-from` line takes the same rule per line: the row reads `ambiguous` with no file written, stderr names the candidates, and the summary line adds `· N ambiguous (names several files, not replayed)` when N is non-zero. `recover::targets` pins the refusal in the five modes, the three ways of naming one file that keep working, and the manifest row; `recover::tests::events::a_target_records_every_file_it_matched_and_nothing_else` pins the recording. csift's own rule: the ledger carries no claim for it. > (116) **The compaction banner names where the turns beside it end** (§6.8). `boundary_banner_line` printed `(turns below predate it)`. The render walks the selected turns in file order and `maybe_boundary_banner` emits a banner where `compactions_before` - the summaries strictly past a turn's LAST content line - drops, so the turns ABOVE a banner end before the summary's line and the turns BELOW end after it: the words named the wrong side. Measured on a fixture whose boundary is L4 and summary L5, with a turn opening at L3 and replying at L6 (a compaction mid-turn): the banner sat between L2 and L3, with L3, L6, L7 and L8 below it, of which only L3 precedes the summary; on the golden reconstruction every turn below each of the three banners follows its summary. The banner now reads `(turns above end before it, turns below end after it)`, true in both shapes: a mid-turn turn opens before the summary and ends after it, which is why the words say where turns END and make no claim about when they began. The reserved banner cost follows through the one builder the renderer and the budget share, so `tests/turns_pre_feature_baseline.txt` moves on its three banner lines and on the header's `4899` -> `4986 / 40000 chars used` (29 characters per banner). `verbatim::windows::the_compaction_banner_names_where_the_turns_beside_it_end` pins the banner and the order on the mid-turn fixture. > (118) **An answer marker opens a turn only on the AskUserQuestion result** (§4.4, §6.4, claim TURN-009). The AskUserQuestion answer's marker fallback - for a carrier with no `toolUseResult` - read the TEXT alone: any `tool_result` whose content opens with `Your questions have been answered:` (or another listed phrasing) was `user.answer` and opened a turn. A shell run over the harness binary prints the template at content start with no `toolUseResult`, so its result read as the person's answer. The fallback now requires the `tool_result` carrying the marker to name, by its `tool_use_id`, an AskUserQuestion `tool_use` of the same transcript, and a result naming no id names none. The join is `model::CallIndex` - the ExitPlanMode plan pointers the rejection render already used plus the AskUserQuestion call ids - built ONCE per transcript by the conversation chain's builder from the full records it holds and read by the turn boundary, the `user.answer` label and the rendered opener alike, so the three cannot disagree; the chained grouper reads the chain's own per-record answer instead of re-deriving it. The index is filled from the records a surface PARSED - a spine row carries no payload (claim REC-104) - so every surface that builds the chain must parse the line carrying the call: `files` and `recover` prefilter for file traffic and dropped it, so a marker-only answer opened its turn on `search`/`show`/`stats`/`verbatim` and on neither of those two, which numbered every later turn one lower. Both prefilters now admit that line by their own needle (`parse::line_may_carry_ask_call`: the tool name, `&&`-gated on the assistant role; a line merely quoting the word costs one parse and adds nothing to the index, which reads only a `tool_use` block of that name). `image` agrees without one, because its `--turn` window parses every line. On the transcript that mentions the tool most (415 MB, 651 assistant lines carrying the word) the host resolves no difference: medians of 21 runs, `files --by timeline` 527 ms without the needle and 519 ms with it, `recover --coverage` 673 and 672 ms over 7, user CPU +0.9% and +1.3%, at a load average near 12. That reading measured the needle alone. The whole `recover` path, read against the build before the relative-path candidate rules of (124) and (126) (interleaved runs, the order rotated each round, 15 rounds, user-CPU medians, a second copy of the same binary as the control band), cost more: on the 397 MiB landmark `recover --no-subagents --file <path> --coverage` took 1.072 and 1.084 times the earlier build's user CPU, for a path the transcript's own tools never write and for its most-edited path, against a control band of 1.7% and 2.0%, at a load average between 167 and 213 (the wall clock at that load reads nothing). Every record path it compared built a joined, normalised candidate string - a file-history snapshot names every tracked file, so that is most of the comparisons - and every `tool_use` copied its id and cwd into the call map. A candidate is now built only where it can match (`recover::path_matches_as` refuses first when the key's last component is a plain name other than the target's), `recover::normalize_path` hands back an unchanged path borrowed, and the call map borrows its ids and cwds: the same readings are 1.050 and 1.025 times the earlier build and 0.980 and 0.946 times the build before the change. A second reading at a load average between 47 and 68, 21 rounds, reads the same shape inside a tighter band: the build before the change took 1.067 and 1.077 times the earlier build's user CPU against a band of 0.3% and 0.9%, and this change takes 1.043 and 1.030 times it, 0.978 and 0.957 times the build before. So the change takes 2 to 5% of user CPU back. What remains over the earlier build depends on the transcript and the path, so it is stated per row, each beside its band. A third reading under the same method - 31 rounds, four arms: the earlier build, a second copy of it as the control, the build before the change and this one - at a load average between 11 and 25: on the landmark's most-touched path (97 events, `files --by file`), 1.028 times the earlier build against a band of 0.1%, the build before the change 1.060; on the largest transcript of the live root (721 MB; its most-touched path, 546 events, every one a shell command), 0.990 against a band of 0.5%, the build before 1.005. So the remainder runs from inside the band on the largest transcript to about 2.5 to 3% on the landmark, and these readings do not attribute it; the AskUserQuestion needle's scan of every line the other needles do not admit is one cost they do not separate. The output is byte-identical on both paths in the restore, `--patches`, `--coverage` and JSON modes. On the live root, which holds no marker-only answer, `files` and `search` give each of 26,744 mutation lines the same turn with and without it. After: 127 records in 21 transcripts, every one answering an AskUserQuestion call; the 6 read `agent.tool.result`, each of their transcripts reads 1 turn, and the phrase query returns 0. All 6 sit inside the measuring session's own subagent transcripts, so with that tree excluded the reading is 121 records in 20 transcripts both before and after. A surface holding no transcript takes the structured signals alone - `list`'s preview and the live tail render an answer from `toolUseResult` and render no marker-only carrier - and the search prefilter re-renders a marker line as though its result answered an ask, the superset its whole-file gate needs. `search::taxonomy::openers::an_answer_marker_opens_a_turn_only_on_the_askuserquestion_result` pins one fixture on `search -t user.answer`, `show --line`, `--count-by turn`, `stats`, `verbatim`, `files --by timeline` (and its `--turn 1` window) and `recover --patches` / `--turn 1 --coverage`; `model::tests::boundaries` pins the join, the missing-id case and the no-transcript case. > (124) **A relative record path is a `--file` candidate** (§6.7). (115) counted only FULL record paths as candidates, so a matched relative path was replayed and never counted: on a fixture whose records all carry the shell cwd `/Users/dev/Projects/w_app`, `recover --file lib.rs` over `/p/m/lib.rs` (m1 -> M2) beside `other/lib.rs` (o1 -> O2) printed `O2` and `(recovered lib.rs: 1 lines, complete ...)` with `--coverage` reading `events: 2 edit · 2 write`, and over `a/lib.rs` beside `b/lib.rs` printed `RB2 ... complete`, exit 0 both. Every matched path is now a candidate: a full path as written, a relative one as the file its own record's shell cwd resolves it to (`recover::candidate`, through the same lexical `join_shell_path` the bash extractors use), and a relative one on a line that carries NO cwd - a file-history snapshot's `trackedFileBackups` key, a delta's `trackingPath` - as the file the transcript's ORIGINAL cwd resolves it to (`recover::original_cwd`, the absolute `cwd` of the first record carrying one, which the target carries as `Target::with_origin_cwd`); the raw string where the transcript carries no absolute cwd at all or the path leads with `$` or `~`. The original cwd is what such a key is relative to, and the live projects root says so (every `*.jsonl` but `journal.jsonl`, `elicitations.jsonl` and `csift-channel/`; one PAIR per distinct transcript, relative key and absolute Write/Edit/MultiEdit/NotebookEdit `tool_use` path ending in `/<key>`): 5,405 snapshot lines, 0 carrying a cwd; 1,999 pairs over 38 top-level transcripts, and the key joined to the first cwd IS the paired path on 1,922, while each of the other 77 pairs is a key whose join is ANOTHER path the same transcript wrote - a different file sharing the tail, which stays a refusal - so every one of the 1,922 paired keys names a file its transcript's tools wrote; the nearest record's cwd - the absolute cwd on the line nearest the key's first occurrence by line distance, the earlier line on a tie - names the paired path on 1,805 and on none the first cwd misses. With the measuring session's own tree excluded: 1,839 pairs over 37 transcripts, 1,764 / 75 / 1,647. The neighbour rule moves the count and never the conclusion: at a later instant of the root (2,017 pairs over 40 transcripts, the first cwd naming the paired path on 1,940) the nearest line read 1,820 and the preceding line carrying an absolute cwd, else the following one, read 1,820 too, neither naming a pair the first cwd misses (1,857 / 1,782 / 1,662 / 1,662 with the measuring session's tree excluded). Eleven distinct keys carry `..` (one transcript), and the lexical join resolves all eleven to a path the transcript wrote where a plain concatenation resolves none. Counting such a key raw had refused every suffix target on those transcripts: over 20 sampled pairs, `recover <transcript> --no-subagents --file <key> --coverage` exited 0 on none, and exits 0 on 18 with the rule, the other two naming several files that end with the key. Both fixtures are refused in every mode, naming the two resolved files; a single relative path alone is not ambiguous. A target also names a relative record path through that resolved file, and every candidate a refusal prints, passed back, replays that file: a resolved one by the ordinary rule (`--file /Users/dev/Projects/w_app/other/lib.rs` replays `O2`), and one the OTHER candidates end with - a raw relative path from a transcript with no cwd beside an absolute file sharing its tail - because a target that is itself one of its candidates re-scans for that file alone (`recover::several_files`, `Target::exact`) and says so on stderr, a note naming the target, the count and that every other candidate ends with it; a manifest line takes the same rule. `recover::targets::a_relative_snapshot_key_names_the_file_under_the_transcripts_first_cwd` and `every_candidate_a_refusal_prints_replays_when_passed_back` pin both halves. A tool result's relative `filePath` resolves against the cwd of the CALL it answers, the anchor law of AGENTS 3.11 (`recover::collect_tool_use_cwds`, paired by `tool_use_id` within the turn, `recover::CarrierCwds`), and against the result's own cwd only where no call is in reach; a Bash result's stale-read hint keeps the carrying record's cwd, the shell's after the command ran. On the live projects root 36 `toolUseResult` objects carry a relative `filePath` (one per object, every `*.jsonl` but `journal.jsonl`, `elicitations.jsonl` and `csift-channel/`), every one pairs with its call, and 0 sit on a record whose cwd differs from the call's, so no reading there moves; `recover::tests::events::a_relative_result_file_path_takes_the_cwd_of_its_call` pins the shape the law decides. The bash extractors match an operand verbatim as a belt beside its resolved form and record the RESOLVED form for either (`path_matches_as`), so one file never mints two candidates. On the live projects root (one count per Write/Edit/MultiEdit/NotebookEdit `tool_use` block with a path, every `*.jsonl` but `journal.jsonl`, `elicitations.jsonl` and `csift-channel/`), 35 of 49,240 structured paths are relative, all `.`-led; 35 of 36,044 with the measuring session's own tree excluded. `recover::targets::a_suffix_naming_a_relative_record_path_beside_another_file_is_refused` pins both fixtures and the passed-back candidates. OUTPUT AND HELP TEXT ARE SURFACE.
> (125) **`verbatim`'s skip note names the budget that renders** (§6.8). A targeted session whose first round-trip does not fit is reported `skipped — its first round-trip needs ≥ N chars`, and N was the doc-header reservation rendered at the budget the run was GIVEN plus the cheapest side of the newest turn. The header prints the budget, so its width follows the budget's digit count and, since (117), its plural: measured on a four-record fixture whose smallest rendering budget is 399, `--budget 1` printed `needs ≥ 392`, `2` and `9` printed 393, `10` and `99` printed 396, `100` through `398` printed 399. N is now the FIXPOINT - the least budget `b` with `reservation(b) + cheapest <= b`, iterated up from `cheapest` (`turns::min_render_chars`, the reservation never shrinking as `b` grows) - so every skipped budget names 399, 399 renders and 398 skips. `verbatim::budget::the_skip_note_names_the_budget_that_renders` pins N from `--budget 1`, the same N across a digit-count and a plural change, N rendering and N - 1 skipping. OUTPUT TEXT IS SURFACE.
> (126) **The several-files refusal names its origin, both outcomes and a bounded list** (§6.7). Three wordings of (115)/(124) said less than the rule: a `--files-from` line's note read `--file \`lib.rs\``, the batch summary `recovered N file(s)` counted an unreplayed ambiguous line among the recovered, and the refusal said replaying the candidates "together would stitch their edits into one buffer", which is what candidates in ONE transcript do - candidates in different transcripts replay one file's history and drop the others'. And the candidate list was uncapped: on a fixture naming 13 files, 13 candidate lines. The refusal now names its origin (`--file` or `manifest line`), says both outcomes, lists the first ten candidates and counts the rest (`  ... +3 more (a longer suffix narrows the list)`); the summary counts an ambiguous line beside the recovered files. Two spellings of one file compare LEXICALLY (`recover::normalize_path`, applied to the target and to every candidate alike): `.` segments are dropped and a run of separators collapses to its first character, while a LEADING pair stays (POSIX leaves a leading `//` to the implementation; it is the Windows UNC shape) and three or more leading separators are one. Before, a relative record path WITH a cwd was normalised through `join_shell_path` and every other spelling was compared as a string, so on sessions writing then editing one file under two spellings the target's spelling decided what replayed: with `./a/lib.rs` then `a/lib.rs` (cwd on every record), `--file lib.rs` replayed `C3` while `--file ./a/lib.rs` replayed the first spelling's events alone and printed `C2 ... complete`, content the later edit replaced; `a//lib.rs` then `a/lib.rs` did the same (`--file a//lib.rs` printed `S2`); `/p//q/lib.rs` then `/p/q/lib.rs` was refused as two files; and `./a/lib.rs` beside `a/lib.rs` with an empty cwd was refused as two while `--file ./a/lib.rs` printed `E2`. Every spelling of each now replays the file's whole history (`C3`, `S3`, `T3`, `E3`). Nothing that needs the filesystem is resolved: a `..` segment stays as written - a symlinked directory makes it name another place - except inside a relative path joined to its record's cwd, which takes the shell join's lexical `..` as a Bash operand does (the eleven `..` keys of (124) resolve that way), and `/tmp/x` beside the `/private/tmp/x` a symlink resolves to stay two files, refused as two, each passed back replaying only the events recorded under its spelling. On the live projects root 0 of 50,155 absolute Write/Edit/MultiEdit/NotebookEdit `tool_use` paths carry `//` or a `.` segment and 4 carry `..`. `recover::spellings` pins the four sessions in every spelling and the `..` and leading-`//` pairs that stay two files; `recover::tests::targets::a_path_is_compared_in_one_lexical_spelling` pins the rule. `recover::targets::a_refusal_lists_ten_candidates_and_counts_the_rest` and the manifest e2e pin the wording, the cap and the summary. OUTPUT AND HELP TEXT ARE SURFACE.
> (127) **The hint's third line puts the TaskStop fact before the SendMessage refusal** (§6.5, claims SUB-020, SUB-035). The line read "SendMessage refuses any `to` carrying `@`, so the routing id Name@Team ... is what TaskStop takes", which offers the refusal as the reason TaskStop takes that form; the two are separate facts about two tools. It now reads "the routing id Name@Team (the `routing:` field above) is a form TaskStop takes; SendMessage refuses any `to` carrying `@`, so that id is never a SendMessage `to`, which takes the bare name or a<Name>-<hex> ...". The facts are unchanged, and `agents::routing::agents_teammate_hint_states_the_two_id_rule` pins the line whole. SUB-020's `depends` now counts the hint as three lines and names all three constants. OUTPUT TEXT IS SURFACE.
> (128) **`show`'s cap continuation stays in the address domain the reader used** (§6.11). The rendered fetch kept the first `cap` units in RENDER order, which sorts an abandoned unit after every live one, and read the continuation's ends off that order: on a live transcript `show --line 1..400` dropped 200 units and printed `--line 201..393` - L393 an abandoned `user.unsent` - so the seven records at L394-L400 fell out of the continuation (it fetched 193 records where `--line 201..400 --max-count 0` fetches 200); with the abandoned record among the KEPT, the continuation's two ends came from different sides of it and the range ran backwards. The cap now keeps the records on the LOWEST lines, whole records - a record's units share its line - as many as fit in `cap` and at least one (`show::cap_units`, which the raw fetch shares, one line a unit). One `--line A..B` range from the first dropped line to the end of what was addressed is exact for a single line range and for nothing else: `--line 1..4,9..14` cut at L3 continued with `--line 3..14`, which also fetches L5-L8 (never addressed); a `--uuid` list continued with `--line <first>..<last>`, every record between; and a `--turn` window continued with a line range carrying the records the window leaves out (an abandoned draft, an attachment line). So the continuation is written in the reader's own domain. A line address continues as its ranges clipped at the first dropped line (`--line 3..4,9..14`), a uuid address as the dropped uuids in the order given (a uuid whose records fall on both sides of the cut - a replay copy carries its survivor's uuid - names its dropped records by line, because `--uuid` would fetch the kept copy again), and a mixed `--line`/`--uuid` address as both parts in one command, a part with nothing dropped left out; each is exact. A turn window continues as `--turn K..<its last turn>`, `K` the turn of the first dropped unit, and a window keeps its earliest turns (by line within a turn) so that what it keeps is a prefix of the turns its continuation re-enters. A window cannot start mid-turn, so when the cap cut inside `tK` the continuation passes over that turn's units already shown with `--skip M`, `M` the units of `tK` this page showed or its own `--skip` passed over (no `--skip` when `M` is 0). `show --skip N` drops the first N record units of what the address selects, in the order the cap keeps them - lowest line first, a sidecar-merged unit last, a window by turn and then line, the units of one record in render order - before the cap applies, and counts lines under `--raw`, which is what the raw cap counts. Without it a turn wider than the cap continued as the command just run: on the 397 MiB landmark, where 11 turns hold more than 200 record units, `--turn 286` (536 units) printed `--turn 286` again with a count of the 200 units it repeated, and the turn's last 336 units were never reached at the default cap. It now takes three pages, 200 + 200 + 136, which together are the `--max-count 0` fetch with no unit missing or repeated. So a continuation always moves forward and, run at any cap, never repeats a unit already shown: following the printed continuations at one cap until none is printed yields every unit of the uncapped fetch exactly once. The line, uuid and mixed forms never needed the skip, because the cap keeps whole records and each continuation starts at the first dropped one: a four-unit record at `--max-count 1` or `2` is kept whole and the next page starts at the next record. A `--skip` that reaches past every unit the address selects prints an empty page and a stderr note, exit 0, because the address resolved; `--skip` is not an address, so an addressless `show` is refused with it as without it, and clap refuses it beside `--branch-points`, which fetches no records. The JSON summary carries the count `--skip` dropped as `dropped_by_skip`. A line list is not used for a window because its dropped lines are not a few ranges: on the 397 MiB landmark `show --turn ..` at the default cap drops 30,495 units on 30,358 lines in 17,534 separate runs, spread over L802-L123036 where 91,877 lines are not fetched by the window at all. The text pointer and JSON `refetch_remainder` print the one command in every form, and the `--raw` stderr note writes its continuation by the same rule. `--line 1..400` on the live transcript above still continues `--line 201..400` and fetches the 200 records it dropped. `show::tests` pins each domain on hand-built unit lists, and `show::caps::every_address_form_pages_forward_to_the_whole_fetch_exactly_once` follows the continuations of every form at caps 1, 2, 3 and 5, rendered and raw, through a transcript carrying a turn wider than the cap, an abandoned draft, an attachment line, a replay copy and a sidecar-merged record. OUTPUT, JSON AND HELP TEXT ARE SURFACE.
> (129) **A section unit is emitted only where the classification reads the section** (§5, claim TURN-017). `Record::record_text_sections` - one unit per `<task-notification>` and inbound peer section - asked neither the record's type nor its flags, while the classification reads those sections only on a `type:"user"` record that is neither an elicitation marker nor a compaction summary (`classify` -> `classify_user`). So an ASSISTANT text opening with the relay preamble and a quoted `<agent-message>` emitted an `agent.communication.inbox` unit on a record labelled `agent.message.plain`, and a compaction summary carrying a peer section emitted one on a record labelled `harness.compaction.summary`: a unit whose `label` its own `labels` lacked, the invariant every hit states. Over the live projects root, 2 of 13,611 inbox hits were such units before and 0 of 13,622 after (1 and 0 with the measuring session's tree excluded). The emission now takes the classification's gate. `search::lanes::an_assistant_quoting_the_relay_framing_emits_no_inbox_unit` pins the assistant, the summary and a real peer record beside them. OUTPUT IS SURFACE.
> (130) **The `[feedback: ...]` tag is one line** (§5, the exposed keys). The tag cut a refusal's typed text to its first 60 characters and kept its newlines, so a refusal typed over several lines printed the rest of its label zone, and of its hit, on new lines. Over the live projects root 14 records carry a `userFeedback` string, 13 of them with a newline inside the first 60 characters, over 5 transcripts; `search '' <T> --no-subagents -t user.rejection` over those 5 printed 110 text lines for 13 hits before and 97 after, one per hit. The tag now flattens the text before it cuts it (`text::collapse_and_truncate`, the rule the `agents` preview already takes); JSON `user_feedback` keeps the words whole, newlines included, and `search --help` says the excerpt is on one line. `search::taxonomy::keys::a_multi_line_refusal_renders_one_line_per_hit` and `search::tests::keys::a_multi_line_refusal_is_one_label_zone_line` pin both halves. OUTPUT AND HELP TEXT ARE SURFACE.
> (131) **A manifest older than its lane settles nothing** (§6.5, §6.13, claim TAIL-008). A workflow lane with no closing journal line was settled by its run's manifest whenever one existed, but `resumeFromRunId` keeps a run's id and its lanes' directory, so a run killed and resumed later runs under the manifest the killed run wrote (`status: killed`) until the resumed run ends and rewrites it: every live lane of the resumed run read `killed` under `agents`, `status` counted none of them live, `whoami --peers` omitted them and `wait --until stop` could stop on them. The manifest's own `timestamp` is its run's end instant (one reading of one live projects root, one manifest file an observation: 261 manifests, all carrying a `timestamp` within a second of the file's modification time, 260 of them within 70 ms of `startTime + durationMs` and the worst 131 ms from it; 227 / 226 / 131 ms with the measuring session's tree excluded), so a manifest now settles a lane only when the lane's newest record is no more than a minute newer than that instant (`JournalCache::run_reading`, `MANIFEST_SLACK_MS`); a lane newer than that is read from its tail and its row says what was observed - the manifest `ended before this lane's newest record: the lane wrote after its run's end, most likely a run resumed under its id` (`settled_by`, and the child's evidence under `status`), because a record newer than the manifest is the evidence and the resume is its likely cause, not a fact the files state. The minute is measured: of the 8,801 lanes of manifested runs on that root, 58 of killed runs carry a newest record 1 to 66 ms after their manifest's end - the kill's own writes - and 11 lanes of 2 resumed runs 7,000 s or more after it (39 and the same 11 with the measuring session's tree excluded). On the five sessions holding such lanes 4 lanes with no closing journal line move from their run's word to their tails (`agents`: 5,999 / 104 / 102 / 57 completed / killed / failed / running, then 5,997 / 102 / 102 / 62, the root grown by one lane between); the other 7 resumed lanes are closed by their own journal lines, which stay decisive. The journal's in-flight count still leaves out a run with a manifest: journal lines carry no instant, and the journal file's write instant sits within a millisecond of its manifest's on normal runs (7 of the 9 journals written after their manifest are 0 ms after it, one of them with 7 unclosed starts), so it cannot tell a resumed run's open agents from a killed run's - a resumed lane is counted by its own tail once its transcript exists. `live::run_settle::a_manifest_older_than_its_lane_settles_nothing` pins a manifest eleven minutes older than its lane (read from the tail, live) beside one 20 ms older (the kill's write, `killed`). OUTPUT AND HELP TEXT ARE SURFACE.
> (132) **`show --help` says a boundary fetched alone reports its mode** (§6.11). The JSON paragraph said the boundary's `mode` "stays null when the fetch did not include one" - the summary it pairs with - while the pairing (`model::SummarizeIndex`) is built from the whole parsed record set before any `--line`, `--uuid`, `--turn` or cap narrows the output (AGENTS 3.5): on a live transcript `show --line 2790 --format json` fetches a boundary alone and reports `"mode":"compact"`. The sentence now says the pairing is file-wide, so a boundary fetched alone reports its mode, and that only the FILE leaves one unpaired - no summary after it, or a second boundary first. No output moved. `compaction::show_help_says_a_boundary_fetched_alone_reports_its_mode` pins the sentence beside the existing single-boundary fetch. HELP TEXT IS SURFACE.
> (133) **`status`'s `last ◂` is the person's newest words, turn-opening or not** (§6.13, claim TAIL-013). The row took the newest TURN OPENER, so a session whose newest prompt was a slash command typed with arguments - `user.message` beside `harness.command.invocation`, and no turn opener - printed no `last ◂` at all (a live session: JSON `last.user` null). The row now takes the newest record labelled `user.message`, `user.answer` or `user.rejection` (`classify` with no transcript context), whether or not it opened a turn, in the text `search` renders for that leaf: the opener render, then `commands::search::record_raw_text` for a relayed message or a queued steer, the one command-to-command edge this adds (AGENTS 5). The tail walk admits a `queued_command` attachment line beside the role-bearing ones, since a steer carries no `role` key. A `<task-notification>` pulse, which the row took as its label, is a machine trigger and no longer takes it; `status --help` says which records count. Measured over the 59 most recently active top-level sessions of the live root (the measuring session excluded): 56 rows unchanged, 2 that read a pulse label now read the prompt the person typed before it, 1 that read a pulse label now reads a slash command; the session above reads `/name args`. `live::tests::background::last_messages_take_the_person_s_newest_words_whether_or_not_they_opened_a_turn` pins five shapes (a with-args wrapper, a bare wrapper that does not count, a relayed message, a queued steer, a rejection) and `live::background::the_last_section_shows_a_slash_command_typed_with_arguments` the section end to end. HELP TEXT IS SURFACE.
> (134) **A return carrier's text unit names the call its result unit names** (§6.2). A subagent-return carrier that holds TEXT beside its `tool_result` - on disk, a fork child's first carrier: the fork call's result, then its `<fork-boilerplate>` directive - emits two `agent.communication.inbox` units, the record-level one for the text (`record_text_emission`) and the block one for the result. The block unit carried the `tool_use_id`, so it read `pairing` `paired`, the tool name and a `result_of` edge, while the text unit carried none and read `pairing` null, no tool name and a `parent` edge alone. `Record::subagent_return_call_id` now names the call a return carrier returns - the one function `is_subagent_return` also reads - and the text unit carries it, so the two units agree: `paired` with the tool name and the call's line where the call is in the file, `orphan` with no tool name and `result_of <id> (not in this file)` where it is not. A unit whose record names no call (a peer message, an idle signal section) stays outside the pairing axis. Measured over the whole live root with a `-t agent.communication.inbox -t agent.tool.result` scan: 441 carriers carry both labels and emit 497 units; before, 56 carriers emitted a text unit reading null beside a `paired` result unit and 441 units carried a tool name and a `result_of` edge; after, all 497 do and the 56 read `paired` twice (38 of 423 carriers with the measuring session's own tree left out). No carrier of the 56 has its call off the file on this root, so the `orphan` arm is pinned on a fixture only. `search::classify::comm::a_return_carrier_s_text_unit_names_the_call_its_result_unit_names` pins both arms.
> (135) **A zero match under `--turn` names the records the window left out** (§6.2 zero-match self-diagnosis). The diagnosis probed only the label filter, so a window that emptied a result was blamed on `-t`: on a live session holding one recalled draft, `search "" @<id> --no-subagents -t user.unsent` finds it and the same query with `--turn ..` finds nothing while the stderr line says the filter DOES occur under 3,598 records of other labels and `-t` excluded them - sending the reader to drop the label filter, where dropping `--turn` is what brings the draft back. An abandoned record belongs to no numbered turn (§3, the survival axis), so any window leaves it out. A zero match under `--turn` now also re-runs the same pattern, label filter, time window and reverse closure with the window dropped and counts the records outside turn numbering it matches (`search::diagnose_zero_match`, exchanges with no `turn_index`); a non-zero count prints one stderr line - the count, the leaves, what such records are and the two remedies, drop `--turn` or window by time - ahead of the label probe's, and the JSON summary carries it as `outside_turn_numbering` (0 when the probe found none, null without `--turn`). The cost is paid only on a zero-match run with `--turn` active over a scope whose first scan counted an abandoned record: the records a window leaves out are exactly the abandoned ones, which that scan already counts (`abandoned_records`), so a scope with none answers 0 without the probe. On five top-level transcripts of the live root with no abandoned record (1.8 to 2.7 MB each), a zero-match `search --turn ..` took 50.1 ms of user CPU against 63.6 ms with the probe and 50.6 ms at the build before the probe existed (medians of 21 interleaved rounds, a second copy of the probing build 0.8% from it, load near 9). The terminal modes print the same stderr lines, since the diagnosis runs before they return. `search::diagnosis::a_turn_window_that_left_out_an_abandoned_match_says_so` pins the line, its order, the terminal modes and the key on a fixture with a recalled draft, a pattern the draft lacks (no line, 0) and a query with no window (null); `a_turn_window_over_a_session_with_nothing_abandoned_adds_no_line` pins the absence. OUTPUT, JSON AND HELP TEXT ARE SURFACE.
> (136) **The environment form's parent line says unknown** (§6.18 `whoami`). With no target `whoami` resolves the lane from `$CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, which names the top-level session in every lane, and its JSON reports `parent_session_id`, `is_subagent` and `depth` as null while its text calls the lane and the depth unknown - yet the `parent` line read `none (a top-level session has no lane above it)`, which is false read from a subagent, whose parent is the very session the line sits under. In the environment form the line now says unknown the way the depth line does and names what each case would be: `parent   unknown from the environment alone (a top-level session has none; a subagent's parent is this session)`. A form that resolved the lane (`@trap:<marker>`, `@<agent-id>`, `@<Name>@<Team>`) keeps the parent it resolved, and a resolved lane with no parent still reads `none`. `channel::whoami::a_top_level_target_shows_its_live_child_lanes_and_no_parent` pins the environment form's line and a teammate target's resolved parent. OUTPUT TEXT IS SURFACE.
> (137) **A raw `--uuid` fetch names the records the rendered one does** (§6.11). Two shapes differed. A uuid two lines carry - a replay copy and the line that survives it (§4.11) - fetched both units rendered and ONE line raw: the raw walk stopped looking for a uuid at its first line, so it printed the COPY and never the survivor. And the uuid of a record merged from the elicitation sidecar rendered as `(elicitation sidecar)` while `--raw` exited 1 with `no such record(s)`, although the address resolved. The raw walk now keeps every line whose own uuid is addressed (a line the uuid's bytes merely occur on - a child naming it as its parent, a body quoting it - is parsed once and does not count), prints them lowest line first, and reads the sidecar only when a uuid is otherwise a miss: a uuid it names prints nothing on stdout and the note `uuid <U> names a sidecar-merged record, which has no physical transcript line — omitted under --raw (the rendered fetch shows it)` on stderr, exit 0, the omission `search --raw` already makes; a uuid no line and no sidecar record carries stays a miss, exit 1. `show::caps::a_raw_uuid_fetch_names_the_records_the_rendered_one_does` pins the three, and the chain pin (128) now runs its sidecar forms raw as well. OUTPUT IS SURFACE.
> (138) **A lane is a fork clone by its line 1's TYPE** (§5.5, claim REC-031). `search` decides whether a subagent transcript is a `/fork` clone - whose first opener is the parent's own human message - or a genuine child - whose first opener is the parent-to-self spawn seed - by line 1, and it read the BARE literal `fork-context-ref` in the first 4096 bytes of that line. A genuine child's line 1 is its spawn prompt, and a prompt about forks names the record in prose: on one projects root 6 of 11,280 lane transcripts carry the literal inside a `type:"user"` seed against 33 whose line 1 is the record (1 against 15 with the measuring session's own lanes left out), and each of the 6 had its seed classified `user.message`, its direction dropped and its words counted as the person's. The test is now the QUOTED value `"fork-context-ref"`, the form the candidate gate reads: inside a JSON string a quote is stored escaped, so the quoted form occurs only where a whole string value reads it, and it agrees with the line's parsed `type` on all 11,280 lines. `search::lanes::a_spawn_prompt_that_names_a_fork_record_is_still_the_seed` pins the seed beside the clone test. OUTPUT IS SURFACE.
> **v0.12.3 CHANGE LEDGER (non-breaking; one added JSON field; `verbatim`'s fold accounting; ported-checker fidelity; the session-lineage leftovers -- `csift 0.12.3`).**
> ① **`body`, the newline-preserving companion of `excerpt`** (§8.2 carries the contract). A hit's `excerpt` is a match-CENTERED fragment and it is one line by contract - `match_excerpt` collapses every whitespace run to a single space - so a consumer reading it as the message loses every paragraph boundary and every table row, and that holds even under `--no-truncate`, where nothing was clipped. Measured on a synthetic table-bearing assistant record: the rendered body is 638 chars with 21 newlines and 72 `|`; `search --format json` reports an `excerpt` of 412 chars / 0 newlines / 47 `|` under the default cap and 636 / 0 / 72 under `--no-truncate`, and `show --line N --format json` reports the same 636 / 0 / 72 in its `text`. The flattening site is `search::match_excerpt` (`normalize_line` on both the head form and the match-centered window). `body` now carries that same rendered section text VERBATIM (638 / 21 / 72), per section on a multi-section record, on every `search` hit and sibling and on every `show` record row. It is non-null EXACTLY when the excerpt cap was lifted, read off the one budget: `search --no-truncate`, and `show`'s `--line`/`--uuid` address; NULL under the default cap, where the excerpt is a fragment anyway and a second full copy of every matched record would multiply a wide scan's stream for a consumer that did not ask for the body. `show --turn` selects records through the default cap (it is a window, not an address), so its rows report a capped `text` and a null `body` - and the §4 note that claimed `--turn` lifts the cap is corrected with it. `body` is the RENDERED form, so a source the shared opener renderer already flattened in the MODEL layer (`user.message` and the other `reconstructed_user_text` cases, via `flatten_content_text`) carries no newline there either; `--raw` stays the only path to the bytes. `excerpt`, `show`'s `text` and every text-mode byte are unchanged in both modes, pinned by a before/after comparison of the whole JSON stream with the new key removed. `verbatim --format json` flattens its unit `text` too (the same 636 / 0 / 72), but at a different layer: `Record::agent_text` normalizes in the model, and `verbatim`'s `full_chars` / `elided_chars` / `elided_lines` accounting and its dedup fingerprints are all computed from the normalized string - so a body there is a change to the unit pipeline rather than an added field, and it is not part of this item.
> ② **The fold marker names what it folded.** A collapsed agent-message run rendered `[X agent message(s), Y tool call(s)[, Z failed]]` -- counts and a line range, with nothing about the prose it stood for, so a folded finding and a folded "let me look into this" read identically. The marker is now `△ L{first}–L{last}  [X agent message(s) collapsed, N chars, Y tool call(s)[, Z failed]]`, where N is the summed `full_chars` of the collapsed bodies, and every collapsed message of at least `COLLAPSED_PREVIEW_MIN_CHARS` (210) is followed by an indented preview line `    L{n}  {first COLLAPSED_PREVIEW_CHARS=60 chars}… (+K chars)` through `text::truncate_excerpt`, so the excerpt states its own remainder. 210 sits ABOVE the pure-declaration band (`declaration_max_chars` 200 at the default profile, 240 at `light`), so a preview means "this was not a declaration"; the band a preview can occupy at all is 210 up to `rich_min_chars` (280 default, 360 at `light`), because a message at or above the rich threshold is KEPT rather than folded. `turns::agent_placeholder_lines` is the ONE list the renderer emits and `agent_placeholder_cost` charges, so the summed-cost == summed-emitted invariant covers the previews too. JSON `collapsed_agents` rows gain `collapsed_chars` and `collapsed_previews[]` (`{line, excerpt}` each). Cost on the installed hook's path (`--slices 4 --window 9000`), measured over the two largest top-level transcripts of one corpus: 2 and 3 fold markers in the whole four-slice document, 0 preview lines, and +41 / +61 chars from the `N chars` clause on a ~24,700-char document. At an unbounded budget over a WHOLE session the preview band is reached by 42 of 1,224 and 212 of 1,759 folded messages.
> ③ **The `L lines elided` note counts the newlines the CUT removed.** It used to be the whole body's newline count, which made it independent of how much was actually deleted: two different cuts of one message printed the same figure, and a 17-char cut could claim 95 lines. Measured over the two largest top-level transcripts of one corpus, 18 of 2,480 truncated units reported MORE elided lines than elided characters -- arithmetically impossible, and the cheapest proof the figure was not describing the cut. A `TurnUnit` now carries `newline_positions`: the char offsets, in the one-line rendered text's own coordinates, at which the original body's newlines landed (a newline is not a character of the collapsed form, so its address is the space that replaced its whitespace run; newlines collapsed together share an offset and are each counted). `turns::elided_newlines` counts the offsets STRICTLY INSIDE the removed span `[cut_from, cut_to)`, so an uncut body reads 0, a cut falling between two newlines reads 0, and a wider cut of the same message reads more. The positions are recorded only when the rendered text IS the normalization of the record's raw body; a FABRICATED body (an AskUserQuestion scaffold, an automation attribution label, a peer-message preview) reports no lines, because the raw record's newlines sit nowhere in a string csift composed -- the old figure was measured against a different string. `model::normalize_line_with_newlines` shares the ONE collapse walk with `normalize_line`, so the string and the offsets cannot drift.
> ④ **A same-prefix RE-SEND folds to one line** (claim MSG-095; the shape rests on CLS-016, the `Stop hook feedback:` isMeta record, and MSG-017, the exit-2 vehicle that produces it). When one turn carries two assistant messages and the LATER one's body CONTAINS the earlier one's whole body -- a re-send with an addendum -- the earlier renders as `△ L{n}  [superseded by the same-prefix re-send at L{m}, N chars]` (N = the suppressed body's own char count) instead of a second copy of the prose, and the survivor prints in full. `turns::mark_resends` is a second stage over the keep/collapse lane: only KEPT entries participate (a message already inside a placeholder is suppressed anyway, and the survivor must itself be rendered, or the fold would hide both bodies), the comparison key is the summary-dedup normalization (`normalize_line` + lowercase, the `DEDUP_PREFIX`-wide head grouping the candidates and the FULL text deciding containment), and a body under `DEDUP_PREFIX` chars never qualifies -- the fingerprint is only the strict discriminator its own doc claims at full width, and below it the marker costs more than the body. JSON emits the superseded unit's row IN FULL with `superseded_by_line`; the text fold loses nothing a machine reader needs. **Why containment and not prefix equality:** censused over every top-level transcript of one corpus (80 files, 6,468 turns, 35,052 assistant messages), 53 same-turn pairs shared their first 80 normalized chars, of which 1 was a true duplicate, 25 diverged before char 120, and 22 had a SHORTER later member -- a fold on the 80-char match alone would have suppressed prose the survivor does not carry. Under containment the same corpus yields 2 folds, both exact duplicates. **Why the name says same-prefix and not Stop-hook:** of that corpus's 36 `^Stop hook feedback` records, 30 have their nearest assistant neighbours in the same turn and 0 of those pairs share 20 or more normalized chars (29 share none), and no same-prefix pair has a feedback record between its members -- so the rule is named for what it matches.
> ⑤ **The docs say WHICH path the per-role caps apply to** (documentation only; `ASST_CAP` is unchanged). The 600/900 body caps are the DIRECT path's -- a plain run, `--out`, and the legacy budget-driven `--slice i` alone -- while `--slices N`, the fixed-fleet hook-injection path, drops them for a window cap and holds its slice count by DISCARDING the oldest turns that do not fit. A reader who met a 900-char assistant body on one path had no way to know the other path does not cap at all, and the `--slices` flag doc was the only place that said so. §6.8 now states it beside the ellipsis rule, with the measured cost of the direct cap: over the last 40 turns of the two largest top-level transcripts of one corpus, the 900-char cap keeps 32,404 of 59,965 assistant chars (27 of 42 units cut) and 67,846 of 117,878 (48 of 101 cut); at 1,800 those become 50,657 (12 cut) and 95,262 (18 cut), so raising it would keep 18,253 and 27,416 more chars. The number is recorded for the owner's decision, not acted on.
> ⑥ **The generation-3 blanking mask reads a backslash with the harness's own rule** (claim BASH-041; AGENTS §3.9 carries the generation table). `out` masks its input twice before the clause walk starts - once blanking every quoted run and comment, for the function-definition scan, once keeping them, for the `set --` scan - and those two answers decide the POSITIONAL allowance, the flag the `$1`/`$@`/`$*`/`$!` target form is gated on. csift's mask treated every backslash outside a single quote as an escape and emitted one character per character. The harness decides it with the same `RNe` the shield reads - never an escape inside a single quote, always one outside quotes, and inside a double quote only before `"`, `\`, `$` or a backtick - and blanks an in-quote escape as a PAIR, so an escaped quote does not close the run that hides it. The old reading ended a double-quoted run at the first escaped quote and flipped the quote state for the whole remainder of the command, which reads a function definition written INSIDE a quoted string as a real one and blanks a definition that genuinely FOLLOWS the string. Both directions are real: over 165,314 distinct (Bash command, version) pairs of one corpus the two readings disagree on 4,097 masked strings and on 165 function-scan answers, 145 of them where the pair rule hides a definition the character rule revealed and 20 the other way. NO corpus lane changes verdict and no output byte moves, for two reasons that are properties of the corpus rather than of the rule: the generations before the rewrite never call a mask at all, and the three differing commands recorded at or past the rewrite floor carry no `$`, which the classifier's entry guard wants before the mask is read. The rule is observable exactly where the escaped character IS a quote, because a blanked pair is two blanks either way - what moves is the quote state, and with it which later text is blanked and whether a later `#` opens a comment.
> ⑦ **`list` names the child a BACKGROUND HANDOFF continued the session in** (claim REC-106).
