A session handed to a background child - the left-arrow gesture, or the background-fork command - leaves one line in the PARENT transcript: `{type:"continued-in", timestamp, sessionId, continuedInSessionId}`, four keys and no `uuid`, so it is not a chain node and its only address is its jsonl line. This is the THIRD lineage link on a `list` row and the only one that is not an inference: `clone_of` is read off a boundary shape and `cleared_from` off a checkpoint sum, while here Claude Code writes the child id itself. Text adds one `handoff` row naming the child's first-8 token; JSON adds `continued_in` (the full id, null when absent). Two facts bound the reading. An in-place `/fork` passes `keepParent` and writes no such line, so absence never means "not forked"; and the append also happens on the failed-spawn path once a rescue row is queued, so presence does not prove the child ran - Claude Code's own reader opens the named child and demands conversation content in it before hiding the parent from the resume picker. WINDOW LIMIT, stated because `list` is a head/tail reader and not a census: the line is appended after the last conversation record, so the backward tail walk normally reaches it before the anchors that stop the walk, but a later `--resume` of the parent appends turns below it and can push it outside both windows, where `continued_in` reads null. The whole-file answer is `stats`' line-type census, which already counts `continued-in` as one of its `types` keys. Cost: the line carries no `role`, so the `list` prefilter gains one `||`-arm memmem on the quoted KEY - the R13 key-only form, which survives a reserialize and which a prose mention of the type value cannot trip.
> ⑧ **`list` reports the BACKGROUND-LANE stamp `sessionKind`, and `--lineage` gives its SPAN** (claim REC-107). Every record a background lane writes carries a top-level `sessionKind:"bg"`; a foreground lane's records carry no such key at all, because ONE stamp builds the value from ONE environment variable whose value is undefined there and `JSON.stringify` drops an undefined property. The key is therefore a per-PROCESS fact, never a per-record one, and it stops appearing in the SAME transcript once a foreground process appends to it - which is exactly what a resume of a backgrounded session does. So `session_kind` is an ARRAY (text row `lane`), EMPTY for an ordinary session rather than a sentinel value, read from the head and tail windows - which is also where Claude Code's own session summary reads it, off the FIRST record of the file. The SPAN cannot come from a window, so it is opt-in: `--lineage` runs ONE whole-file pass and fills `session_kind_first_line` / `session_kind_last_line`, with `lineage_scanned` separating "not asked for" from "no carrier"; without the flag both are null and the row says so. That pass is a depth-1 key walk that never parses a payload, so a nested key is not a field, a torn line simply carries no key, and it contributes NO malformed count - `skipped_lines` keeps meaning the head/tail lines read (R12) and `stats` stays the whole-file census authority. `--lineage` also resolves ①'s `continued_in` over the whole file. COST, measured with hyperfine on the largest transcript of a real corpus (720,932,882 bytes, 21 reps, four arms interleaved round by round, one of them the base binary run twice to size the host): medians 254.5 ms base, 308.4 ms for that same-binary control, 266.4 ms for this tip's default path and 646.0 ms under `--lineage`. The default arm's 1.047x therefore sits INSIDE the 1.212x control band, while `--lineage` is 2.1x the control and 3.68x its user CPU (+314 ms) - which is why it is a flag and not the default. At corpus scale (9,600 files, 6,401,813,687 bytes) the default arm's user CPU is 4.674 s against 4.537 s base and 4.621 s control, again inside the band.
> ⑨ **`plan --audit` QUALIFIES each binding with four facts its own records carry** (claim PLAN-022, resting on PLAN-002, PLAN-005, PLAN-006, PLAN-011, PLAN-014 and PLAN-016). The audit said which plan file a session binds; these four say how much that binding is worth, each as a text block and a JSON field, and each reading a fact on disk rather than deriving one. (a) The slug's CHANGE POINTS - `slug  L<n>  none -> <value>` for the mint, `<a> -> <b>` for a binding that MOVED (JSON `slug_changes:[{line, from, to}]` with `from:null` for the mint, plus `first_slug_line`/`first_slug_utc`/`first_slug_local`). The binding csift reports is the harness's; this says WHEN it became so, which matters because a fork strips the slug from the records it copies and a first own compaction can mint a fresh one. Measured over 9,601 transcripts, 7,810 carry a slug and every one of them has exactly one distinct value and exactly one absent-to-value transition, with zero value-to-value and zero value-to-absent transitions - so a second change point is a shape the corpus has never shown, and printing them is how a reader would ever see it. (b) Whether the bound plan file is on DISK: `[exists]`/`[missing]` beside the path and `plan_exists` in JSON, the SAME `PlanRef.plan_exists` field the forward view prints, so the two cannot disagree. `[missing]` is an ordinary state (PLAN-014: the name is minted at Plan-Mode entry and the `.md` lands only when content is first written), not a fault. (c) A `plan_file_reference` attachment present while NO record carries a slug: plan text with nothing bound to it, which nothing will re-inject, because only the BOUND plan comes back in full after a compaction (PLAN-016). It emits a `plan-unbound-text` row and a warning and is counted in `unbound_plan_text` and in `warnings`. It has ZERO corpus specimens - all 14 `plan_file_reference` transcripts carry a slug - and is kept because the fork path that strips a slug can produce it; the detector reads the attachment's own `type`, so a prose mention never fires it. (d) The first slug-carrying record against the plan file's BIRTH instant (`std::fs::Metadata::created`): `before` is the ordinary order, `after` means this transcript bound a file that already existed, and `unknown` carries its reason in `slug_vs_plan_file_reason` - no file, no timestamp, or a platform that records no birth time - rather than a guessed direction. Cost: ONE walk per in-scope transcript beside the binding resolution the audit already runs, and the slug half goes through the depth-1 key walk rather than a record parse, because a slugged transcript carries the key on most of its records.
> **v0.12.2 CHANGE LEDGER (non-breaking; the cleared-session surfaces, one new leaf and one matching fix -- `csift 0.12.2`).**
> ① **`list` joins a cleared session to the transcript it was cleared FROM** (claims MISC-044, MISC-045, REC-105, QT-042, QT-044; §4.10 carries the facts). `/clear` mints a new session id inside the same process and writes no lineage on disk, so the join is an inference and the row says so. A top-level transcript is `minted_by: "clear"` when its first non-isMeta user record is the `/clear` slash wrapper - the wrapper for the command that ENDED the previous session lands in the file that command CREATED, which is what makes the new file self-identifying. Its predecessor is the sibling in the SAME project directory carrying a `cost-state` line whose `startTime + totalDuration` sits within 2000 ms of the wrapper's timestamp; the nearest LINE wins, and a tie between two different FILES is reported and joined to neither, exactly as `clone_of` refuses its own ambiguity. Text adds one `cleared` row naming the predecessor's first-8 token, the distance and its direction; JSON adds `minted_by`, `cleared_from`, `cleared_from_distance_ms` and `cleared_from_candidates`. Never a file mtime, never the time adjacency of ordinary records: two busy sessions share those, and only the checkpoint sum is the boundary between two sessions. The probe rides the head scan `list` already runs, and the sibling sweep runs only for a transcript that opens with the wrapper.
> ② **`status` and `wait` report a tail checkpoint as EVIDENCE** (claim QT-043). When a transcript's LAST line is a `cost-state` line, a `checkpoint` evidence row prints directly under the `tail` row it qualifies, naming the line and the four events that write one, and JSON carries `last_checkpoint: {line, kind}`. There is NO new verdict: the seven-verdict set stays closed so a consumer matching on it keeps matching, and the registry row keeps deciding liveness. The checkpoint is repeatable - 35 of 44 checkpoint lines over one corpus are not their file's last - so a mid-file one is reported as nothing at all. When there is no registry row AND the tail is a checkpoint, that note gains the clause that the session closed or was handed over.
> ③ **`status` finds the task store a clear left behind** (claim REG-018). The store is named after the id the PROCESS started with, and `/clear` never touches it, so the owner's own id is now the first candidate rather than the only one: then the ROOT of the `cleared_from` chain, then the team file whose `createdAt` sits within 5 s of the registry row's `startedAt`, taking its `name` as the list id because `leadSessionId` can be a throwaway id after a `--resume` launch. A tier is consulted only when the tier above it found nothing, a directory matching no candidate is never read, and every store that answered prints as `tasks store: <dir> (via <candidate>)` with a `tasks_stores` array in JSON - a list recovered by inference never reads as the session's own, and an EMPTY store prints too, marked `- empty`, because the directory existing is the answer to where the list came from. This is the one place v0.12.2 changes a PRE-EXISTING JSON key rather than adding one: a session whose store is found through the `cleared_from` root or the team file now reports its tasks where it reported none, so `tasks` moves from null to an array and `tasks_completed` from null to a count. Over one corpus that is 2 of 80 top-level sessions; the other 78 resolve through the session's own id exactly as before.
> ④ **`plan` states the clear corollary** (claim PLAN-021). The id regeneration calls `forgetPlanSlug` on the outgoing id, so a plan binding does not survive a clear: a clear-minted transcript has none until plan mode is entered again, and `plan` reports none rather than inheriting the predecessor's. No code change - the resolver already keys on the transcript's own `plan_mode` attachment.
> ⑤ **A new leaf `harness.schedule.fire`: the PROMPT a scheduled task fires** (claims CLS-031, CLS-032; §5.1 carries the full leaf text). When a cron entry or a `ScheduleWakeup` timer comes due, Claude Code writes a `system`/`scheduled_task_fire` record naming the instant and then SUBMITS the armed text as its own `type:"user"` record - `isMeta:true`, `promptSource:"system"`, parented to that record's uuid. The text reaches the record VERBATIM: the fire path applies exactly one rewrite, the autonomous-loop sentinel resolution, whose two entry points each read `resolve() ?? resolveFile() ?? prompt`, so an ordinary armed prompt falls through unchanged. With no marker to match, such a record matched nothing and fell into the isMeta exclusion - no label, invisible to a flagless scan, absent from every census, which is why a whole project's scheduled work was unsearchable. The leaf keys on the record's FIELDS instead and its arm sits LAST among the harness marker arms, so a tick that DOES carry a loop marker keeps `harness.schedule.wakeup` / `harness.meta.loop`. An inbound peer message carries the identical `isMeta` + `promptSource` stamp (the submit path puts it on every isMeta submission, not just the scheduler's), so two guards refuse one: the relay framing through the shared `is_peer_message` predicate, and the `origin` object the harness stamps on everything it did not submit itself - the framing guard alone misses a tagless delivery. The INSTANT rides the label zone (`[scheduled fire <when>]`, JSON `scheduled_at`), display-only like `[summarize <dir>]` because it comes from another record; it is joined by `parentUuid` through a per-file index built from the fire lines one more `&&`-gated C-40 conjunction admits (`scheduled_task_fire` AND the key-only `"subtype"`) and DEMOTED back to spine rows once read, so a flagless scan gains no `harness.meta.system` hit from that keep. `Class::ALL` grows to 38 leaves and every search hit's JSON gains `scheduled_at` (null on every other hit). Size, so a reader can weigh it, every figure counted per raw record over the 80 top-level transcripts of one corpus: 171 records carry the fire shape (`type:"user"`, `isMeta` true, `promptSource:"system"`, no `origin`) over 5 files, 168 take the new leaf and 3 keep a tick leaf by arm order; the 465 `scheduled_task_fire` records (top-level `type:"system"` with that `subtype`) sit in those same 5 files. The 7 files that carry the literal ANYWHERE are a different rule and a different number.
> ⑥ **The autonomous-loop markers anchor at CONTENT START** (claim CLS-014). `harness.schedule.wakeup` and `harness.meta.loop` matched their five markers - two headers, two body sentences and the sentinel - ANYWHERE in a body, so a record that merely QUOTED a fired tick was read as one and lost the leaf it had earned: a skill's instruction text that embeds the loop preamble to explain it, a peer message relaying a tick, a compaction summary recapping one. The harness resolver builds the WHOLE delivered prompt and the fire writes it as its own record, so a real tick carries its marker at offset 0; both arms now test exactly that, after the same leading-whitespace trim the resume-prompt arm uses. A genuine tick classifies as before. Over one corpus 11 records carry a loop marker only mid-body, and one of them - the skill's instruction text - was the single record either leaf claimed wrongly. The same trial that found it produced the FIRST specimen of both leaves (one wakeup, two driver ticks), which raises CLS-014 from producer-only to end-to-end.

> **v0.12.1 CHANGE LEDGER (non-breaking; one render fix, one prefilter conjunction, and the survival axis's serial cost removed -- `csift 0.12.1`).**
> ① **An orphan reconciliation pulse renders EVERY task id it closes, and names the kind** (claim BG-020). At the next session start Claude Code reconciles the tasks a previous session left open with ONE `<task-notification>` carrying several `<task-id>` tags, a `__orphan_summary__:<kind>` sentinel that names no task, and status `stopped`. The background scan already joined every id; the RENDER read only the first, so a pulse closing five tasks showed one and hid four, and the sentinel could take the id slot outright. `model::section_task_ids` now splits one section's tags by the shared `__orphan_summary` prefix Claude Code gives its own scan markers: the real ids render comma-joined in the id slot of `[<kind> <task-id>[, <task-id>...] <status>]`, the sentinel's kind renders as a trailing `(orphan reconciliation: <kind>)`, and `AutomationTrigger::task_id` resolves to the first REAL id. `automation_label_for_section` is the one helper behind `search`, `show`, `list` and `verbatim`, so every record surface follows. `search` hit rows gain `task_ids` (array) and `orphan_kind`, carried per SECTION because a batched record's sections name different tasks. Rarity, so a reader can size the change: over one corpus, of 3076 notification sections on the top-level transcripts, 2 carry more than one `<task-id>`, both orphan reconciliations, naming 3 and 2 real ids in 2 sessions.
> ② **The D7 compaction-boundary keep is a CONJUNCTION** (claims CMP-002, CLS-021, QT-SYS-001; §4.7 carries the counting rule, §7d the prefilter statement). Behind the same `-t` gate the keep now wants the key-only bytes `"subtype"` beside the literal `compact_boundary`, the literal tested first so the second `memmem` runs only on the few lines carrying the rare word. The literal alone was not the discriminator, because a payload can write that word in prose. A refused line is still lifted to a structural spine row, so the survival chain reads through it, and an explicit address still fetches it whole. What this closes is a queued prompt whose content names the literal: it used to be admitted on a flagless scan, classified `user.queued`, emitted and censused, while the same run's honest-empties note reported that leaf as unscanned.
> ③ **The survival axis's serial cost is removed; NO output byte moves** (C-41; §7g carries the measurement, claim REC-104 stands unchanged). Through 0.12.0 every full-scan surface put the chain-structural spine rows of the lines its prefilter dropped in the SAME vector as its own records, so each element was sized to a `Record` and the whole vector was moved out of the scan and then split. The two kinds now ride separate output streams from the scan down (`parse::scan_lines_parallel_split`, one `SplitVerdict` per line, the same chunking and malformed accounting), the spine row is its own narrow type (`parse::SpineRow`, 72 bytes against `Record`'s 1184, its width asserted by a unit pin), and the consumers merge the two back by line into a BORROWED file-order row list that is the index space every chain answer is addressed by. The chain reads both kinds through one shape, `model::ChainNode`, whose message-derived accessors answer the same `false`/`None` a message-less record gave. This is a performance change only: stdout, stderr and exit code are byte-identical to the 0.12.0 binary on 851 (command, file) pairs over the 76 top-level transcripts of one corpus, and on a further 1368 from a supplementary sweep of 18 more command shapes over the same files, 2219 pairs in all.

> **v0.12.0 CHANGE LEDGER (BREAKING; the survival axis, the resume repair pair, the boundary's survivor fields and the background entrances -- `csift 0.12.0`).**
> ① **THE SURVIVAL AXIS: Claude Code's own chain rule replaces three opener heuristics** (claims MISC-034, MISC-035, MISC-036, MISC-037, MISC-038, MISC-039, MISC-041, MISC-043, REC-103, TURN-026, TURN-032, CMP-019, CLS-030). Claude Code does not reconstruct a session by reading its jsonl top to bottom. It streams every `user`/`assistant`/`attachment`/`system` line carrying a string `uuid` into a map where the LAST occurrence of a uuid wins (MISC-034), picks ONE leaf through four gates (MISC-035: a `last-prompt` line's `leafUuid`, recorded with every write batch and wiped by a later `compact_boundary`; `explicit:true` with a null leaf clearing the set; a recorded leaf that is a proper ANCESTOR of the file-order-last record yielding to that record; a leaf naming no record in this file falling back to the tail, never to the next-newest `leafUuid`), and walks `parentUuid` from it to the head, reading that field and nothing else (MISC-036). `logicalParentUuid` is never read on that path, so a `compact_boundary`, whose own `parentUuid` is null, ends the walk. Only what the walk reaches is the conversation. Two passes bracket it: BEFORE any leaf is picked, the records a `compact_boundary` names in `preservedMessages` / `preservedSegment` are re-parented onto its anchor and everything below is cut (MISC-037); AFTER the walk, membership widens past plain ancestry by two more (MISC-038: the same-`message.id` assistant siblings of a chain assistant, plus the `tool_result` carriers parented to them, and every non-conversation descendant of the leaf). `model::Chain` mirrors all of it hop for hop, adding only the 5 s same-`isSidechain` parent repair and the cycle guard, and yields `Survival::{Live, PreCut, Abandoned{root}}` per record. csift carried three special cases that approximated the same answer - the same-parent draft rule, the replay-copy collapse and the boundary's implicit cut - and not one of them could see a turn the operator rewound past, or say which child of a fork the conversation continued from. All three are gone; the rule itself is the replacement.
> ② **What the axis changes for a reader.** An ABANDONED record sits outside turn numbering, is skipped by a bare `-t user` / `-t agent` / `-t harness` (survival is the THIRD exclusion axis beside leaf visibility and record-level delivery, §5.6) and is reached by a glob or an exact leaf path with an `[abandoned]` / `[rewound]` / `[replay copy of L<n>]` marker in the label zone. An abandoned OPENER carries ONE leaf, and the discriminator is a DESCENDANT: `user.unsent` when no assistant record hangs below it, the new `user.rewound` when one does (TURN-032, over the corpus's 910 shared-parent opener pairs: 858 earlier members with no assistant descendant, 52 with one, 0 of the 910 on the final chain). Both leaves are retroactive for the same reason `user.unsent` always was: nothing on the record marks it, the fact lives in the DAG around it (TURN-026, MISC-041). The replay collapse keeps its EFFECT and flips its DIRECTION (CMP-019 read against MISC-034): the loader's map holds the LAST line carrying a uuid, so that line is the survivor for opener treatment and for addressing by uuid, while the earlier line renders `[replay copy of L<n>]` and stays searchable and counted. Turn numbering therefore shifts on any transcript carrying a rewind or a replayed block, the v0.5 and 0.9.2 precedent. The census over the reference corpus CONSERVES the opener count, 3,650 before against 3,650 + 7 after: seven records that used to count as turn-opening `agent.communication.signal` are now `user.rewound`, so that leaf reads 397 where it read 404. Nothing was dropped; the seven moved leaf. Every count is disclosed - the text footer and the JSON summary carry `abandoned_records`, `rewound_turns`, `replay_copies`, `boundary_cut_line` and `leaf_source`, and each hit carries `survival`, `abandoned_root_line` and `replay_copy_of`.
> ③ **TWO fail-open guards, each with its own measured cause.** csift's §7d prefilter keeps only role-bearing lines, and a chain built from those alone resolves nothing: 1 of 30,613 conversation records reachable on one real session. So every surface splices back the STRUCTURAL row of each line its own prefilter dropped, through `parse::spine_record` - a depth-1 key walk lifting `type`, `uuid`, `parentUuid`, `timestamp`, `isSidechain` and the boundary fields into an otherwise EMPTY record, never the payload. The SECOND guard is a FLOOR, and its cause is where csift's own walk stops. A `compact_boundary` carries a null `parentUuid` (260 of 260 boundary records over every transcript under one projects root), so the walk's null-parent arm ends the walk ON the boundary exactly as Claude Code's does, and everything physically above it is a BLIND REGION csift did not resolve. The walk's terminating record therefore floors the verdict: an off-chain record above it is `PreCut`, NEVER `Abandoned`, and inside that region the measured same-parent opener rule still marks a draft, so draft detection never depends on the chain reaching that far. What csift adds above a cut is not a crossing but a refusal to discard: Claude Code drops that history, csift keeps every record the walk did not reach, flagged, and every selector still reaches it, because what the model saw AT THE TIME is the question an archive answers. The `logicalParentUuid` arm in the walk is a GUARD for one shape only, a boundary whose own `parentUuid` is non-null and resolves to no record, and the corpus holds zero specimens of it: renaming the field on five real compacted transcripts of 9 to 21 MB leaves the per-hit `survival` census, the `stats` chain totals and the search-summary disclosures byte-identical. The blind region is not a partial record set either: no admitted record's `parentUuid` names a progress or unparsed line (12 transcripts probed, empty census). MISC-043 records what the guard would meet if it ever fired: 52 of 255 `compact_boundary` records, across 14 of the 21 compacted transcripts among 77 top-level ones, name a `logicalParentUuid` matching no `uuid` on any line of their own file, the pre-compaction records having been deleted from disk by the pre-boundary deletion path.
> ④ **C-34: the resume repair pair gets its own harness leaves, and one leaf is RENAMED** (claims CLS-013, CLS-028, CLS-029). When Claude Code resumes a transcript whose last non-system/non-progress record is a `type:"user"` one -- an esc-recalled draft never resent, or any prompt that drew no reply -- the LOADER repairs the dangling tail before handing the conversation to the model. It appends an `isMeta` user record reading `Continue from where you left off.`, and it splices a stand-in assistant record reading `No response requested.` in right after the trailing user record, both with one identical timestamp. **The model receives both**: the request assembler's drop predicate reaches neither, because its `<synthetic>`-model arm also requires `isApiErrorMessage`, which the loader does not set. Only Claude Code's own `--reply-on-resume` flag suppresses the splice. Corpus, one event per marker record over the uuid-named top-level transcripts: 75 scanned, 10 carry a marker, 8 prompts + 18 placeholders = 26 events.
> ⑤ **`harness.schedule.continuation` -> `harness.resume.prompt` (BREAKING).** Same predicate, same records, new path. The record comes from the resume loader and never from the scheduler, and the sibling placeholder needed a family to sit in; `harness.schedule` keeps only `wakeup`. The retired spelling is a HARD parse error naming its successor -- the v0.6.3 precedent for the pre-v0.5 flat values, not a compat shim. Anything keying on the old leaf path, in a script or a saved query, must be updated.
> ⑥ **`harness.resume.placeholder` is new, and it is a correctness fix, not just a label.** Those 18 records classified `agent.message` before -- which asserts the assistant wrote a sentence no model produced. The predicate is a 1:1 port of Claude Code's own recogniser (assistant, `isApiErrorMessage` falsy, `message.model` the `<synthetic>` sentinel, single-text content exactly the sentence); the model sentinel is the load-bearing half, so a genuine reply that happens to say the same words keeps `agent.message`.
> ⑦ **The pairing is a FACT on the hit, not a second leaf.** ONE writer produces every placeholder and its gate is only "the tail is a user record", while the prompt's gate is the tail classifier's interrupted-turn verdict -- so the same splice lands ALONE after an interrupt marker, a `<local-command-stdout>` echo or a slash wrapper. Measured: of 18 placeholders, 8 have a repair prompt as their `parentUuid` and 10 do not (7 `[Request interrupted by user]`, 1 `...for tool use]`, 1 stdout echo, 1 slash wrapper). Splitting one producer across two leaves would have hidden that, so both forms carry the leaf and the verdict rides beside it: JSON `resume_paired` (`true`/`false`, `null` on every non-placeholder hit) and the label-zone marker `[paired]` / `[unpaired]`.
> ⑧ **Neither half opens a turn, and `status`/`wait` walk past both.** The prompt is `isMeta` (already excluded from every opener case); the placeholder is an assistant record. The `last` section needed the explicit skip for the placeholder -- it is an assistant record with real text, so a resumed session reported `No response requested.` as its newest reply.
> ⑨ **The prompt predicate requires `isMeta`, and that is a safety property, not a detail.** Claude Code's own recogniser tests the flag, and the injecting call site is what stamps it. Without the same requirement a person who merely BEGINS a real message with the sentence -- `Continue from where you left off. and also fix the tests` -- would have their prompt reclassified as machinery and dropped from turn numbering: the C-28 delivery error in reverse, applied to a human. With it, that message stays `user.message` and opens its turn, and both of this leaf's exclusions hold THROUGH the gate `is_genuine_user` already reads. csift does not apply the recogniser's third test (`promptSource` absent), so its set is a superset by exactly the records carrying that field; none exists in the corpus, and the gap is recorded as residue rather than guessed at.
> ⑩ **C-33: the compaction boundary's survivor fields, and the two summarize modes are distinguishable** (claims CMP-001, CMP-002, CMP-020, CMP-021). `compactMetadata` was always more than the four scalars csift rendered, and two of the extra keys say what a compaction KEPT. The boundary excerpt now names every field the record carries, in a fixed order and only when present: `[compaction boundary: trigger=… preTokens=… postTokens=… durationMs=… messagesSummarized=… cumulativeDroppedTokens=… preserved=<N uuids, M allUuids, anchor <first8>> segment=<head first8>..<tail first8>]`. `cumulativeDroppedTokens` is the running total of context tokens every compaction of the session has dropped; `preservedMessages.uuids` is the on-disk subset of what was kept and `allUuids` the in-memory superset, which is why an `allUuids` entry can resolve to no line (2 of 19 on the specimen). The one-line excerpt COUNTS those lists and prints first-8 handles, because a boundary line has to stay scannable; JSON `compact_metadata` carries the object verbatim, uuid lists included. **A boundary carrying only the four original scalars renders byte-for-byte what it always did** (pinned by a unit test and an e2e test).
> ⑪ **The `/rewind` summarize modes get a name.** Claude Code compacts three ways and writes the same two records for all three, so an ordinary `/compact` and a `/rewind` → "Summarize up to here" were indistinguishable on csift's surfaces. They are separated by ONE key, and it sits on the SUMMARY: the writer emits `summarizeMetadata:{messagesSummarized, userContext?, direction}` INSTEAD of `isVisibleInTranscriptOnly` (a genuine either/or in one ternary), with `direction` reading `"up_to"` or `"from"`. csift derives a MODE - `compact` | `summarize-from-here` | `summarize-up-to-here` - and prints it on BOTH records: JSON `mode` on the `search` hit, the `show` record row and the `verbatim` `compaction_boundary` row, a `[summarize <direction>]` label-zone tag on the summary's text line, and a `· summarize <direction> ·` segment in the `verbatim` banner. The BOUNDARY has no direction of its own, so it learns its mode by pairing with the summary that follows it (`model::SummarizeIndex`, one pass per file, carried on `ClassifyCtx` beside the spawn lookup); an UNPAIRED boundary and an unmodeled `direction` both read null rather than a guessed `compact`. The pairing is built from the WHOLE parsed record set before any `--turn` window, `--max-count` cap or `show` address narrows the output, so it is window-independent by construction: `show --line <the boundary>` still reports the mode, and only the file itself (no summary after the boundary, or a second boundary first) leaves one unpaired. `userContext` is written from a possibly-undefined value and is absent from the only corpus specimen, so the discriminator is `direction` alone, never a three-key shape.
> ⑫ **A summarize IS a compaction, and `verbatim` treats it as one.** The gesture clips turns exactly like an auto-compact - the summariser reads the in-memory message array and the file keeps every record it dropped (claim CMP-020) - so reconstruction is unchanged and the mode only labels the event. The label-zone tag needs no synthesized-marker registration (it is display-only, like `[narration summary]` and `[not delivered]`), and the boundary excerpt's new fabricated bytes are already covered by the `-t`-gated `compact_boundary` marker, which is a raw substring of the same line.
> ⑬ **The `/rewind` menu's other rows, recorded because their on-disk trace is EMPTY** (claims REC-101, FH-051, FH-052, REC-102, MISC-032, MISC-033). "Restore conversation" truncates the live message array in memory and appends nothing - none of thirteen record-writing names occurs anywhere in its body, and 0 of 30,658 `last-prompt` lines in the corpus carry a `rewound` or `explicit` flag. "Restore code" writes only the working tree and can DELETE a file whose backup is null; the reducer arm it dispatches bumps a counter and writes neither a `file-history-delta` nor a `file-history-snapshot` line, which the corpus confirms by key set (2,181 delta lines and 4,359 snapshot lines, one key set each, no restore marker). The only trace that follows is the next turn's `edited_text_file` attachment, an mtime-gated re-read carrying a DIFF, whose snippet is emptied past a 16,384-character budget. And the branch a rewind leaves behind stays on disk in full: 1,481 abandoned roots in 44 of 75 transcripts, 11,142 records, 422 tool calls - which is why csift replays FILE ORDER and never a surviving chain when it attributes a mutation.
> ⑭ **`show --branch-points` names the live child.** The fork rows are unchanged - every record with 2+ conversation children, children with lines and timestamps, ranked by the widest inter-child gap - but the question the v0.8.0 ledger left open is now answered by Claude Code's own conversation chain (`model::Chain`), not by a sibling-shape heuristic. Each fork prints `live child: L<n>` (or `live child: none` when the whole fork sits off the surviving conversation, or `live children: L<a> L<b>` on the rare row the chain's membership passes keep two of), and every child carries a verdict: `live` · `rewound` (it was answered, and the conversation was rewound past it) · `draft` (nothing ever answered it) · `abandoned` (off the chain, mid-branch) · `pre-cut` (above a compaction boundary, where the loader stops and csift keeps reading). The PARENT lookup widened at the same time: a fork parent is often not a conversation record at all - a prompt submitted after a SessionStart hook is parented to that hook's `attachment` line - so the scan now lifts the structural spine row of every line the role prefilter drops (`parse::spine_record`, the same splice `search` uses), locates the parent among them and prints its line AND its type. `(parent line not located)` is retired: a uuid that names no line in the file reads `parent uuid not in this file`, which is the only case left. JSON branch-point rows gain `parent_line`, `parent_type`, `live_child_line` (null unless exactly one child is live) and, per child, `survival` (`live` | `pre-cut` | `abandoned`) and `verdict` (the five values above); `line` keeps its envelope meaning and equals `parent_line`, since on this row the record IS the fork parent. Measured over the reference corpus, one row per record with >=2 conversation children: 745 fork rows over 1,799 children across 33 of 75 top-level transcripts, and the parent located on 745 of 745 - a `system` record on 539 rows, an `assistant` on 94, a `user` on 68, an `attachment` on 44, so 583 of 745 (78%) are lines the role prefilter never keeps, exactly the population the old lookup could not resolve. Those figures held across two passes; the live/abandoned SPLIT of the same children did not, and that is the expected direction rather than noise. Appending to a transcript moves its leaf, the leaf decides the chain, and the chain decides which child is live - while the fork itself, a plain parentUuid fact, does not move. Two passes read 0 live children on 679 then 686 rows, 1 on 65 then 58, 2 on 1 both times; child verdicts read draft 871 / pre-cut 824 / live 67 / rewound 36, then 873 / 831 / 60 / 35. The shape is what is stable: most forks have NO live child because they sit above a compaction cut, and exactly one row has TWO, the membership widening keeping a same-`message.id` assistant sibling.
> ⑮ **`stats` counts LIVE turns, and discloses what left the numbering.** `stats` splices the spine exactly as `search` does, so its `turns` is now the count of numbered LIVE turns - the same numbering `search` prints as `·tN`, `show --turn` addresses and `search --count-by turn` enumerates as rows - instead of a count derived from role records alone. A `--turn` window resolves against that live numbering too. Three whole-file chain totals ride along, per session row and in the scope TOTAL: `abandoned_turns` (turn openers the conversation chain no longer reaches), `rewound_turns` (the subset of those that drew a reply before the rewind) and `replay_copies` (lines a compaction re-anchor re-appended). They are FILE facts like `lines` and the `line_types` census and are never windowed by `--since`/`--until`/`--turn`, because an abandoned opener carries no turn index to window on. The text block gains one line, printed only when there is something to say: `chain  abandoned turns N (M rewound) · replay copy lines K  (whole-file facts, outside \`turns\`)`. The line-type census is unchanged and stays the exact corruption-census authority: a non-candidate line is still fully validated by its own type probe, and the spine row is lifted OUT OF that same parse (`parse::line_type_and_spine`) rather than by a second byte walk: on the corpus's largest transcript by bytes (720 MB, 91,975 lines of which 49,902 are attachments) the UNFUSED shape - a narrow type probe plus a separate `spine_record` walk - costs about 40 ms of user CPU more than the fused one, and the SIMD in-string hop inside `spine_record` is worth a further 136 ms to `show --branch-points` on the same file. Method for both: medians of 16 and of 20 runs per arm, arms interleaved forwards and backwards each round, user CPU from `getrusage` deltas, a same-binary control drifting 0.3-0.6%. Parity receipt over the reference corpus, one observation per transcript: `stats` `turns` equals the count of NUMBERED rows `search --count-by turn` prints on 75 of 75 top-level transcripts (a transcript carrying records off the chain gains one more row, `t? (abandoned)`, which counts those records and is no turn) (6,323 live turns at one pass, 6,326 at a later one - the corpus is live, and it is the AGREEMENT that is the claim, not the sum).
> ⑯ **`verbatim`, `files`, `recover` and `image` join the same numbering, through `model::ChainView`** (claims CMP-020, FH-015, FH-021, QT-025, REC-025, MISC-030, REC-075). `search`/`show`/`stats` splice the spine rows into their own record list; these four index everything by their OWN record positions and cannot, so `parse::parse_candidates_with_spine` lifts a dropped line's structural row in the SAME pass (one parse per line, never two) and the view merges the two slices by line number, runs the ONE chain and answers back in the surface's own index space: `survival(i)`, `stamp(i)` (`TurnStamp::{Live(n), Abandoned{root_line}}`), `order_turn(i)`, `turns()` and `groups()`. A turn number therefore means the same thing on every command: a `verbatim` turn 7, a `search --count-by turn` row `t7`, a `show --turn 7` and a `files --turn 7` name one turn (checked per session over 75 top-level transcripts: 64 agree, 0 disagree, 11 too small to compare). `image --turn` joins them; it had stayed on the older, more conservative grouper. The agreement was re-established, not assumed: on a 19-transcript `files`-to-`search` join, one observation per paired row, a build that had wired the axis into only some surfaces read 2,085 mismatched turn stamps, 23.1% of the paired rows, where 0.11.1 read 0 and 0.12.0 reads 0. This REPLACES the interim state the chain landed in, where these four saw a role-only DAG and every record above a compaction cut read abandoned.
> ⑰ **`verbatim` replays LIVE turns only - a correctness rule, not a display choice.** A turn the operator rewound past, or a prompt recalled and re-typed, is counted in a per-session footer that carries the exact `csift show @<id> --line N` fetching it, and is never re-emitted; JSON adds `abandoned_turns` to the summary and `survival` to every unit. The reason is claim CMP-020: the compaction summariser's input is the in-memory message array, so it never saw that turn either, and replaying it would put text no model read into a reconstruction of what a compaction clipped. `--slice` follows the live turns. Reconstruction still crosses a compaction CUT where Claude Code's own loader stops - those units read `survival` `pre-cut` rather than being dropped, because what was written was the conversation at the time.
> ⑱ **`files` and `recover` KEEP every abandoned row and event, and MARK it - disk truth is FILE ORDER.** An Edit on a branch the operator later rewound past LANDED on disk, so the row stays where the file put it; its turn slot reads `abandoned (root L<n>)`, the text row carries `[abandoned]`, and JSON nulls `turn_index` beside `survival` and `abandoned_root_line`. `files --by summary` counts the abandoned share ALONGSIDE the total (`N op(s) (M from abandoned branches)`, JSON `abandoned`) rather than subtracting it, and `recover` discloses how many replayed events came from abandoned records. A `--turn` window on these two admits such a row by the LIVE turn it physically follows (`order_turn`): a window over a span must not silently omit a write that happened inside it. The conversation surfaces make the opposite call for the same reason.
> ⑲ **`image` lists and extracts an abandoned image, marked.** The bytes are on disk and `--out` writes the same file, so the listing keeps the row and adds `[abandoned]` / JSON `survival`. `--turn` is the one exception: it windows on NUMBERED turns and an abandoned record belongs to none, so a turn window leaves it out.
> ⑳ **`recover`'s external-write inference widens from the settings family to the `--file` target, on any path** (claims FH-015, FH-021, REC-025, MISC-030). The C-24 file-history instrument - a tracked file's `version` jumping with no replayed tool write - now produces an `external_write` boundary on a recover run's single target reading `external write (inferred, snapshot vN->vM, no tool record since L<line>)`, so the row NAMES the interval it bounds instead of asserting a moment. `files` keeps the narrow settings-family scope on purpose: its tracked set spans every written path, and a global timeline row would flood the output (censused: 58,115 such jumps over 1,121 non-settings paths in 31 of 75 top-level transcripts, against 47 over 5 in the settings family); a row about the ONE file a caller named cannot flood anything. Both file-history line shapes now feed the version sequence - the per-prompt `file-history-snapshot` table and the per-write `file-history-delta`'s `trackingPath` + `backup` - admitted by widening recover's candidate needle to the shared `file-history-` prefix, so the interval is as tight as the harness's own bookkeeping allows. The motivating producer is the `/rewind` "Restore code" gesture, which writes nothing to the transcript; the next prompt's `edited_text_file` attachment stays the authoritative boundary it already was.
> ㉑ **Cost, stated in numbers.** The spine pass reads payload bytes it deliberately never parses, and that is the whole of the added cost: a probe build returning from `spine_record` before it walks puts every slower command back at the pre-axis baseline within noise, while the merge, the chain and the turn grouping measure at zero. The scale is the point - on a 397 MiB transcript the pass builds 100,387 structural rows against as few as 183 records the command itself wanted. Measured against 0.11.1 on that transcript, medians of 21 runs per arm on an interleaved CPU-time driver with rotated cells and a same-binary control drifting under 2%, the ratios quoted being wall clock: `image` 1.74 to 1.77x, `verbatim` 1.35x, `show --branch-points` 1.54x, `recover` 1.19x, `stats` 1.17x, and a heavy `-t user.unsent` search on a 721 MB transcript 1.17x; `files` reads 0.84x, FASTER, because the view replaced four per-file chain builds with one. The cost is not where a reader would expect it. The byte walk runs inside the parallel scan, so on `image` it is 103 ms of CPU and 17 ms of wall, and building the row is 25 ms of CPU and under 2 ms of wall. What lands on the clock is serial: two bulk moves of a row vector at 1,184 bytes a row, and the chain build itself, which grows from 1 ms over 183 nodes to 46 ms over 100,570. FOUR levers were measured and rejected on the way to that finding. An early stop in the key walk is impossible, because Claude Code writes `uuid` and `timestamp` AFTER the payload key on 662,470 of 673,710 admitted lines (claim REC-104). A reverse scan from the line end lands within one percent of the forward walk on every shape. A leaner row buys about 1.5% on `search` and half a percent on `image` and `verbatim`, because its construction was never the cost. Two reductions ARE in: the SIMD `memchr2` jump over string interiors, and the fused `line_type_and_spine` entry that lifts the row out of the parse `stats` already runs. The remaining headroom is in the serial carry, and taking it means the spine rows must stop sharing a collection with full records: scoped and measured at a lower bound of 13% on `image` and 8% on `verbatim`, and shipped as a later patch, not as a last-minute change to the seam this release just settled. Section 7's structural contract is otherwise untouched - mmap, the memchr line scan, the byte prefilter, the tail reads, rayon and the whole-file gate all work exactly as they did.
> ㉒ **C-38: a shell backgrounded by ctrl+b, a timeout or a delivered message is a background task** (claims BG-040, BG-001, BG-002, BG-003, BASH-027). `run_in_background` is the only door the MODEL asks for; Claude Code opens three more on a command the model ran in the FOREGROUND -- the user presses ctrl+b on the in-flight call, the command hits its timeout, or it is moved aside so a queued message can reach the model. The launching `tool_use` is already on disk and is never rewritten, so the RECEIPT is the only trace: ONE formatter, FOUR arms (`Command was manually backgrounded by user with ID: …` / `… was moved to the background (ID: …) so that a message …` / `Command did not complete within its <N>s timeout and was moved to the background (ID: …)` / the ordinary `Command running in background with ID: …`), all four interpolating `Output is being written to: <path>.` because the path is a plain parameter rather than an arm. The manual arm is the only SINGLE-SENTENCE ack: it drops both trailing sentences. `status` and `wait` read all three, so the tasks now count toward `idle-background-open` and hold `--until stop` open -- on three real sessions the open-task count rose 3 -> 5, 5 -> 6 and 51 -> 54. Each row says which door (`entered by ctrl+b` / `entered by timeout after 2m` / `entered to deliver a message`) and JSON gains `entered_by` (`model` | `user` | `timeout` | `deliver-message`, null for an agent or a Monitor), `timed_out_after_ms` and `launch_note`. Because the launch line carries no background needle, one targeted SECOND pass over the same bytes -- keyed on the minted tool_use ids -- recovers the launch instant, command and description; a row whose launch line is absent keeps the RECEIPT instant under `launch_note` (`launched-at unknown; receipt at <ts>`) instead of fabricating one. The mint gate is the WHOLE SENTENCE - the opening clause, a `local_bash` task id (`b` plus 8 base36, claim BG-009) in the id slot, and the arm's own closing clause, with the manual arm's output path required to name the task's OWN file - because a transcript can RENDER the template instead of receiving one (a grep of the harness binary is a real corpus shape; `${e}` and `<id>` both observed), and minting from one would be this fix's mirror image: a fabricated task that flips a stopped session to `idle-background-open` and holds `--until stop` open forever. The corpus census that measures the arms runs that same predicate, so the gate and the instrument coincide by construction. A FIFTH way in, a plugin's turn abort, renders the ORDINARY sentence and stays invisible: a bounded blind spot, recorded rather than guessed.
> ㉓ **C-39: a gated attachment the scan cannot search is still a node of the chain.** Two candidate needles are DEFAULT-ON, and both admit `attachment` lines a flagless scan has no leaf for: the v0.11.0 `csift-channel v1` literal, and the D7 `compact_boundary` value substring, which keeps any payload that merely uses the word. v0.11.0 dropped such a record from the record list before the match phase so it could not leak out under a gated leaf, and that deletion is a chain hole: the survival axis walks `parentUuid` THROUGH attachment records, so the walk below the hole cannot resolve its parent, the 5 s same-`isSidechain` repair only bridges a gap that narrow, the walk ends, and its terminating record becomes a FLOOR that paints everything physically above it `PreCut` - or `Abandoned` where the hole sits off the main line. The record is now DEMOTED instead: `scan::search_one_file` reduces it in place through `parse::spine_from_record` to exactly the structural row `parse::spine_record` lifts off the same line (pinned field for field by a unit test), so it is invisible to every emission pass - no hit, no sibling, no census key, no turn opener - and visible to `model::Chain`. Nothing an explicit query could reach changes: an address or either attachment flag still admits the record whole, a real delivery is still kept whole under `agent.communication.channel`, and the two v0.11.0 leak pins (an `edited_text_file` snippet quoting the header, a hook context merely mentioning it) still emit nothing on a flagless scan. **Measured over the 76 uuid-named top-level transcript files of a real corpus, one observation per file.** 173 lines on 5 of them were admitted-then-deleted, every one by the D7 needle and none by the channel needle (the corpus holds no delivery at all). Chain diagnostics from the JSON summary, flagless scan versus `--attachments` on the same binary: 3 of 76 disagreed before (`abandoned_records` 26 against 1031 on a 7.2 MB transcript, 63 against 59 on a 162 MB one, and a 160 MB one reporting `boundary_cut_line: null` where the gate-open arm named its newest boundary), 0 of 76 disagree after. On the 7.2 MB file - which carries no `compact_boundary` record at all - 1,190 of 1,246 emitted rows read `pre-cut` before and 0 do after, and `search` versus `show --line` on a 12-line sample of them agreed 0 of 12 before and 12 of 12 after, because an address opens the gates and `show` was reading the same lines correctly the whole time.
> ㉔ **C-30: an inbound `<cross-session-message>` was invisible** (claim TURN-031). A message sent from one Claude Code session to another lands in the RECEIVER's transcript under a THIRD peer framing, distinct from `<teammate-message>` and `<agent-message>`: a `type:"user"` / `role:"user"` / STRING record carrying `isMeta:true`, `promptSource:"system"`, `userType:"external"`, `queueSkipAttachments:true` and a top-level `origin` object `{kind:"peer", from, verifiedPeerPid, msg_id?, name?, fromMode?, body?}`, whose content is the usual relay preamble, then `<cross-session-message from="uds:…" from-session=… hop-chain=… from-name=… from-mode=…>` (five attributes, fixed order, all conditional), the body, `</cross-session-message>`, and the security footer. `is_peer_message` accepted only the other two, so the record matched NO marker, its `isMeta` gate emitted the EMPTY label vector, and the delivered message had no leaf, no census row, no `-t` result and no rendered `show` address - only `show --raw`. It now classifies `agent.communication.inbox` and opens a turn like the other two framings; the direction is `from-name` where present, else `from` (the first is what a human named the sending session, the second a socket or bridge address). The close tag joins the section-boundary set, so a BATCHED record's later sections are still recognised, and detection stays boundary-anchored - this repository's own documentation quotes the literal tag, and Claude Code's own reader anchors the same way.
> ㉕ **The queue rider is refused by construction.** A `queue-operation` `enqueue` line carries the identical framed content one line before the user record, and `user.queued` is defined as "the queue line carries the HUMAN's text" - the same `is_peer_message` predicate decides both, so folding the third framing in stops the rider being counted as typed text without a second rule that could drift. Measured over the top-level transcripts: 24 `enqueue` + 6 `remove` lines carry `<agent-message>`, 1 `enqueue` carries `<cross-session-message>`.
> ㉖ **`origin.name` / `origin.body` are preferred over a tag scan, but only for a single-section record.** Claude Code fills those fields by re-parsing the framed string, so they agree with the tag's own attributes by construction; reading the structured pair survives a body a tag scan cannot bound. One `origin` describes ONE message, so a record batching several peer sections falls back to each section's own attribute rather than stamping the first sender onto all of them.
> ㉗ **`record_text_sections` renders the peer BODY, not the section slice.** `inbound_comm_preview` (`verbatim`/`list`) already stripped the wrapper tags, the relay preamble and the security footer; the `search`/`show` render emitted the whole section including the open and close tags, so the same message read one way in one command and another way in the next. Both now render the same tag-stripped body (claim TURN-015). Consequence for a matcher, and it is a LOSS, not a flag away: the tag and its attributes are no longer part of the rendered text, and rendered text is the only thing `search` matches — so `search 'teammate_id='` now returns zero. **`--raw` does not recover it.** `search --raw` is OUTPUT-only: the per-file mmap backfill onto `Hit.raw` runs AFTER the match phase, over the lines of hits that already matched, so it changes what a hit PRINTS and never what the matcher SEES (scan.rs, the `if args.raw` backfill; the same is true of `--format json`'s `raw` field). What survives: the peer's own words match as before; the direction (`from ⇨ to`) still carries the sender; and the verbatim line of a record you can ADDRESS is reachable with `csift show @<id> --line N --raw`, which needs no match at all. The rendered body stays a VERBATIM substring of the source line, so the §7d literal prefilter and the §7f whole-file gate hold with no synthesized-marker registration.
> ㉘ **The fetch domain now matches the refetch law.** `classify` deliberately yields NO label for some real message records - an `isMeta` pseudo-turn matching no harness marker (§4.2's M2b rule: better silent than mislabeled `user.message`), a block record with no text - and `show --line`/`--uuid` on such a record answered `no such record(s)` on a line that is plainly there. An ADDRESSED record with no modeled leaf now renders as ONE UNLABELED unit: `Hit.class` became `Option<Class>`, text prints `? (no label)` (the `?` marker is new, beside `◂ ▸ ⚙ ·`), and JSON carries `"label": null` with `"labels": []`. The fallback is gated on the address, so a SCAN emits nothing new and no census, `-t` result or count moves; a line that is no record at all (a session-state cache line, a content-less queue dequeue, a torn line) is still a hard miss pointing at `--raw`.
> Taxonomy: 35 -> 37 leaves.

> **v0.11.1 CHANGE LEDGER (non-breaking surfaces + ONE correctness fix that SHIFTS TURN NUMBERS -- `csift 0.11.1`).**
> ① **CORRECTNESS: a replayed record is no longer read as a resend, and turn numbering shifts where that happened** (claim CMP-019). A compaction RE-ANCHOR re-appends a contiguous block of already-written records with their uuids PRESERVED - and a turn-opening user record in that block keeps its `parentUuid` too, differing from the original in exactly one top-level field, `promptId`. The C-18 draft rule groups openers by `parentUuid` and keeps the last, so it read the copy as a later sibling and marked the ORIGINAL - a message that was sent and answered - as a `user.unsent` draft, dropped it from turn numbering, and (from the C-27 diff line) rendered it as differing from the sent message in 0 chars. Measured: 7 genuine messages mislabeled in one session; whole-file, 464 uuids appear twice, 462 with the same `parentUuid` (attachment 289, assistant 117, user 44, system 14). `model::collapse_openers` now decides both opener corrections in ONE walk and collapses same-uuid openers under one parent to their FIRST occurrence: the first keeps its label and its turn; the copy supersedes nothing and OPENS nothing, but stays a turn MEMBER, so it is still addressable and consistent with the replayed assistant and attachment records, which have always rendered at both of their lines. **What is de-duplicated is the OPENER treatment - the draft label and the turn - and nothing else: a replayed block's records are still counted and matched at BOTH lines.** In the measured specimen `-t user.message --count-by session` reads 666 where 0.11.0 read 659, the seven originals AND their seven copies, where 0.11.0 counted the copies and labelled the originals `user.unsent`. That is the SAME double-count law §3.5 states for a clone, one file down instead of across files: a spanning surface counts duplicated records once per line until the duplicate is scoped away, and csift discloses the shape rather than silently picking a copy. **Turn numbers therefore shift on any transcript carrying a replayed block** - the same class of change as the v0.5 slash-wrapper fix and the 0.9.2 draft work, and the same rule applies: a `tN` you copied from an earlier csift may name a different turn, so re-read the header rather than reusing a number. `superseded_draft_indices` / `superseded_draft_map` / `group_turn_indices_deduped` all derive from the one walk, so `search`, `show`, `files`, `recover` and `verbatim` cannot drift apart on it.
> ② **A draft whose text did not change says so.** When the diff is exactly 0 and exact - a recalled message resent unedited - the line reads `identical to the sent message` instead of `differs from the sent message in 0 chars (0.0% of the final)`, which invited the reader to hunt for an edit that is not there. JSON is unchanged (`diff_chars: 0`, `diff_exact: true`).
> ③ **C-27, the unsent diff line.** A RENDERED `user.unsent` draft now states its distance from the message that replaced it, in text as one line under the draft header (`differs from the sent message in N chars (P% of the final): may carry an addition or a correction`) and in JSON as `superseding_line`, `superseding_uuid`, `diff_chars`, `diff_pct`, `diff_exact` - on the `search` exchange row where `superseded_draft` already lives, and on the `show` record row (null there on every other row). N is INSERTIONS PLUS DELETIONS of a shortest CHARACTER-level edit script between the draft's reconstructed text and the final's - the characters covered by the differing regions - never a length difference, so a mid-text replacement counts as the work it was; P is N over the final's character count and can exceed 100 when the draft was the longer text. Both sides come from `Record::reconstructed_user_text`, the engine the draft's own hit renders through, and both are compared as chars so a multi-byte body is never split. The engine is `src/chardiff.rs` (a char-level Myers O(ND) walk, no new dependency) and the §7 contract is what shapes it: the cost is O(N*D) and the greedy loop alone walks about D^2/2 diagonals, so FOUR bounds apply in order - the common head and tail are stripped, one linear subsequence test settles the dominant corpus shape exactly and for free (a short draft replaced by a longer message that still contains it), the residual length difference is a STRICT floor whenever that test fails and is the floor every giving-up arm reports (never the weaker `MAX_DIFF_CHARS` = 200,000 cap), and what survives walks under a step budget. Every inexact result is a proven strict floor rendered as `more than N`, never an estimate. The diff runs only at EMISSION, for a draft that produced a hit, and only when exchanges will be rendered: `-c` / `-l` / `--count-by` print no exchange and pay nothing. Measured over the whole corpus AFTER the ① guard, on a population of 887 drafts (the live corpus gains drafts as sessions run, so the number a re-run sees drifts up): 877 exact and 10 reporting a floor between 3,813 and 9,360 characters, each within a few percent of the true distance; the largest draft (237,434 chars) diffs in 0.755 ms, and both `search -t user.unsent --max-count 0` and a broad `search the` measured within noise of the previous release.
> ④ **C-26, the retroactive label, stated as such** (claim TURN-026). `user.unsent` is a statement about a LATER record and can only ever be assigned after the fact. CENSUSED OVER THE FULL POPULATIONS, on csift's own output and after the ① guard: all 886 superseded drafts in 28 sessions, each joined to the survivor its own `superseding_line` names (so no forward scan, no cap, no locally defined opener predicate, and no unpaired draft), against all 2,595 `user.message` records (2,567 exchange rows) of the same sessions. The draft and its resend carry the same top-level key set on 852 of 886 pairs (13 distinct key sets on the draft side, 15 by pair union; the one-sided keys are `imagePasteIds` 29, `origin` 5, `queueSkipAttachments` 1), and among shared keys `promptId`, `uuid` and `timestamp` differ on all 886, `message` on 862, `promptSource` on 28, `origin` on 15, `version` on 1 - identity and time apart, the two records are the same shape. Nor does the window between them mark anything: it is almost never empty (1 of 886) and holds ordinary end-of-turn telemetry, with rates (drafts/control) `file-history-snapshot` 99.3/91.2, `last-prompt` 64.4/53.7, `permission-mode` 64.3/53.7, `mode` 64.3/53.4, `ai-title` 64.3/53.8, `hook_additional_context` 56.4/51.3, `agent-name` 42.0/37.8, `total_tokens_reminder` 27.8/29.7 and 40-odd further shapes below 11% on the draft side. Eight shapes read ZERO on the control side, and every one of those zeros is the control window's assistant cut rather than a fact about drafts: with the window cut only at the next opener, `system/stop_hook_summary` goes 0 -> 85.2%, `system/turn_duration` 0 -> 65.8%, `attachment/queued_command` 0 -> 15.0%, `attachment/batching_reminder_sent` 0 -> 7.9%, `attachment/bash_output_audience_note` 0 -> 4.7%. So no shape separates the populations at all (the claim's `enumeration` carries the whole table, shape by shape, each verdict written from its own two numbers). What is genuinely absent is a write for the recall itself: the local recall path refills the input box and slices the in-memory array without appending anything (TURN-025's traced hops). The consequence a consumer needs: a hook running at prompt submit cannot apply the label, since the superseding record IS the prompt being submitted; the closest approximation - skip a trailing turn-opener that no assistant record follows - hides 817 of 886 drafts (92.2%) and wrongly skips 74 of 2,595 genuine messages (2.9%), and is documented as a heuristic in SKILL's wrong-assumptions table, never as the label. 69 of 886 (7.8%) drew an assistant record before the resend, which is why even that window shape is not a rule.
> ⑤ **C-28, record-level delivery visibility (BREAKING for the bare-role sets).** A bare ROLE selector now selects what the model RECEIVED, decided per RECORD instead of per leaf (§5.5). The assembler that builds an API request runs ONE drop predicate, read whole in the shipped binary (claim CLS-026), and it disagrees with the leaf table in three cases: a `system`/`local_command` record - a slash command's own echo and its stdout - is RE-MINTED as a user message and sent, while an `isVirtual` record and the `<synthetic>` API-error placeholder are dropped. `Record::delivery_override` mirrors those three and nothing more; the predicate's other two arms (an `api_system` KEEP, a `progress` drop) need none, because csift models no leaf for either type. The seam is `collect_record_hits`, once per record, right after classify and before any label is tested, so the hits path and the label census cannot drift; `labels[]` stays classification and the verdict rides beside it as the JSON hit field `delivered`, with `[not delivered]` in the label zone under an explicit selector. Corpus effect, the two builds run back to back over 72 top-level transcripts: `-t harness` gains 16 records (the whole `local_command` population), `-t agent` loses exactly 118 `agent.message` records (the whole `isApiErrorMessage` population, 118 of 118 also carrying the sentinel model). Globs, intermediate prefixes and exact leaves are untouched. Two gate consequences ride with it: the key-only `"subtype"` needle is admitted under a bare `harness` role so the delivered record can be reached at all, and the §7f synthesized-marker set reads the SAME predicate, because a narrower one would prune a file whose only hit lies in the fabricated `[<subtype> <level>]` head. `gated_leaves_unreached` and its stderr note now report what the scan PARSED, and the note's leaf list is derived from `GATED_LEAVES` instead of a hard-coded string.
> ⑥ **What a slash command leaves on disk, and what of it the model sees** (claims TURN-027 through TURN-030, MISC-031). A local-jsx command decides what is WRITTEN through the `display` option its completion callback returns, and the executor has exactly four outcomes: `skip` writes nothing; `system` writes the command echo and a `<local-command-stdout>` body through the `system`/`local_command` factory; any other value writes an ordinary `type:"user"` wrapper plus a `<local-command-stdout>` user record; and a panel closed with ESCAPE writes nothing whatever the command's own option was. The `local` command type has no display axis and its result map is separate: a `text` result goes through the SAME `system`/`local_command` factory (choosing the `local-command-stderr` tag when the level is error), a `compact` result writes a user record, a `query` result writes the wrapper plus stdout plus an isMeta prompt, and a `skip` result writes nothing. So the 169 `<local-command-stdout>` USER records in the reference corpus come from the local-jsx default arm (login 69, model 75, effort 4, passes 1, config 1 = 150) and from `/compact`'s own compact arm (19), never from the `local` text arm. Three consequences a consumer needs. The absence of a wrapper is not evidence a command was not run: `/btw`, `/tasks` (= `/bashes`) and `/release-notes` return skip on every normal exit, any Escape-dismissed panel writes nothing, and `/rewind` (= `/checkpoint`/`/undo`) and `/stop` take the `local` skip result. A record on disk is not a record the model saw: `/release-notes` appends a `system`/`informational` notice and THEN returns skip, so it is rendered to the human and dropped by the assembler, like every harness notice. And the one delivered carrier has a single minting site - the `subtype:"local_command"` literal has exactly two occurrences in the executable, the factory and an SDK rehydration mapper - which is why csift keys the delivered case on the record shape alone and never on a command name or a tag.

> **v0.11.0 CHANGE LEDGER (BREAKING; THE CSIFT CHANNEL -- `csift 0.11.0`).**
> ① **The contract change, first, because everything else rests on it: csift's read-only law is amended.** Through 0.10.5 csift wrote nothing at all. From 0.11.0 there are exactly THREE writers - `send`, `deliver` and `ack` - and they write exactly ONE place: the csift-owned sidecar directory `<session-sidecar-dir>/csift-channel/` (§1.1), the `<uuid>/` directory beside `subagents/`. Never a transcript, never the team mailbox, never the cross-session messaging socket, never the session registry, never any settings file. csift does not install its own hook: `deliver --recipe` PRINTS the settings block and the user pastes it. csift never performs an official send either - where an official transport exists the receipt prints the exact call for the model to make, because those transports are model tools and csift is a binary. Every other subcommand stays read-only. This is why the release is a MINOR bump with the `!` marker: the promise a reader had ("it only reads") is narrower now, and a narrower promise is a breaking one even when nothing that used to work stops working.
> ② **Four new subcommands (13 -> 17), one message channel.** It exists because the official channel is STRUCTURALLY absent for some receivers, not because it is nicer: a running workflow lane cannot be reached by the send tool at all (its transcript lives under `workflows/<run>/`, a path the harness's own resume resolver never reaches), an unnamed subagent has no arm to address its parent subagent (the official `to` grammar resolves `main`, a name, a `[ref]` or an `a…` id, so addressing `main` reaches the top-level conversation instead of the spawning agent), nothing reaches an idle top-level session that published no socket, a sender OUTSIDE Claude Code holds no tool to call at all, and a build below the version floors or with the gates off has nothing. `send` (§6.15) queues one message for one resolved lane and prints ONE verdict (`OK` | `FULL` | `MAY-FAIL` | `UNPREDICTABLE` | `REFUSED`; exit 0 for every one of them, a refusal being a definitive answer rather than a usage error). `deliver` (§6.17) is the hook entry: the user pastes `csift deliver --slot k` lines on the eight delivery events, and slot k emits chunk k of what is waiting. `msg` (§6.16) joins the ledger's INTENT to the receiver transcript's FACT into one verdict (`DELIVERED` | `INTENT-ONLY` | `QUEUED` | `HELD` | `EXPIRED` | `ACKED` | `REFUSED`); `ack` appends the one line only the receiver can write. The design is thin-hook / fat-csift on purpose: the pasted line is identical on every event and carries no logic, while append-only accounting, dedupe by id, atomic appends, chunking, slot ordering and block-cap awareness all live in the binary, where they can be tested.
> ③ **`whoami` grew a lane layer** (§6.18): a `@<agent-id>` or `@<Name>@<Team>` target answers for THAT lane; three sections (`self`, `parent`, `topology`) follow the identity output; `--to <TARGET>` runs the same policy table as a prediction and writes nothing; `--peers` lists every live lane as `id kind state` and NOTHING else - no description, no agent type, no name read as a role, because that is exactly the material one lane would use to claim standing over another. Outside Claude Code, `whoami` prints the not-a-lane answer (the one channel out, and what a receiver needs installed) on stdout and still exits non-zero, because the identity question has no answer there and guessing one is the documented trap.
> ④ **The 35th leaf, `agent.communication.channel`** (§5.1). A delivery lands in the receiver's transcript as a `hook_additional_context` attachment; the leaf classifies it when the FIRST content string opens with `[csift-channel v1 `, keeps `harness.meta.hook` beside it and LEADS it. It is the one attachment leaf a DEFAULT scan reaches - every other attachment keep is flag-gated, and a message addressed at this lane is not machinery. Because the keep admits any LINE carrying the literal, an ordinary attachment that merely MENTIONS the header (a quoting hook context, an `edited_text_file` snippet) is parsed too and is dropped again before it can surface: on a flagless scan with no address, a record whose only views are the two gated attachment leaves is not kept. The render is verbatim (the header line plus the body chunk), so no synthesized-marker machinery is needed and the §7d/§7f gates hold unchanged; the candidate needle `csift-channel v1` is a bare value substring with no JSON-escaped character, `&&`-gated on the label selector, so a `-t user` / `-t harness` query pays nothing. The envelope's own fields live in the CONTENT array rather than in the hook name, because a hook attachment's `hookName`/`hookEvent` are not part of its searchable text - a channel whose id and sender lived there would be unauditable by any default query, csift's own included.
> ⑤ **The settings cascade as a shared model** (§1.2, `path::settings`). Five scopes folded in Claude Code's order, `env` per key and `hooks` concatenated per event, the composed first-wins policy tier, plugin hook manifests unioned at read time, the three policy switches evaluated after the fold, and a named UNOBSERVABLE list (`--settings`, `--managed-settings`, `--setting-sources`/`--restricted`, the trust-dialog state, MDM) so a gate verdict says "unknown" with its evidence instead of implying the file scopes are all there is. `plan`'s `plansDirectory` lookup moved onto it with its semantics unchanged: the same three file scopes in the same precedence, so a git-root local file that the general model reads does not enter the plan binding.
> ⑥ **The teammate ROUTING form `@<Name>@<Team>`** (§2, §6.5). A teammate carries two ids, minted apart at spawn: the routing form is what the official `SendMessage` addresses, the transcript form `a<Name>-<16 hex>` is what its hook payload and its `agent-<id>.jsonl` stem carry. Only the routing form can COLLIDE (two same-named teammates in one team share it), so csift keys targeting, its lane files and the envelope's `to` on the transcript form, resolves the routing form through the teammate metas (`name` and `teamName` matched exactly, no case folding) and fails loud on an ambiguity by listing every matching transcript id. Every surface that prints a teammate now prints both forms: the `agents` node line gains `routing: <Name>@<Team>` and its JSON a `routing_id`, and the teammate control hint gains the sentence that names which tool needs which id.

> **v0.10.5 CHANGE LEDGER (non-breaking; the ledger correction round -- `csift 0.10.5`).**
> ① Four defects the ledger's own `drifted` verdicts had named and 0.10.3/0.10.4 shipped around, each fixed with its claim: (a) `image` joined `#N` by a positional zip of the `[Image #N]` markers against the image blocks; the record's `imagePasteIds` lists the ids in BLOCK order (the submit path writes the blocks and the array in one ascending-id pass) while the markers keep the operator's text order, and the two diverge on 18 of 662 corpus records (ids always ascending, the same set, two or three swapped in the text), so those got a silently wrong handle. The join is now by number through `imagePasteIds` (block k takes id k), with the positional zip only as the fallback for records without the array, under its count guard; a `queued_command` attachment's prompt takes the same join - IMG-005. (b) `recover` replayed a Read echo with no text: the harness blanks the text of a tool result older than its retention window before persisting it (`content` empty, `numLines`/`totalLines` intact; 320 in the reference corpus) and four result arms (`file_unchanged`, `pdf`, `parts`, `notebook`) never carry one; a blanked echo of a complete read satisfied the whole-file test and replayed as a snapshot of nothing. Such an echo is now `EventKind::BlankedRead`: counted (`blanked-read` in the coverage line, `events.blanked_read` in JSON), disclosed as a soft authoritative boundary (`blanked_read`), never spliced; a genuinely empty file stays an ordinary read - FH-048. (c) The `status`/`wait` pid probe ran `ps -o lstart=` with the inherited locale and parsed two English field orders in the local zone; a German or French `LC_TIME` renders non-English names (`Fr  4 Sep`, `Ven  4 sep`) and the pid-reuse guard was silently skipped. The probe pins `LC_ALL=C` and `TZ=UTC` on its own call, exactly as the harness pins its `procStart` acquisition, and parses as UTC - MISC-024. (d) A line number is a durable address only while the transcript is append-only, and three harness paths rewrite a live transcript in place (a stream tombstone truncates and rewrites the tail; the local compaction rewrite; a remote-ingress resume), shifting the lines above the cut so a kept `--line` pointer resolves silently to another record. Every search hit now carries `refetch_uuid` (`csift show @<id> --uuid <uuid>`) beside `refetch`; the search help states the durability rule - REC-093.
> ② Four statements csift printed or taught were wrong at 2.1.258 and are corrected with their dating: TaskStop resolves a teammate by its name or `name@team` from Claude Code 2.1.198 (the teammate-aware resolver is absent in 2.1.197 and present in 2.1.198, dated against the cached native builds); the agents footer, the JSON `control_hint`, the SKILL rows and hook recipe, AGENTS 3.7 and section 6.5 said it rejects every form, which was true on the build of the original incident - SUB-020. The file-history checkpoint store is written by the structured file tools AND by an approved in-place `sed` preview (the shell tool applies a previewed `sed -i` through the checkpoint path instead of running it); `recover --list-backups`, its help and the module doc said bash edits never land there - FH-024. The pending AskUserQuestion split (a multi-question ask on disk while its dialog is open, a single-question ask buffered) is a timing outcome of the persistence frontier, which has no question-count branch and did not change at 2.1.258; the verdict comment, the tail note, the live help, AGENTS 3.4, section 4 and 6.13 stated a question-count rule since 2.1.258 - ELI-009/ELI-012. The registry row writer stamps `procStart` unconditionally on every platform; the two-key `procStartFt` schema belongs to the `<pid>.<hash>.key` companion, and the reader's comment attributed it to the row - WIN-007.
> ③ Ledger schema and gate: the attribution `upstream` (`producer_trace: upstream` + `specimen: observed`: the producer lies outside the shipped binary by construction - the model or API side, the operating system, a native runtime binding - while the client-side treatment is traced; a failed search is never upstream); gate rule 9 (a claim's latest check is never `drifted`: the fix or the retirement appends its own check), rule 10 (no open leg is an unconsumed `TEXT` correction or a recorded rejected rewrite: both mean a refuted text still ships), rule 11 (every claim below end-to-end carries both leg fields); the README tally gains the `upstream` row. The prose law for ledger text: no calendar dates, no csift versions, no audit narrative in any text field; the commit and the release carry the when and the which. The README verification section states what the project will never publish (the method, a mechanical ledger comparison, any corpus) and why the ledger is re-verified periodically rather than per Claude Code release.
>
> **v0.10.4 CHANGE LEDGER (non-breaking; the live-file reader -- `csift 0.10.4`).**
> ① `wait` mapped the whole transcript on every poll and `tail_shape` mapped it for its 512 KB window. A memory map of a file another process truncates faults with SIGBUS on the first page touched past the new end (measured: a 1 MB map, a truncation to 4 KB by a second writer, one read past the end - signal 10 on macOS, 7 on Linux), and the ledger's MISC-020 trace shows Claude Code rewriting a transcript in place on two paths (a rewind tombstone: truncate plus tail rewrite, ungated since at least 2.1.191; the local GC compaction rewrite, gated). Both repeated readers now use plain positional reads (`parse::read_range`, `parse::read_tail`); a shrink returns fewer bytes. `wait` detects a file shorter than its baseline between two polls, moves the baseline to the new end and reports `transcript shrank N time(s) (lane: -bytes): rewritten in place, baseline moved` in its activity line and JSON `activity.shrinks`; 0.10.0-0.10.3 skipped a shrunk file silently. The one-shot full scans keep the section 7a map (their sub-second life bounds the exposure; the performance contract rests on it), and section 7a now states the hazard as it is.
>
> **v0.10.3 CHANGE LEDGER (non-breaking; the attribution push -- `csift 0.10.3`).**
> ① The freeform AskUserQuestion answer.
